for recent, closely related works, and references therein. In this section we will review the construction. This will be needed to set-up the rest of the paper, since the method will be used to derive sharp estimates on the multi-point current-current correlators, which will play a key role in the asymptotic validity of linear response.

The starting point is the outcome of the integration of the ultraviolet and high-frequency degrees of freedom, Eq. (
4.54
). Due to the singularity of the covariance of the field at the Fermi points, it turns out that the perturbation theory obtained expanding the theory around the Gaussian case is divergent at all orders, in the limit
\beta,L\to\infty
. In order to obtain a meaningful expansion, we will perform a multiscale analysis, by decomposing the field into a sum of single scale fields. The single scale fields will be integrated in a progressive way, starting from the high energy scales until the lowest energy scales. The covariance and the effective action at a given scale will be defined inductively, via the combination of suitable
localization
and
renormalization
operations. This will ultimately allow to exploit
cancellations
in the naive expansion in a systematic way, and to prove analyticity of the correlation functions for
|\lambda|<\lambda_{0}
for some
\lambda_{0}
independent of
\beta,L
.

Remark 4.1
.

In the rest of the paper, we will use the following short-hand notations:

\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}\,(\cdots):=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}}(\cdots)\;,\qquad\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{p}\,(\cdots):=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{p}\in\mathbb{M}_{\beta,L}}(\cdots)

(4.55)

with

\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}=\Big\{\underline{k}\ |\ k_{0}\in\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\Big(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}\Big),\ k\in B_{L}\Big\}\;,\quad\mathbb{M}_{\beta,L}=\Big\{\underline{p}\ |\ p_{0}\in\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\mathbb{Z},\ p\in B_{L}\Big\}.

(4.56)

For
\beta,L
finite, the summands that we will consider will be supported on a finite number of momenta.

4.3.1
Setting up the multiscale integration

Let
h_{\beta}:=\lfloor\log_{2}(\pi/\delta\beta)\rfloor
, and let
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{-}
,
0\geq h\geq h_{\beta}
. Suppose that the generating functional can be written as:

\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)=\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)+\log\int P_{h}[d\psi^{(\leq h)}]e^{V^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)}

(4.57)

with the following meaning of the various objects entering (
4.57
).

1.

The notation
\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h)}
is a short-hand for
\sqrt{Z_{h,\omega}}\psi^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}
, meaning that each field
\psi^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}
appearing in
V^{(h)}
is multiplied by a factor
\sqrt{Z_{h,\omega}}
.

2.

The Grassmann Gaussian measure
P_{h}(d\psi^{(\leq h)})
has covariance given by:

\begin{split}\int P_{h}(d\psi^{(\leq h)})\psi^{(\leq h)-}_{\underline{x},\omega}\psi^{(\leq h)+}_{\underline{y},\omega^{\prime}}&=\delta_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,e^{i\underline{k}^{\prime}\cdot(\underline{x}-\underline{y})}\hat{g}^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\\
\hat{g}^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})&=\frac{1}{Z_{h,\omega}}\frac{\chi_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})}{ik_{0}+v_{h,\omega}k^{\prime}}(1+r_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}))\;,\end{split}

(4.58)

where

\chi_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\chi\Big(\sqrt{k_{0}^{2}+v_{h,\omega}^{2}{|k^{\prime}|_{\mathbb{T}}^{2}}}/(2^{h}\delta)\Big)\;;

(4.59)

\hat{g}^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
is the
renormalized propagator
on scale
h
. The quantities
r_{h,\omega}
,
Z_{h,\omega}
,
v_{h,\omega}
are analytic functions of
\lambda
for
|\lambda|<\lambda_{0}
such that, for
a>0
, and for
\underline{k}^{\prime}
in the support of
\chi_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
:

\big|\text{d}_{k_{0}}^{n_{0}}\text{d}_{k_{1}}^{n_{1}}r_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\big|\leq C2^{h(a-n_{0}-n_{1})}\;,\qquad\big|v_{h,\omega}-v_{\omega}\big|\leq C|\lambda|\;,\qquad\Bigg|\frac{Z_{h+1,\omega}}{Z_{h,\omega}}\Bigg|\leq e^{c|\lambda|}\;.

(4.60)

Furthermore,
Z_{h,\omega}
,
v_{h,\omega}
are real. The constants
Z_{h,\omega}
are called the wavefunction renormalizations, and while the constants
v_{h,\omega}
are called the effective Fermi velocities, on scale
h
.

3.

The effective interaction on scale
h
takes the form (suppressing the
(\leq h)
label on the fermionic fields):

V^{(h)}(\psi;\phi,A)=\sum_{\Gamma=(\Gamma_{\psi},\Gamma_{\phi},\Gamma_{A})}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}\,\psi_{\Gamma_{\psi}}(\underline{X}_{\psi})\phi_{\Gamma_{\phi}}({\underline{X}_{\phi}})A_{\Gamma_{A}}({\underline{X}_{A}})W_{\Gamma}^{(h)}(\underline{X})

(4.61)

with translation-invariant kernels
W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}
analytic for
|\lambda|<\bar{\lambda}
;
\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L}
admits an analogous expansion, with the constraint
\Gamma_{\psi}=\varnothing
. The kernels satisfy the following estimate, for
P_{\sharp}=|\Gamma_{\sharp}|
:

\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}\,\prod_{i<j}\|\underline{x}_{i}-\underline{x}_{j}\|_{\beta,L}^{n_{ij}}\Big|W_{\Gamma}^{(h)}(\underline{X})\Big|\leq\beta LC_{\{n_{ij}\}}2^{h(2-(1/2)P_{\psi}-P_{A}-(3/2)P_{\phi})-c|\lambda|P_{A})}\;.

(4.62)

Our goal will be to determine a recursion relation for the kernels, as functions of the scale parameter
h
, that allows in particular to check the estimate (
4.62
) on all scales, until
h_{\beta}
. To conclude the Section, we remark that all inductive assumptions are true on scale
h=0
, as a consequence of the cluster expansion that has been used to integrate the ultraviolet degrees of freedom.

4.3.2
Localization and renormalization

In this section we define a localization operator, that allows to extract from the effective interaction on scale
h
the relevant and marginal contributions; these are terms that a priori might expand under the renormalization group iteration, and that will be studied separately. Let us define:

V^{(h)}_{n,m,l}(\psi;\phi,A)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma\\
|\Gamma_{\psi}|=n,|\Gamma_{\phi}|=m,|\Gamma_{A}|=l\end{subarray}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}\,\psi_{\Gamma_{\psi}}(\underline{X}_{\psi})\phi_{\Gamma_{\phi}}({\underline{X}_{\phi}})A_{\Gamma_{A}}({\underline{X}_{A}})W_{\Gamma}^{(h)}(\underline{X})\;.

(4.63)

We will be interested in the following contributions to the effective interaction:

\begin{split}V^{(h)}_{2,0,0}(\psi;\phi,A)&=\sum_{\omega}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\\
V^{(h)}_{4,0,0}(\psi;\phi,A)&=\sum_{\underline{\omega}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}_{1}d\underline{k}^{\prime}_{2}d\underline{k}^{\prime}_{3}\,\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}_{1},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}_{2},\omega_{2}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}_{3},\omega_{3}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}_{1}+\underline{k}^{\prime}_{3}-\underline{k}^{\prime}_{2},\omega_{4}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4,0,0;\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k}^{\prime}_{1},\underline{k}^{\prime}_{2},\underline{k}^{\prime}_{3})\\
V^{(h)}_{1,1,0}(\psi;\phi,A)&=\sum_{\omega,\rho}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\Big(\hat{\phi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega},\rho}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{1,1,0;\rho,\omega}(\underline{k^{\prime}})+\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\phi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega},\rho}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{1,1,0;\omega,\rho}(\underline{k^{\prime}})\Big)\\
V^{(h)}_{2,0,1}(\psi;\phi,A)&=\sum_{\underline{\omega},\nu}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}d\underline{p}\,\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega_{2}}\hat{A}^{\nu}_{-\underline{p}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,1;\underline{\omega},\nu}(\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{p})\;,\end{split}

(4.64)

for suitable kernels. Let
\underline{0}^{\pm}_{\beta}:=(\pm\pi/\beta,0)
. We set:

F(\underline{0}_{\beta}):=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma=\pm}F(\underline{0}^{\sigma}_{\beta})\;.

(4.65)

Definition 4.2
.

The localization operator
\mathfrak{L}
is a linear operator that acts as follows on
V^{(h)}_{n,m,l}
:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{n,m,l}(\psi,\phi,A)&=0\qquad\text{if}\qquad(n,m,l)\neq(2,0,0),(4,0,0),(2,0,1)\\
\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{n,m,l}(\psi,\phi,A)&=V^{(h)}_{n,m,l}\qquad\text{if}\qquad(n,m,l)=(1,1,0)\;,\end{split}

(4.66)

and for the remaining cases, its action is defined by the following identities for the momentum space kernels:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{L}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega}\,(\underline{k}^{\prime})&:=\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})+\underline{k}^{\prime}\cdot\underline{\mathrm{d}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})\\
\mathfrak{L}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4,0,0;\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k}^{\prime}_{1},\underline{k}^{\prime}_{2},\underline{k}^{\prime}_{3})&:=\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4,0,0;\underline{\omega}}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta})\\
\mathfrak{L}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,1;\underline{\omega},\nu}(\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{p})&:=\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,1;\underline{\omega},\nu}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega_{1}}(\lambda)-\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega_{2}}(\lambda))\;,\end{split}

(4.67)

with
\text{d}_{0},\text{d}_{1}
the discrete derivatives. Observe that, by momentum conservation and by assumption (
2.12
):

\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4,0,0;\underline{\omega}}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta})=0\qquad\text{unless $\omega_{1}=\omega_{2},\,\omega_{3}=\omega_{4}$}\ \text{or}\ \omega_{1}=\omega_{4},\omega_{2}=\omega_{3}\;.

(4.68)

Furthermore, one may check that
\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\omega_{3}\omega_{4}}(\underline{k}_{1},\underline{k}_{2},\underline{k}_{3})=-\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\omega_{3}\omega_{2}\omega_{1}\omega_{4}}(\underline{k}_{3},\underline{k}_{2},\underline{k}_{1})
, which implies

\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4,0,0;\omega\omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta})=-\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4,0,0;\omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}\omega\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta})

(4.69)

so that the case
\omega_{1}=\omega_{4}
,
\omega_{2}=\omega_{3}
in (
4.68
) can be reduced to the case
\omega_{1}=\omega_{2}
,
\omega_{3}=\omega_{4}
.
Next, keeping (
4.67
)-(
4.69
) in mind, let:

\begin{split}z_{h,0,\omega}&:=-i\mathrm{d}_{0}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})\qquad\qquad z_{h,1,\omega}:=\mathrm{d}_{1}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})\\
\tilde{\nu}_{h,\omega}&:=2^{-h}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})\qquad\qquad\tilde{\lambda}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}:=2\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4,0,0;\omega\omega\omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta})\;,\end{split}

(4.70)

so that

\begin{split}\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{2,0,0}(\psi;\phi,A)&=\sum_{\omega}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,[2^{h}\tilde{\nu}_{h,\omega}+iz_{h,0,\omega}k_{0}+z_{h,1,\omega}k^{\prime}_{1}]\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\\
\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{4,0,0}(\psi;\phi,A)&=\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{p}\,\tilde{\lambda}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}\hat{n}_{-\underline{p},\omega}\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\\
\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{2,0,1}(\psi;\phi,A)&=\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime},\nu}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{p}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,1;\omega\omega^{\prime},\nu}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega}(\lambda)-\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda))\hat{A}^{\nu}_{-\underline{p}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p},\omega}\psi^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}}\;.\end{split}

(4.71)

One may check that
\mathfrak{L}
is idempotent,
i.e.

\mathfrak{L}^{2}=\mathfrak{L}
; defining the
renormalization
operator
\mathfrak{R}:=1-\mathfrak{L}
, one immediately has that
\mathfrak{R}^{2}=\mathfrak{R}
as well.

Remark 4.3
.

The constants
\tilde{\nu}_{h,\omega},\tilde{\lambda}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}},z_{h,0,\omega},z_{h,1,\omega}
are real numbers, since the functional integral defining the partition function
\mathcal{Z}_{\beta,L,N_{0}}
in (
4.16
) is invariant under the transformation

\Psi^{-}\to\Psi^{+}\qquad\Psi^{+}\to-\Psi^{-}\qquad c\to\bar{c}\qquad k_{0}\to-k_{0}\;,

(4.72)

where
c
is a generic constant entering in the action. It should be noted that this symmetry can be implemented for any finite
N_{0}
, since the cut-off function
\chi_{N_{0}}
is even in
k_{0}
.

Next, we absorb the
\underline{k}
-dependent part of
\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{2,0,0}
in the covariance of the Gaussian measure
P_{h}
, obtaining:

P_{h}[d\psi^{(\leq h)}]e^{V^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)}=e^{\beta Lt_{h}}\widetilde{P}_{h}[d\psi^{(\leq h)}]e^{\widetilde{V}^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)}\;;

(4.73)

here
t_{h}
is a normalization constant, due to the change of covariance of the Grassmann integration. The new covariance is given by

\tilde{g}^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\widetilde{Z}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})}\frac{\chi_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})}{ik_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})k^{\prime}}[1+\tilde{r}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})]\;,

(4.74)

for a suitable new error term
\tilde{r}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
, and where:

\begin{split}\widetilde{Z}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})&=Z_{h,\omega}[1+z_{h,0,\omega}\chi_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})]\\
\widetilde{Z}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\widetilde{v}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})&=Z_{h,\omega}[v_{h,\omega}+z_{h,1,\omega}\chi_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})]\;.\end{split}

(4.75)

The effective potential
\widetilde{V}^{(h)}
has the form

\widetilde{V}^{(h)}(\psi;\phi,A)=\mathfrak{L}\widetilde{V}^{(h)}(\psi)+B^{(h)}(\psi;\phi)+C^{(h)}(\psi;A)+\mathfrak{R}V^{(h)}(\psi;\phi,A)\;,

(4.76)

with:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{L}\widetilde{V}^{(h)}(\psi)&=\sum_{\omega}2^{h}\tilde{\nu}_{h,\omega}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}+\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{4,0,0}(\psi;0,0)\\
B^{(h)}(\psi;\phi)&=\sum_{\omega,\rho}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\Big(\hat{\phi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega}(\lambda),\rho}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{1,1,0;\rho,\omega}(\underline{k^{\prime}})+\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\phi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega}(\lambda),\rho}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{1,1,0;\omega,\rho}(\underline{k^{\prime}})\Big)\\
C^{(h)}(\psi;A)&=\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime},\nu}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}d\underline{p}\,\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}}\hat{A}^{\nu}_{-\underline{p}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,1;\omega\omega^{\prime},\nu}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega}(\lambda)-\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda))\;.\end{split}

Finally, we set
Z_{h-1,\omega}:=\widetilde{Z}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})
and we rescale the fields:

e^{\beta Lt_{h}}\widetilde{P}_{h}[d\psi^{(\leq h)}]e^{\widetilde{V}^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)}=e^{\beta Lt_{h}}\widetilde{P}_{h}[d\psi^{(\leq h)}]e^{\widehat{V}^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z_{h-1}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)}\;,

(4.77)

where
\widehat{V}^{(h)}
can be represented as follows:

\widehat{V}^{(h)}(\psi;\phi,A)=\mathfrak{L}\widehat{V}^{(h)}(\psi)+\widehat{B}^{(h)}(\psi;\phi)+\widehat{C}^{(h)}(\psi;A)+\mathfrak{R}\widehat{V}^{(h)}(\psi;\phi,A)\;,

(4.78)

where

\begin{split}\mathfrak{L}\widehat{V}^{(h)}(\psi)&=\sum_{\omega}2^{h}\nu_{h,\omega}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}+\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{p}\,\hat{n}_{-\underline{p},\omega}\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\\
\widehat{B}^{(h)}(\psi;\phi)&=\sum_{\omega}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\Big[(\hat{\phi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega}(\lambda)},\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}))\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}+\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}(\mathsf{Q}^{-}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}),\hat{\phi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega}(\lambda)})\Big]\\
\widehat{C}^{(h)}(\psi;A)&=\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime},\nu}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{p}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,\hat{A}^{\nu}_{-\underline{p}}\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}}\;,\end{split}

(4.79)

with effective couplings

\nu_{h,\omega}:=\frac{Z_{h,\omega}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}\tilde{\nu}_{h,\omega}\qquad\qquad\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}:=\frac{Z_{h,\omega}Z_{h,\omega^{\prime}}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}Z_{h-1,\omega^{\prime}}}\tilde{\lambda}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}\\

(4.80)

and

\begin{split}\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h,\omega,\rho}(\underline{k}^{\prime})&=\sqrt{\frac{Z_{h,\omega}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{1,1,0;\rho,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\;,\qquad\mathsf{Q}^{-}_{h,\omega,\rho}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\sqrt{\frac{Z_{h,\omega}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{1,1,0;\omega,\rho}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\\
\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}&=\frac{\sqrt{Z_{h,\omega}Z_{h,\omega^{\prime}}}}{\sqrt{Z_{h-1,\omega}Z_{h-1,\omega^{\prime}}}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,1;\omega\omega^{\prime},\nu}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega}(\lambda)-\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda))\;.\end{split}

(4.81)

In the following, we shall use the notation:

\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega}\equiv\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega\omega}\;.

(4.82)

Remark 4.4
.

Notice that, since
\mathfrak{L}\widehat{V}^{(h)}_{4,0,0}
can be written in real space as

\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{x}\,n_{\underline{x},\omega}n_{\underline{x},\omega^{\prime}}\;,

(4.83)

we can suppose without loss of generality that:

\lambda_{h,\omega\omega}=0\;,\qquad\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}=\lambda_{h,\omega^{\prime}\omega}\;.

(4.84)

4.3.3
Single-scale integration

We now perform the single-scale integration that will yield
\mathcal{W}^{(h-1)},V^{(h-1)}
. In order to do so, we split the propagator as:

\begin{split}\tilde{g}^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}&=\hat{g}^{(\leq h-1)}_{\omega}+g^{(h)}_{\omega}\\
g^{(h)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})&=\frac{1}{\widetilde{Z}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})}\frac{f_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})}{ik_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})k^{\prime}}[1+r_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})]\\
\hat{g}^{(\leq h-1)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})&=\frac{1}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}\frac{\chi_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})}{ik_{0}+v_{h-1,\omega}k^{\prime}}[1+r_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})]\;,\end{split}

(4.85)

with
f_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\chi_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-\chi_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
. In the last identity, we used the fact that, for
\underline{k}^{\prime}
in the support of
\chi_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
:

\widetilde{Z}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\widetilde{Z}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})\equiv Z_{h-1,\omega}\;,\qquad\widetilde{v}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\widetilde{v}_{h-1,\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})\equiv v_{h-1,\omega}\;.

(4.86)

recall equation (
4.75
). Denoting with
\widetilde{P}_{h}(d\psi^{(h)})
the Grassmann Gaussian integration associated with
g^{(h)}
, we use the addition principle to write:

\begin{split}\int&\widetilde{P}_{h}[d\psi^{(\leq h)}]e^{\widehat{V}^{(h)}\big(\sqrt{Z_{h-1}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A\big)}\\
&\quad=\int P_{h-1}[d\psi^{(\leq h-1)}]\int\widetilde{P}_{h}[d\psi^{(h)}]e^{\widehat{V}^{(h)}\big(\sqrt{Z_{h-1}}(\psi^{(\leq h-1)}+\psi^{(h)});\phi,A\big)}\;,\end{split}

(4.87)

where
P_{h-1}[d\psi^{(\leq h-1)}]
is the Grassmann Gaussian integration associated with
\hat{g}^{(\leq h-1)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
. This identity allows to define the effective potential
V^{(h-1)}
and the effective generating functional
\mathcal{W}^{(h-1)}
as:

\begin{split}&\mathcal{W}^{(h-1)}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)+V^{(h-1)}\Big(\sqrt{Z_{h-1}}\psi^{(\leq h-1)};\phi,A\Big)\\
&\quad:=\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)+\beta Lt_{h}+\log\int\widetilde{P}_{h}[d\psi^{(h)}]e^{\widehat{V}^{(h)}\big(\sqrt{Z_{h-1}}(\psi^{(\leq h-1)}+\psi^{(h)});\phi,A\big)}\;.\end{split}

(4.88)

As reviewed in Section
4.5
, equation (
4.88
) is the starting point for the Gallavotti-Nicolò tree expansion for the effective potential, which will allow to control the flow of the effective potential. A key ingredient for this graphical representation are the bounds for the running coupling constants, whose flow is discussed in the next Section.

4.4
Flow of the effective couplings

The above iteration gives rise to a discrete dynamical system for the running coupling parameters, which has the following form:

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{h-1,\omega}}{Z_{h,\omega}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+z_{h,0,\omega}=:1+\beta^{z}_{h,\omega}

\displaystyle v_{h-1,\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{h,\omega}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}(v_{h,\omega}+z_{h,1,\omega})=:v_{h}+\beta^{v}_{h,\omega}

\displaystyle 2^{h}\nu_{h,\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{h,\omega}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2;\omega}(\underline{0}_{\beta})=:2^{h+1}\nu_{h+1,\omega}+2^{h+1}\beta^{\nu}_{h+1,\omega}

\displaystyle\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{h,\omega}Z_{h,\omega^{\prime}}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}Z_{h-1,\omega^{\prime}}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{4;\omega\omega\omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0}_{\beta},\underline{0})=:\lambda_{h+1,\omega\omega^{\prime}}+\beta^{\lambda}_{h+1,\omega\omega^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h+1,\omega\omega^{\prime}}+\beta^{\mathsf{z},\nu}_{h+1,\omega\omega^{\prime}}\;.

(4.89)

with initial data given by

\begin{split}Z_{0,\omega}&=1\;,\qquad v_{0,\omega}=v_{\omega}+O(\lambda)\;,\qquad\nu_{0,\omega}=\nu_{\omega}+O(\lambda)\;,\\
\lambda_{0,\omega\omega^{\prime}}&=-\lambda+O(\lambda^{2})\;,\qquad|\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{0,\omega\omega^{\prime}}|\leq C\end{split}

(4.90)

where the higher-order corrections in
\lambda
are due to the integration of the ultraviolet degrees of freedom. The vector field:

\displaystyle\beta_{h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{h}\big(\{Z_{k},v_{k},\nu_{k},\lambda_{k},\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{k}\}_{k=h+1}^{0}\big)

(4.91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\beta^{z}_{h,\omega},\beta^{v}_{h,\omega},\beta^{\nu}_{h,\omega},\beta^{\lambda}_{h,\underline{\omega}},\beta^{\mathsf{z},\nu}_{h,\omega})_{\underline{\omega}}

defined by (
4.4
) is called the
beta function
of the theory, and it drives the flow of the couplings. Precise estimates on this object are needed in order to safely iterate the equations in (
4.4
) in
h
. The control of the running coupling constants is the content of the following proposition. The result has been obtained for lattice fermions with two Fermi points in
[
11
,
13
]
, and it has been generalized to multiple Fermi points in
[
35
]
. We state the result, without discussing its proof.

Proposition 4.5

(Bounds for the running coupling constants.)
.

There exists
\bar{\lambda}>0
such that for
|\lambda|<\bar{\lambda}
the following is true. There exists a choice of real parameters
\{\nu_{\omega}\}
, with
\nu_{\omega}\equiv\nu_{\omega}(\lambda)
,
|\nu_{\omega}|\leq C|\lambda|
, such that:

\begin{split}\left|\frac{Z_{h-1,\omega}}{Z_{h,\omega}}\right|&\leq e^{c|\lambda|}\;,\qquad|v_{h,\omega}-v_{\omega}|\leq C|\lambda|\;,\qquad|\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}-\lambda|\leq C|\lambda|^{2}\\
|\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega}|&\leq C|\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{1,\omega}|\;,\qquad\left|\frac{\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h-1,\omega\omega^{\prime}}}{\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}}\right|\leq e^{c|\lambda|}\end{split}

(4.92)

for some positive constants
c,C
independent of
\beta,L
, for all
\omega,\omega^{\prime}=1,\dots,N_{f}
,
\nu=0,1
, and for all
h\geq h_{\beta}:=\lfloor\log(\pi/\beta)\rfloor
.

Remark 4.6
.

It should be noted that the independence of these bounds from
\beta
allows to control the dynamical system also in the zero temperature limit
h_{\beta}\to-\infty
.

The proof of this result uses the structure of the scaling limit of the system, which describes relativistic, chiral
1+1
dimensional massless fermions. The model describing this system, called the reference model, will be reviewed later on. The boundedness of the flow of the running coupling constants is a deep consequence of the vanishing of the beta function for the reference model, which can be viewed as an instance of the integrability of the scaling limit.

Observe that the wave function renormalization
Z_{h,\omega}
, has a divergent flow. We have:

Z_{h,\omega}\sim 2^{\xi_{\omega}h}\;,\qquad\text{with $\xi_{\omega}>0$, $\xi_{\omega}=O(\lambda^{2})$.}

(4.93)

As it is evident from the form of the single-scale propagator (
4.85
), the divergence of the wave function renormalization changes the scaling of the two-point function, and introduces an interaction dependent anomalous exponent. This is the reason for the rescaling of the fields in the multiscale analysis. Moreover, the vertex renormalizations
\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}
for
\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}
might also diverge with an anomalous power law. The fact that
\mathsf{Z}^{\nu}_{h,\omega}
stays bounded is non-trivial, and follows from the emergent (anomalous) chiral gauge symmetry of the model
[
12
]
.

Finally, proceeding as in
e.g.
Section 3.4 of
[
24
]
, it is possible to prove that the external
\phi^{\pm}
source term kernels
\mathsf{Q}^{\pm}_{h,\omega}
satisfy the recursion relation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h+1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-\widehat{W}_{2,0,0;\omega\omega}^{(h)}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\sum_{h^{\prime}=h+1}^{1}\hat{g}^{(h^{\prime})}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h^{\prime},\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}^{-}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})}\;.

(4.94)

In particular, for
\underline{k}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{supp}\,\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega}
, equation (
4.4
) reduces to

\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})[1-\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})]\;,

(4.95)

which, thanks to the estimates (
4.92
), and the bounds for
\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{2,0,0;\omega\omega}
which will be derived in Section
4.5
, implies that
\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h,\omega}=\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{1,\omega}(1+O(\lambda))=\xi^{\omega}(1+O(\lambda))
.

4.5
Tree expansion for kernels

In this Section, we recall the
Gallavotti-Nicolò
(GN)
tree
representation of the kernels
W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}
contributing to the effective potential
V^{(h)}
and of the generating functional
\mathcal{W}^{(h)}
. Although this is by now a standard construction (see
[
22
]
for a review), we prefer to recall some of its basic elements because they will be useful in deriving the improved estimates in Section
4.6
. We will discuss how to obtain a tree expansion for the effective potential in terms of trees that extend up to scale
0
, which takes as input the effective potential generated after the integration of the ultraviolet degrees of freedom; this is the infrared construction, and it is the technically most demanding one. The effective potential on scale
0
is in turn the outcome of an ultraviolet multiscale analysis, which is much easier to carry out and will be recalled in a second moment.

Employing the fact that the logarithm of a Grassmann Gaussian expectation may be written in terms of cumulants, we may transform equation (
4.88
), upon replacing
h-1
with
h
, by as

\begin{split}\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)&+V^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)\\
&=\mathcal{W}^{(h+1)}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)+\beta Lt_{h}+\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\mathbb{E}_{h+1}[\underbrace{\widehat{V}^{(h+1)}\,;\cdots\,;\widehat{V}^{(h+1)}}_{\text{$n$ times}}]\end{split}

(4.96)

where
\mathbb{E}_{h}[\,\cdot\,;\cdots;\,\cdot\,]
denotes the
truncated expectation
(or cumulant) with respect to the single-scale Gaussian Grassmann measure
\widetilde{P}_{h}
. Equation (
4.96
) can be iterated over all scales
h+1,\,h+2,\,\ldots
, until
h=0
and the result can be graphically expressed as a sum over GN trees.

Let us first introduce some definitions.

1.

An
unlabelled
tree is a connected, acyclic
1
1

1

That is, it has no loops.
finite graph connecting a point
r
, called the root, with an ordered set of
n\geq 1
points, the endpoints of the tree, so that
r
is not a branching point. The number
n
will be called the
order
of the unlabelled tree; in particular, the root
r
is not counted as a vertex. We will denote by
V(\tau)
the set of vertices of the unlabelled tree
\tau
.
The unlabelled trees have a natural partial ordering
\prec
, that is: orienting the tree from the root, we set
v\prec v^{\prime}
if there is an oriented path from
r
to
v^{\prime}
that passes through
v
.

We denote by
V_{f}(\tau)\subset V(\tau)
the set of endpoints, also called
leaves
or
final vertices
, of
\tau
. For each vertex
v
, we will denote by
s_{v}
its branching number: in particular, vertices with
s>1
will be called the
non-trivial vertices
. Notice that
s_{v}=0
if
v\in V_{f}(\tau)
.

Two unlabelled trees are identified if they can be superposed by a continuous deformation, which is compatible with the partial ordering of the non-trivial vertices. It is then easy to see that the number of unlabelled trees with
n
endpoints and only non-trivial vertices is bounded by
4^{n}
.

We shall also consider
labelled trees
(or just trees, in the following), see Fig.
2
; they are defined by associating suitable labels to the vertices of unlabelled trees, as explained in the following points.

2.

Given a labelled tree
\tau
, to each vertex
v
of the labelled tree
\tau
we associate a scale label
h_{v}
in
\{h+1,\ldots,0\}
, as in Fig.
2
. The scale of the root
r
is set to be
h
. Note that if
v_{1}\prec v_{2}
, then
h_{v_{1}}<h_{v_{2}}
.

3.

There is only one vertex immediately following the root, which will be denoted by
v_{0}
and cannot be an endpoint. Its scale is
h_{v_{0}}=h+1
.

4.

With each endpoint
v
on scale
h_{v}=0
we associate one of the monomials contributing to
\widehat{V}^{(0)}(\sqrt{Z_{-1}}\psi^{(\leq 0)};\phi,A)
. Instead, with each leaf
v
on scale
h_{v}<0
we associate one of the monomials contributing to
\mathfrak{L}\widehat{V}^{(h_{v})}(\sqrt{Z_{h_{v}-1}}\psi^{(\leq h_{v})};\phi,A)
. In particular, a leaf
v\in V_{f}(\tau)
will be called:

i.

of type
\lambda
if it is associated with
\mathcal{L}\widehat{V}^{(h_{v})}_{4,0,0}
;

ii.

of type
\nu
if it is associated with a
\nu_{h_{v}}
coupling constant;

iii.

of type
\phi
if it is associated with a
\widehat{B}^{(h_{v})}
term;

iv.

of type
A
if it is associated with a
\widehat{C}^{(h_{v})}
term.

5.

We introduce a
field label

f
to distinguish the field and external source variables appearing in the monomials associated with the endpoints. The set of field labels associated with the endpoint
v
will be called
I_{v}=\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{|I_{v}|}\}
and it will admit a partitioning
I_{v}=I^{\psi}_{v}\cup I^{\phi}_{v}\cup I^{A}_{v}
.

If
f
labels a field
\psi
, we denote by
\underline{x}(f)
its space-time position, by
\omega(f)
its quasi-particle label, and by
\epsilon(f)
its particle-hole label. We have an analogous labelling for the external sources
\phi,A
associated to the endpoint
v
, so that the monomial associated to it is given by

\prod_{f\in I^{\psi}_{v}}\psi^{(\leq h_{v}),\epsilon(f)}_{\underline{x}(f),\omega(f)}\prod_{f\in I^{\phi}_{v}}\phi^{\epsilon(f)}_{\underline{x}(f),\rho(f)}\prod_{f\in I^{A}_{v}}A^{\nu(f)}_{\underline{x}(f)}\;.

(4.97)

If
v
is not an endpoint, we shall call
I_{v}
the set of field labels associated with the endpoints following the vertex
v
, namely

I_{v}:=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v^{\prime}\in V_{f}(\tau)\\
v\prec v^{\prime}\end{subarray}}I_{v^{\prime}}\;.

(4.98)

r
v_{0}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{L}
h
h+1
h+2
\cdots
\cdots
h_{v}
h_{v}+1
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots
0
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{R}
v
\mathfrak{R}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{L}
\mathfrak{L}

Figure 2:
Example of a Gallavotti-Nicolò tree: each endpoint is associated to a monomial contributing to
\mathfrak{L}\widehat{V}
, while each internal vertex carries an action of
\mathfrak{R}
.

We shall denote as
\mathcal{T}^{\,m}_{h,n}
the set of GN rooted, scale-labelled trees with root scale
h
,
n
endpoints of type in
\{\lambda,\nu\}
, and
m
leaves of type in
\{\phi,A\}
. Then, we may rewrite equation (
4.96
) as a sum over GN trees, that is

\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)+V^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z_{h-1}}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)=\mathcal{W}^{(h+1)}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)+\sum_{n,m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\,m}_{h,n}}V^{(h)}[\tau]\;;

(4.99)

where the contribution subordinated to
\tau
is defined iterating on the vertices: if
v_{1},\dots,v_{s_{v_{0}}}
are the successors of
v_{0}
, and if we denote with
\tau_{v_{i}}
the (unique) sub-tree with root
v_{0}
and first vertex
v_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,s_{v_{0}}
, we have

V^{(h)}[\tau]=\frac{(-1)^{s_{v_{0}}+1}}{s_{v_{0}}!}\mathbb{E}_{(h+1)}\big[\overline{V}^{(h+1)}[\tau_{v_{1}}]\,;\cdots\,;\overline{V}^{(h+1)}[\tau_{v_{s_{v_{0}}}}]\big]\;,

(4.100)

with

i)

\overline{V}^{(h+1)}[\tau_{v_{i}}]=\mathfrak{L}\widehat{V}^{(h+1)}(\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h+1)};\phi,A)
if
\tau_{v_{i}}
is trivial
2
2

2

A tree
\tau
is said to be trivial if it contains only one edge (and hence only one leaf).
and
h+1<0
;

ii)

\overline{V}^{(h+1)}[\tau_{v_{i}}]=\widehat{V}^{(0)}(\sqrt{Z_{-1}}\psi^{(\leq 0)})
if
\tau_{i}
is trivial and
h+1=0
;

iii)

\overline{V}^{(h+1)}[\tau_{v_{i}}]=\mathfrak{R}\widehat{V}^{(h+1)}(\sqrt{Z_{h}}\psi^{(\leq h+1)})
if
\tau_{v_{i}}
is not trivial.

Using this inductive definition, the right-hand side of Eq. (
4.100
) can be further expanded, and in order to describe the resulting expansion we introduce the last ingredient to the labellings of our GN tree
\tau
.

6.

To any vertex
v\in V(\tau)
, we associate a subset
\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}\subseteq I^{\psi}_{v}
of fields
external
to the cluster
v
, with the following constraints:

i.

if
v\in V_{f}(\tau)
, then
\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}\equiv I^{\psi}_{v}
;

ii.

if
v_{1},\dots,v_{s_{v}}
are the vertices immediately following
v
on
\tau
, then

\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{s_{v}}\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{i}}\;.

We will denote by
Q_{v_{i}}:=\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}\cap\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{i}}
the set of fields external to
v
subordinated on
v_{i}
; the union of the subsets
\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{i}}\setminus Q_{v_{i}}
will constitute the set of fields
internal
to
v
, and each one of these subsets has to be non-empty if
s_{v}>1
.

For external sources, we simply set
\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}=I^{\phi}_{v}
and
\Gamma^{A}_{v}=I^{A}_{v}
.

We shall denote with
\mathfrak{L}_{\tau}
the set of all possible choices of external fields subordinated to the tree
\tau
, and we shall indicate its elements,
i.e.
given labellings, with

\mathbf{\Gamma}=(\Gamma_{v})_{v}\equiv(\Gamma^{\psi}_{v},\Gamma^{\phi}_{v},\Gamma^{A}_{v})_{v}\;.

Remark 4.7
.

In order to better explain the outcome of the iteration of (
4.100
) by employment of the external fields labellings
\mathbf{\Gamma}
, let us suppose for a moment that
\mathfrak{R}=1
: we shall take its action into account in a second moment.

Given an internal vertex
v\notin V_{f}(\tau)
, let us call
\tau_{v}
the (unique) sub-tree having
v
as first vertex, so that the scale of the root of
\tau_{v}
is
h_{v}-1
; we set

\begin{split}V^{(h_{v}-1)}[\tau_{v}]&=\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau_{v}}}V^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\tau_{v}]\\
V^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\tau_{v}]&=\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}}\widetilde{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}-1)}_{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}}\phi_{\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}}A_{\Gamma^{A}_{v}}\,W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}};\tau_{v}]\end{split}

(4.101)

where, as usual,
d\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}}=\prod_{f\in\Gamma_{v}}d\underline{x}(f)
and

\widetilde{\psi}^{(\leq h)}_{\Gamma}:=\prod_{f\in\Gamma}\sqrt{Z_{h,\omega(f)}}\psi^{(\leq h),\epsilon(f)}_{\underline{x}(f),\omega(f)}\;,\quad\phi_{\Gamma}:=\prod_{f\in\Gamma}\phi^{\epsilon(f)}_{\underline{x}(f),\rho(f)}\;,\quad A_{\Gamma}:=\prod_{f\in\Gamma}A^{\nu(f)}_{\underline{x}(f)}\;.

(4.102)

Remark 4.8
.

Strictly speaking, it should be noted that the kernels
W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}};\tau_{v}]
that we have defined depend on all the coordinates
\underline{X}_{I_{v}}
, not just on the coordinates
\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}}
; with a slight abuse of notation, we explicit only the latter ones because we want to highlight the ones associated to fields external to
v
.

We shall denote by
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}
a labelling for the sub-tree
\tau_{v}
; hence
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v_{0}}\equiv\mathbf{\Gamma}
. The recursion (
4.100
), together with the representation (
4.101
), implies the following recursion for the kernels
W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
:

\begin{split}W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}};\tau_{v}]=\frac{(-1)^{s_{v}+1}}{s_{v}!}\Big[\prod_{f\in\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}}\sqrt{\frac{Z_{h_{v},\omega(f)}}{Z_{h_{v}-1,\omega(f)}}}\Big]\Big[\prod_{i=1}^{s_{v}}W^{(h_{v_{i}}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v_{i}}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{i}}};\tau_{v_{i}}]\Big]\\
\cdot\mathbb{E}_{(h_{v})}\Big[\widetilde{\psi}^{(h_{v})}_{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{1}}\setminus Q_{v_{1}}};\cdots;\widetilde{\psi}^{(h_{v})}_{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{s_{v}}}\setminus Q_{v_{s_{v}}}}\Big]\end{split}

(4.103)

where
h_{v_{i}}=h_{v}+1
by construction, and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v_{i}}
is the restriction of
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}
to the sub-tree
\tau_{v_{i}}
. This iteration stops when
v
is a leaf, in which case we will have that
W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}\equiv W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\Gamma_{v}}
is equal to one the kernels of the monomials contributing to
\mathfrak{L}\widehat{V}^{(h_{v})}(\sqrt{Z_{h_{v}-1}}\psi^{(\leq h_{v})})
; in momentum space, we get

\widehat{W}^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\underline{K}_{\Gamma_{v}};\tau_{v}]=\begin{cases}\lambda_{h_{v},\omega\omega^{\prime}}&v\ \lambda\mathrm{-type},\ h_{v}<0\\
\nu_{h_{v},\omega}&v\ \nu\mathrm{-type},\ h_{v}<0\\
\mathsf{Q}^{\pm}_{h_{v},\omega}(k^{\prime})&v\ \phi^{\pm}\mathrm{-type},\ h_{v}<0\\
\mathsf{Z}^{\mu}_{h_{v},\omega\omega^{\prime}}&v\ A^{\mu}\mathrm{-type},\ h_{v}<0\\
\widehat{W}^{(0)}_{\Gamma_{v}}[\underline{K}_{\Gamma_{v}}]&h_{v}=0\;.\end{cases}

(4.104)

where as usual
k^{\prime}=k-k^{\omega}_{F}(\lambda)
, and where
W^{(0)}_{\Gamma}
indicates a kernel appearing in the monomial appearing in
\widehat{V}^{(0)}(\sqrt{Z_{-1}}\psi^{(\leq 0)};\phi,A)
parameterized by
\Gamma
.

Therefore, noticing that
\tau_{v_{0}}=\tau
, and writing the overall monomial associated to a given choice of
(\tau,\mathbf{\Gamma})
, namely

V^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]=\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}}\widetilde{\psi}^{(\leq h)}_{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{0}}}\phi_{\Gamma^{\phi}_{v_{0}}}A_{\Gamma^{A}_{v_{0}}}\,W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v_{0}}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}};\tau]\;,

(4.105)

we get

W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}};\tau]=(-1)^{n+m}L[\tau]\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}\frac{1}{s_{v}!}\sqrt{\frac{Z_{h_{v}}(\Gamma^{\psi}_{v})}{Z_{h_{v}-1}(\Gamma^{\psi}_{v})}}\mathbb{E}_{(h_{v})}\Big[\widetilde{\psi}^{(h_{v})}_{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{1}}\setminus Q_{v_{1}}};\cdots;\widetilde{\psi}^{(h_{v})}_{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{s_{v}}}\setminus Q_{v_{s_{v}}}}\Big]

(4.106)

with the short-hand
Z_{h}(\Gamma):=\prod_{f\in\Gamma}Z_{h,\omega(f)}
and with
L[\tau]
taking into account the contribution of the leaves, namely

L[\tau]=\prod_{v\in V_{f}(\tau)}W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\Gamma_{v}}[\underline{X}_{v};\tau_{v}]\;.

(4.107)

Comparing (
4.61
) with (
4.99
) and (
4.99
), we have thus obtained the following GN representation for kernels
W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}
appearing in
\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L}
and
V^{(h)}
:

W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}(\underline{X})=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{m}_{h,n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}\\
\Gamma_{v_{0}}=\Gamma\end{subarray}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}_{I_{v_{0}}\setminus\Gamma_{v_{0}}}W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\underline{X};\tau]\;.

(4.108)

This is useful because it allows to prove bounds analogous to (
4.32
) for the kernels
W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}
, with an explicit
h
-dependence which is uniform in
\beta,L
, as we now illustrate.

Remark 4.9
.

The
Battle-Brydges-Federbush (BBF) formula
, see
[
32
,
17
]
and references therein, and
[
22
]
for a review, allows to express a connected expectation between clusters as a sum over
spanning
trees over these. Given clusters
\{\Gamma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{s}
such that
\sum_{i}|\Gamma_{i}|=2N
, we have that

\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\Gamma_{1}};\cdots;\Psi_{\Gamma_{s}}]=\sum_{T}\sigma_{T}\Big[\prod_{\ell\in T}g_{\ell}\Big]\int d\mu_{T}(\mathbf{t})\,\det[G_{T}(\mathbf{t})]

(4.109)

where:

•

the sum is over so-called anchored (or spanning) trees
T
,
i.e.
collections of edges in
E(\bigcup_{i}\Gamma_{i})
that become a tree if each
\Gamma_{i}
is collapsed to a point
i
; furthermore,
\sigma_{T}\in{\pm 1}
is a sign associated to
T
: it will not play any role in the analysis;

•

the product is over the oriented edges
\ell
composing the anchored tree
T
, the factor being the propagator
g
evaluated on it;

•

the integration is over parameters
\mathbf{t}=(t_{ij})_{i,j=1}^{s}
, the probability measure
\mu_{T}
is supported on a set of
\bf t
such that
t_{ij}=\vec{u}_{i}\cdot\vec{u}_{j}
, for some family of unit-norm vectors
(\vec{u}_{i})_{i=1}^{N-s+1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{s}
;

•

G_{T}(\mathbf{t})\in M_{N-s+1}(\mathbb{C})
is a matrix whose entries are given by

(G_{T}(\mathbf{t}))_{(i,a)(j,b)}=t_{ij}\,g(\underline{x}(i,a)-\underline{x}(j,b))

where
i,j
parameterize the clusters and
a
(resp.
b
) a half-edge in
i
(esp.
j
), such that the oriented edge
((i,a),(j,b))
does not lie in
T
.

Employing the BBF formula for the expectations taken at each internal vertex
v
of the GN tree
\tau
, we may rewrite equation (
4.106
) as

\begin{split}&|W^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}};\tau]|\\
&\quad=\left|L[\tau]\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}\frac{1}{s_{v}!}\sqrt{\frac{Z_{h_{v}}(\Gamma^{\psi}_{v})}{Z_{h_{v}-1}(\Gamma^{\psi}_{v})}}\sum_{T_{v}}\Big[\prod_{\ell\in T_{v}}Z_{h_{v}-1,\omega_{\ell}}g^{(h_{v})}_{\ell}\Big]\int d\mu_{T_{v}}(\mathbf{t})\,\det[G^{(h_{v})}_{T_{v}}(\mathbf{t})]\right|\;.\end{split}

(4.110)

Now, recall the bounds on the effective couplings listed in Proposition
4.5
:

\left|\frac{Z_{h,\omega}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}\right|\leq e^{c|\lambda|}\;,\qquad|v_{h-1,\omega}-v_{\omega}|\leq C|\lambda|\;,\qquad|\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}|\leq C|\lambda|\;,\qquad|\nu_{h,\omega}|\leq C|\lambda|\;.

These bounds can be used to prove, following
e.g.

[
10
]
, Section 3.14:

\displaystyle\|\det G^{(h_{v})}_{T_{v}}({\bf t})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\sum_{i=1}^{s_{v}}\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{i}}|}{2}-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|}{2}-(s_{v}-1)}2^{h_{v}\big[\sum_{i=1}^{s_{v}}\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{i}}|}{2}-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|}{2}-(s_{v}-1)\big]}

\displaystyle|Z_{h-1,\omega}\partial_{0}^{n_{0}}\mathrm{d}_{1}^{n_{1}}g_{\omega}^{(h)}(\underline{x}-\underline{y})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{n+n_{0}+n_{1}}2^{h(1+n_{0}+n_{1})}}{1+(2^{h}\|\underline{x}-\underline{y}\|_{\beta,L})^{n}}\;,\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\;.

(4.111)

The first bound follows from the Gram-Hadamard inequality for determinants (using that the single-scale propagator admits a Gram representation, Eq. (3.97) of
[
10
]
), while the second follows from the smoothness and support properties of the single-scale propagator in momentum space: in particular, it implies
\|g^{(h)}\|_{1}\leq c\,2^{-h}
. Let
V^{od}_{f}(\tau)\subseteq V_{f}(\tau)
be the set of endpoints of the tree associated with
\mathsf{Z}^{\mu_{v}}_{h_{v},\omega_{v}\omega^{\prime}_{v}}
with
\omega_{v}\neq\omega_{v}^{\prime}
. Using the fact that

\sum_{T_{v}}1\leq s_{v}!\,C^{\sum_{i=1}^{s_{v}}\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{i}}|}{2}-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|}{2}-(s_{v}-1)}\;,

and defining the intensive
L^{1}
-norm

\|W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]\|_{1}:=\frac{1}{\beta L}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}_{I_{v_{0}}}|W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}};\tau]|\;,

(4.112)

the bounds (
4.5
) allow to prove (we refer to
e.g.

[
22
]
for the details) that

\begin{split}\|W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]\|_{1}&\leq C^{n+m}|\lambda|^{n}\prod_{v\in V^{od}_{f}(\tau)}|\mathsf{Z}^{\mu_{v}}_{h_{v},\omega_{v}\omega^{\prime}_{v}}|\\
&\quad\cdot 2^{h\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{0}}|+3|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v_{0}}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}_{v_{0}}|\big)}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|+3|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|+c|\lambda|\big)}\;,\end{split}

(4.113)

where
v^{\prime}
is the vertex immediately preceding
v
on the tree
\tau
, and hence
h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}}>0
(in fact,
h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}}=1
).
This bound however
does not
imply summability in the scale labels of the nontrivial vertices for all
{\bf\Gamma}
, since the factor multiplying
h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}}
may be positive; this happens when

(|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|,|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}|,|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|)=\begin{cases}(2,0,0)\\
(4,0,0)\\
(2,0,1)\;.\end{cases}

(4.114)

If
(v,\Gamma_{v})
is such that the first case is true, then it is called a
relevant
cluster; for the second or third case, it is called
marginal
; otherwise it is called
irrelevant
.

Remark 4.10
.

Another potentially dangerous case would be
(|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|,|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}|,|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|)=(1,1,0)
, but this cannot occur in an internal vertex, since it contributes to the local part, and hence it contributes only to the leaves. Moreover, cases with
|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|=0
also cannot occur in internal vertices other than
v_{0}
.

We take care of this apparent non-summability by reintroducing the action of the operator
\mathfrak{R}
in each single-scale expectation. Equation (
4.101
) becomes

V^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\tau_{v}]=\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}}\widetilde{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}-1)}_{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}}\phi_{\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}}A_{\Gamma^{A}_{v}}\,\mathfrak{R}W^{(h_{v}-1)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}_{v}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v}};\tau_{v}]

(4.115)

for all internal vertices
v
. Then, proceeding as in Section 3.14 of
[
10
]
, one may show that
W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]
admits the improved bound

\|W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]\|_{1}\leq C^{n+m}|\lambda|^{n}\Big[\prod_{v\in V^{od}_{f}(\tau)}|\mathsf{Z}^{\mu_{v}}_{h_{v},\omega_{v}\omega^{\prime}_{v}}|\Big]2^{hD_{v_{0}}}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}

(4.116)

with
D_{v}
being the (renormalized)
scaling dimension
of cluster
(v,\Gamma_{v})
, defined as

D_{v}:=2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|+3|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|-z_{v}+c|\lambda|

(4.117)

with the improvement
z_{v}
given by

z_{v}=\begin{cases}2&\text{if}\ (|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|,|\Gamma_{v}^{\phi}|,|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|)=(2,0,0)\\
1&\text{if}\ (|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|,|\Gamma_{v}^{\phi}|,|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|)=(4,0,0)\\
1&\text{if}\ (|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|,|\Gamma_{v}^{\phi}|,|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|)=(2,0,1)\\
0&\text{otherwise}\;.\end{cases}

This ensures that, for
|\lambda|
small enough,
D_{v}<0
for all internal vertices
v
, so that summation over
(n,\tau,\mathbf{\Gamma})
yields (see
[
22
]
for details, specifically Lemma A3 and Section A6.1)

\|W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}\|_{1}\leq C^{m}2^{h\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}|+3|\Gamma^{\phi}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}|-c|\lambda||\Gamma^{A}|\big)}\sum_{n\geq 0}\tilde{C}^{n}|\lambda|^{n}

(4.118)

for some constants
C,\tilde{C}
uniform in
\beta,L
, and with
m=|\Gamma^{\phi}|+|\Gamma^{A}|
.
In a similar way, one can prove that:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\beta L}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}\,\prod_{i,j\in\Gamma}\|\underline{x}_{i}-\underline{x}_{j}\|_{\beta,L}^{n_{ij}}|W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}(\underline{X})|\\
&\qquad\leq C_{\{n_{ij}\}}^{m}2^{h\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}|+3|\Gamma^{\phi}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}|-c|\lambda||\Gamma^{A}|-\sum_{i,j}n_{ij}\big)}\sum_{n\geq 0}\tilde{C}^{n}|\lambda|^{n}\;.\end{split}

(4.119)

Analyticity in
\lambda
follows from analyticity of all the effective couplings on scales
\geq h
, and from the convergence of the GN tree expansion. Finally, the bound (
4.119
), combined with equation (
4.95
), also allows to prove that
\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{h,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})=\mathsf{Q}^{+}_{1,\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})(1+O(\lambda))
, as anticipated at the end of section
4.4
.
We conclude this section with two important properties of GN trees, both of which will be employed in the next sections.

Remark 4.11
.

(On the external dimension). The term
-c|\lambda||\Gamma^{A}|
in the estimate (
4.119
) takes into account the (pessimistic) situation in which all
A
-endpoints are associated with off-diagonal vertex renormalizations, which diverge with an anomalous exponent; recall (
4.92
). In the application that follows we will be interested in bounding momentum-space correlations where the choice of the external momenta will imply that at most one
A
-endpoint is off-diagonal.

Remark 4.12
.

(The short memory property).
Thanks to the presence of the
z_{v}
factors in equation (
4.116
), each branch between two non-trivial vertices
v^{\prime}\prec v
comes with a factor
2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}
, where
D_{v}\leq D=-1+c|\lambda|
. This implies that
long trees are exponentially suppressed
: if one restricts the sum
\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}}
to trees having at least one vertex on scale
k>h
, then the final bound is improved by a factor
2^{a(h-k)}
, for
a\in(0,|D|)
. Clearly, the constants appearing will depend on the choice of
a
. This property is usually referred to as the
short memory property of GN trees
.

Remark 4.13
.

(The continuity property). Consider a tree
\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{m}_{h,n}
and denote its value with
\mathrm{Val}(\tau)
. Suppose that
|\mathrm{Val}(\tau)|\leq B_{D}(\tau)
, where
B_{D}(\tau)
is the dimensional bound of the tree, obtained as in (
4.116
), with
D_{v}\leq D=-1+c|\lambda|
for all
v
. Consider a propagator
\hat{g}^{(h_{v})}_{\omega}
arising in the truncated expectation associated to a given vertex
v\in\tau
. Suppose that
\hat{g}^{(h_{v})}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
is replaced by
\hat{g}^{(h_{v})}_{\omega}+\delta\hat{g}^{(h_{v})}_{\omega}
, with
|\delta\hat{g}^{(h_{v})}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})|\leq C2^{-h_{v}}2^{ah_{v}}
for some
1/2>a>0
, and admitting a Gram representation. Let
\mathrm{Val}(\tau)+\delta\mathrm{Val}(\tau)
be the new value of the tree. The contribution
\delta\mathrm{Val}(\tau)
can be bounded as follows. Let
\gamma_{r\to v}
be the path on
\tau
that connects the root
r
to
v
. Then, writing
2^{ah_{v}}=2^{ah}\prod_{v\in\gamma_{r\to v_{0}}}2^{a}
and using that
D_{v}\leq D
, we have:

|\delta\mathrm{Val}(\tau)|\leq 2^{ah}B_{D+a}(\tau)\;.

(4.120)

in a similar fashion, suppose that
Z_{h_{v},\omega}/Z_{h_{v}-1,\omega}
is replaced by
Z_{h_{v},\omega}/Z_{h_{v}-1,\omega}+\delta z_{h_{v},0,\omega}
, with
|\delta z_{h_{v},0,\omega}|\leq C2^{ah_{v}}
, or suppose that the effective coupling
\lambda_{h_{v},\omega\omega^{\prime}}
is replaced by
\lambda_{h_{v},\omega\omega^{\prime}}+\delta\lambda_{h_{v},\omega\omega^{\prime}}
, with
|\delta\lambda_{h_{v},\omega\omega^{\prime}}|\leq C2^{ah_{v}}
. Then, the new value is
\mathrm{Val}(\tau)+\delta\mathrm{Val}(\tau)
, with
\delta\mathrm{Val}(\tau)
again satisfying the bound (
4.120
). We shall refer to this property as the
continuity property of GN trees
.

Finally, the representation of the effective potential can be made even more explicit by using the tree expansion for the effective potential on scale
0
. This tree expansion is similar to the one described above, but much simpler (the ultraviolet regime is superrenormalizable). It is completely standard and largely model independent, see
e.g.

[
25
,
26
]
. Since we will use this more explicit representation later on, let us briefly describe it.

In the ultraviolet regime, the single-scale propagator
\hat{g}^{(h)}
is supported for momenta such that
2^{h-1}\leq|k_{0}|\leq 2^{h+1}
,
h=0,1,\ldots,N_{0}
. In configuration space, it satisfies the estimate:

\|g^{(h)}(\underline{x}-\underline{y})\|\leq\frac{C_{m}}{1+2^{hm}|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{m}_{\beta}+|x-y|_{L}^{m}}\;.

(4.121)

The single-scale propagator is not renormalized; in particular, there is no rescaling of the fields, in contrast to the infrared regime. The trees have endpoints on scale
\leq N_{0}
. The endpoints on scale
N_{0}
correspond to the bare quartic interaction or to the bare source terms. The endpoints on scale
h<N_{0}
have
|\Gamma_{v}|=|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|=2
; they can actually be “unfolded” in trivial trees with root on scale
h
and endpoint on scale
N_{0}
. The value of the corresponding kernel is the tadpole graph, evaluated with the propagator
g^{[h,N_{0}]}:=\sum_{h^{\prime}=h}^{N_{0}}g^{(h^{\prime})}
; it is finite uniformly in
h\geq 0
and
N_{0}
and of order
\lambda
. Finally, the scaling dimension associated with a vertex
v
that is not an endpoint is:

D_{v}=(1-n_{v})

(4.122)

where
n_{v}
is the number of endpoints following the vertex
v
on the tree. This can be written as
n_{v}=n_{v}^{\psi}+n_{v}^{A}
, where
n_{v}^{A}=|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|
and
n_{v}^{\psi}
takes into account the other two types of endpoints (quadratic or quartic in the fermions, and independent of
A
). Observe that by construction
D_{v}\leq-1
: the case
n_{v}=1
and
|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|=0
cannot take place, since the trivial subtrees are taken into account in the definition of endpoints. With these ingredients, it is clear that the estimate (
4.116
) can be replaced by a similar bound, for trees that are now extended all the way until the scale
N_{0}
, and with scaling dimension equal to (
4.122
) if the vertex is on scale
h_{v}\geq 0
, or equal to (
4.117
) if the vertex is on scale
h_{v}<0
. In the following, we shall denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{h,n}^{m}
the set of trees that extend in the ultraviolet regimes, with root on scale
h
, with
n
endpoints independent of
A
, and with
m
external source terms.

4.6
The correlation functions

Thanks to the previous Section, we may write the GN expansion for the generating functional
\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L}
. Indeed, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)&=\mathcal{W}^{\text{(u.v.)}}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)+\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{0}\sum_{n,m\geq 0}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{m}_{h,n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}\\
\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{0}}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\phi,A;\tau]\\
\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\phi,A;\tau]&=\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}_{I_{v_{0}}}\,\phi_{\Gamma^{\phi}_{v_{0}}}A_{\Gamma^{A}_{v_{0}}}W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}};\tau]\;,\end{split}

(4.123)

where
\mathcal{W}^{\text{(u.v.)}}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)
collects the contribution of trees with root on positive scales (purely ultraviolet contributions). In particular, since

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\,\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m-1}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}=(\beta L)^{m}\left.\frac{\partial^{m}\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L}(\phi,A)}{\partial\hat{A}^{0}_{-\underline{p}_{1}}\cdots\,\partial\hat{A}^{0}_{-\underline{p}_{m-1}}\partial\hat{A}^{\mu}_{-\underline{p}_{m}}}\right|_{A=\phi=0}\;,

(4.124)

we have:

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}=\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{u.v.})}+\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.})}

(4.125)

where the first term takes into account the purely ultraviolet contributions, and the second term all the other trees:

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.})}=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{0}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]\;,

(4.126)

where:

\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]:=\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}_{I_{v_{0}}}\,W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}};\tau]\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in V_{f}(\tau)\\
v\ A\text{-type}\end{subarray}}e^{-i\underline{p}_{v}\cdot\underline{x}_{v}}\;.

(4.127)

In equation (
4.126
), and until the end of this Section, the set
\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}
denotes

\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}=\{\tau\ |\ \text{$\tau$ has: root scale}\ h;\ n\ \lambda,\nu\text{-leaves,}\ 0\ \phi\text{-leaves,}\ m-1\ A^{0}\text{-leaves,}\ 1\ A^{\mu}\text{-leaf}\}

and the set
\mathcal{L}_{\tau}
parameterizes labellings of
\tau
with no fermionic fields
\psi
at the first vertex
v_{0}\in V(\tau)
and no external fields
\phi
, namely

\mathcal{L}_{\tau}:=\{\mathbf{\Gamma}\ \text{labelling of}\ \tau\ |\ \Gamma_{v_{0}}\equiv\Gamma_{v_{0}}^{A}\}\;.

Notice that the labelling
\mathbf{\Gamma}
is such that an
A
-type leaf
v
, having label
-\underline{p}_{v}
, carries a factor
e^{-i\underline{p}_{v}\cdot\underline{x}_{v}}
(
\underline{x}_{v}
being the integration coordinate associated to
v
), which allows to see (
4.127
) as a Fourier transform.

Remark 4.14
.

Recall that
\underline{p}_{i}=(\eta_{\beta},p_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,m
, and that
\sum_{i=1}^{m}\underline{p}_{i}=0
, hence
\|\underline{p}_{j}\|\geq\eta\geq 2^{h_{\eta}}
for all
j=1,\dots m
. Here
h_{\eta}:=\left\lfloor\log_{2}\eta_{\beta}\right\rfloor
, but for
\beta
large one may think of it as essentially equal to
\left\lfloor\log_{2}\eta\right\rfloor
. In what follows, we shall use this identification freely.

4.6.1
Bound for the correlation functions

We wish to find an
L^{\infty}
-estimate for
\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]
in terms of
\eta
, summable in
n,h,\tau
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}
.

Proposition 4.15

(Bound for density correlators)
.

Let
m\geq 3
, and let
\underline{p}_{i}=(\eta_{\beta},p_{i})
for all
i=1,\dots,m-1
, with
|p_{i}|\leq B|\theta|
. Then, there exists positive constants
C,c,\varepsilon
such that, for
\beta,L
large enough, for any
\mu\in\{0,1\}
:

\frac{1}{\beta L}|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m-1}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}|\leq m!\,C^{m}\eta^{2-m}(1+\mathbbm{1}_{m\geq m(\theta)}|\theta|^{\varepsilon/2+c|\lambda|}\eta^{-c|\lambda|})\;,

(4.128)

with
m(\theta)=K|\theta|^{-1}
and
C=\kappa\min_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}:\,\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}}|k_{F}^{\omega}-k_{F}^{\omega^{\prime}}|
for some
\kappa>0
.

Remark 4.16
.

For
|\theta|\leq K\eta
, as assumed in Theorem
2.6
, and choosing
\eta
small enough, the last term in (
4.128
) is subleading, and the bound reads:

\frac{1}{\beta L}|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m-1}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}|\leq m!\,C^{m}\eta^{2-m}\;.

(4.129)

The proof of the Proposition will use the following lemma.

Lemma 4.17

(Existence of a lower bound for the scale of a cluster with few external fermions)
.

Let
\tau
be a tree with
j>0
A-type leaves carrying momenta
\|\underline{p}_{i}\|\geq\eta_{\beta}
and such that
\left|\sum_{i=0}^{j}p_{i,0}\right|\geq\eta_{\beta}
. Consider a vertex
v
between the root and the last vertex with
\Gamma^{A}_{v}=j
. Then, if
|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|=2,4
, we have that
h_{v}\geq h_{\eta}-4
.

Proof.

Suppose
|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|=2
, then by momentum conservation the monomial associated to
v
has the general form

\widehat{W}_{\omega_{v},\omega^{\prime}_{v}}^{(h_{v})}[\underline{k},\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{j};\tau_{v}]\,\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{k}^{\omega_{v}}_{F}-\underline{p}_{\text{tot}},\omega_{v}}\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{k}^{\omega^{\prime}_{v}}_{F},\omega^{\prime}_{v}}

with
\tau_{v}
being the sub-tree with first vertex
v
, and where
\underline{p}_{\text{tot}}=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\underline{p}_{i}
. By construction, we have that

\|\underline{k}-\underline{k}^{\omega^{\prime}_{v}}_{F}\|,\|\underline{k}-\underline{k}^{\omega_{v}}_{F}-\underline{p}_{\text{tot}}\|\leq 2^{h_{v}+1}\;,

and using
\|\underline{p}_{\text{tot}}-\underline{k}^{\omega_{v}}_{F}+\underline{k}^{\omega^{\prime}_{v}}_{F}\|\geq|p_{\text{tot},0}|\geq 2^{h_{\eta}}
we obtain

2^{h_{\eta}}-2^{h_{v}+1}\leq\|\underline{k}\|\leq 2^{h_{v}+1}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad h_{v}\geq h_{\eta}-2.

If
|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|=4
, we have

\widehat{W}_{\underline{\omega}_{v}}^{(h_{v})}[\underline{k},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{q},\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{j};\tau_{v}]\,\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{k}^{\omega_{v}}_{F}-\underline{p}_{\text{tot}},\omega_{v}}\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{k}^{\omega^{\prime}_{v}}_{F}+\underline{q},\omega^{\prime}_{v}}\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}-\underline{k}^{\omega^{\prime\prime}_{v}}_{F},\omega^{\prime\prime}_{v}}\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}-\underline{k}^{\omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{v}}_{F}-\underline{q},\omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{v}}

and reasoning in an analogous way we obtain
\|\underline{q}\|\geq 2^{h_{\eta}}-2^{h_{v}+2}
; the computation above implies then that
h_{v}\geq h_{\eta}-4
.
∎

Remark 4.18
.

The argument of the above proof has the following corollary. Let
j=2
and suppose that
p_{\text{tot},0}=-m\eta_{\beta}
. Then, the scale
h_{v}
of the corresponding quadratic monomial is such that
h_{v}\geq\lfloor\log_{2}m\eta_{\beta}\rfloor-2
. This provides a lower bound on the scale of the endpoint corresponding to the external field
\hat{A}^{\mu}_{-\underline{p}_{m}}
.

Proof.

(of Proposition
4.15
.) Recall the expansion in equation (
4.126
). To begin, it is easy to check that the purely ultraviolet contributions is bounded in
\eta
: from the ultraviolet tree expansion, we have:

|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{u.v.})}|\leq m!\,C^{m}\;.

(4.130)

Consider now the second term in (
4.126
). Since by assumption
|p_{i}|\leq B|\theta|
for
i=1,\ldots,m-1
, for
|\theta|
small enough the marginal couplings associated with the fields
\hat{A}_{\underline{p}_{i}}^{\nu_{i}}
,
i=1,\ldots,m-1
, correspond to the vertex renormalizations
\mathsf{Z}^{\mu_{v}}_{h_{v},\omega_{v}\omega_{v}}
, that is with equal fermionic chiralities, by momentum conservation. These running coupling constants have a bounded flow, recall Eq. (
4.92
). Observe that this does not apply to
\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{m}}^{\nu_{m}}
: for large enough
m
, this external field might multiply fermions with different chiralities. The corresponding vertex renormalization might have an unbounded flow, (
4.92
); this is ultimately the reason for the last term in the right-hand side of (
4.128
).

Let us write:

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.})}=\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{d})}+\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{od})}

(4.131)

where the first term takes into account GN trees such that all
A
-endpoints on scale
<0
multiply multiply fermions with the same chirality (“diagonal endpoints”), while the second term collects the GN trees where
\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{m}}^{\nu_{m}}
multiplies fermions with different chiralities (“off-diagonal endpoints”). Let us start by considering the first term.

As a first simple step, we employ equation (
4.116
) to see that

\frac{1}{\beta L}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq 2^{h(2-m)}|L[\tau]|\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}},

(4.132)

where we used
|\Gamma^{A}_{v_{0}}|=m
, and where
L[\tau]
denotes the contribution of the leaves, namely

L[\tau]:=\prod_{v\in V_{f}(\tau)}\text{val}(v)\qquad\qquad\text{val}(v)=\begin{cases}\lambda_{h_{v},\omega\omega^{\prime}}&v\ \lambda\mathrm{-type},\ h_{v}<0\\
\nu_{h_{v},\omega}&v\ \nu\mathrm{-type},\ h_{v}<0\\
\mathsf{Z}^{\mu}_{h_{v},\omega}&v\ A^{\mu}\mathrm{-type},\ h_{v}<0\\
\|W^{(0)}_{\Gamma_{v}}\|_{1}&h_{v}=0\;.\end{cases}

(4.133)

Therefore, since
D_{v}<0
for all
v
, we obtain the thesis easily as long as the root scale
h
is greater or equal to
h_{\eta}-4
, in which case (
4.132
), combined with Proposition
4.5
, immediately yields

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{h=h_{\eta}-4}^{0}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,C^{m}\eta^{2-m}\sum_{h=h_{\eta}-4}^{0}2^{(2-m)(h-h_{\eta}+4)}\;.

(4.134)

Let us now consider the case
h\leq h_{\eta}-5
. Let us call
v^{*}
the first vertex, encountered starting from the root, such that all external field leaves are in the sub-tree
\tau_{v^{*}}
whose first vertex is
v^{*}
, and let us denote by
h^{*}
its scale label; then, for all
v
in the path
\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}
going from
v_{0}
to
v^{*}
, the number of
A
-fields is constant, so that

D_{v}=2-m-\frac{\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}}{2}+c|\lambda|

which implies

\frac{1}{\beta L}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq 2^{h^{*}(2-m)}|L[\tau]|\prod_{v\in\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}}2^{-(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})(|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|/2-c|\lambda|)}\prod_{v\notin\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\;.

(4.135)

If
h^{*}\geq h_{\eta}-5
, we do not need to obtain anything better than equation (
4.135
), since
|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|>0
for all vertices
v\in\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}
ensures summability over the labellings of
\tau
.

Let us now consider the case
h^{*}<h_{\eta}-5
. In this case, we need to examine carefully the structure of the GN trees contributing to (
4.126
). We notice that each
A
-type leaf
v
with label
(\mu_{v},\omega_{v},-\underline{p}_{v})
attached to the tree on scale
<0
is associated with a monomial (written in momentum space)

\mathsf{Z}^{\mu_{v}}_{h_{v},\omega_{v}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega_{v}}\hat{\psi}^{(\leq h_{v}),-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{v},\omega_{v}}\;,

which implies

2^{h_{v}+2}\geq\|\underline{p}_{v}\|\geq 2^{h_{\eta}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad h_{v}\geq h_{\eta}-2\;;

(4.136)

hence,
A
-type leaves attach at a scale (roughly) at least
h_{\eta}
: at least one of the
\psi
fields appearing in the labelling of each
A
-type leaf needs to be contracted at a scale greater or equal to (roughly)
h_{\eta}
.

All in all, we may summarize these findings as follows: as graphically represented in Figure
3
, there exists a collection of
1\leq s\leq m
disjoint sub-trees with root
v^{*}
containing all of the
A
-type leaves; if we call them
\{\tau_{i}\}_{i=1}^{s}
, we have

\Gamma^{A}[\tau_{i}]\neq\varnothing\ \ \forall i\;,\qquad\qquad\bigcup_{i=1}^{s}\Gamma^{A}[\tau_{i}]=\Gamma^{A}_{v^{*}}=\Gamma^{A}_{v_{0}}\;.

Since
h^{*}<h_{\eta}-5
, and since all
A
-type endpoints attach at scale at least
h_{\eta}-2
, each sub-tree
\tau_{i}
will have
1\leq\ell_{i}\leq|\Gamma^{A}[\tau_{i}]|
internal vertices
\{v^{**}_{i,j}\}_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}}\subseteq V(\tau_{i})
, such that:

1.

the associated subtrees contain all of the
A
-type leaves in
\tau_{i}
, that is

\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}}\Gamma^{A}_{v^{**}_{i,j}}=\Gamma^{A}[\tau_{i}]\;;

2.

h^{**}_{i,j}:=h_{v^{**}_{i,j}}=h_{\eta}-5
.

Noticing that condition 1 implies

\bigcup_{i=1}^{s}\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}}\Gamma^{A}_{v^{**}_{i,j}}=\Gamma_{v^{*}}\;,

we may number these vertices simply as
v^{**}_{j}
with
j=1,\dots,\ell
, and
s\leq\ell\leq m
.

r
v_{0}
v^{*}\;\;\;\;
v^{**}_{1}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;
v^{**}_{\ell}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;
h
h+1
h^{*}
h_{\eta}-5

Figure 3:
A representation of the structure of
\tau
: white diamonds denote
A
-type leaves, and big circles indicate sub-trees involving only
\lambda
or
\nu
type leaves.

Setting
\gamma_{v^{*}\to v^{**}_{j}}
to be the path from
v^{*}
to
v^{**}_{j}
,
\gamma_{*\to**}
to be the union of all such paths, and by setting
\gamma_{0\to**}:=\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}\cup\gamma_{*\to**}
, we are led to the following bound for the second product appearing in the left hand side of equation (
4.135
):

\begin{split}\prod_{v\notin\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}&=\prod_{v\in\gamma_{*\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\prod_{v\notin\gamma_{0\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\\
&=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}2^{(h^{*}-h^{**}_{j})|\Gamma^{A}_{v^{**}_{j}}|}\prod_{v\in\gamma_{*\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|}{2}-z_{v}+c|\lambda|\big)}\prod_{v\notin\gamma_{0\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\end{split}

(4.137)

where in the second passage we just used telescopic sums to factor out only the external field contribution to
D_{v}
. Keeping in mind that
h^{**}_{j}=h_{\eta}-5
for all
j=1,\dots,\ell
, and inserting (
4.137
) in (
4.135
), we obtain

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\beta L}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq|L[\tau]|\prod_{v\in\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}}&2^{-(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})(|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|/2-c|\lambda|)}\prod_{v\notin\gamma_{0\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\,\,\times\\
\times\,\,&2^{2h^{*}-m(h_{\eta}-5)}\prod_{v\in\gamma_{*\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|}{2}-z_{v}+c|\lambda|\big)}.\end{split}

(4.138)

Whereas the first line of (
4.138
) is summable over the scale labels, we see that the product over
v\in\gamma_{*\to**}
is possibly not summable in
h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}}
whenever
\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}=2,4
, since

2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|}{2}-z_{v}\big)}=\begin{cases}2^{h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}}}&\text{if}\ |\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|=2,|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|>1\\
1&\text{if}\ |\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|=2,|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|=1\ \text{or}\ |\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|=4,\end{cases}\ \ \geq 1;

(4.139)

this is obviously due to us extracting the contribution relative to the
A
-type external fields. In order to prove summability in the scale jumps
h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}}
, we would like to show that these instances cannot occur,
i.e.
that

\{v\in\gamma_{*\to**}\ |\ |\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|\leq 4\}=\varnothing\;.

We proceed by
reductio ad absurdum
: suppose there exists
\bar{v}\in\gamma_{*\to**}
such that
|\Gamma^{\psi}_{\bar{v}}|\leq 4
, then Lemma
4.17
would imply
h_{\bar{v}}\geq h_{\eta}-4
, but this contradicts
h_{v}\leq h_{\eta}-5
for all
v\in\gamma_{*\to**}
. Therefore, we have that

\prod_{v\in\gamma_{*\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|}{2}-z_{v}+c|\lambda|\big)}=\prod_{v\in\gamma_{*\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|}{2}-z_{v}+c|\lambda|\big)}\mathbbm{1}_{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|\geq 6}\;.

(4.140)

Inserting this estimate (
4.140
) into (
4.138
), we are led to

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\beta L}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq 2^{5m}|L[\tau]|\prod_{v\in\gamma_{v_{0}\to v^{*}}}&2^{-(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|/2}\prod_{v\notin\gamma_{0\to**}}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\,\,\times\\
\times\,\,&2^{2h^{*}-mh_{\eta}}\prod_{v\in\gamma_{*\to**}}\Big[2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\big(2-\frac{|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|}{2}-z_{v}+c|\lambda|\big)}\mathbbm{1}_{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|\geq 6}\Big]\;.\end{split}

(4.141)

The first factor in the second line is used to sum over
h^{*}\leq h_{\eta}-5<h_{\eta}
, and it allows to obtain the correct dimensional scaling in
\eta
. Furthermore, summability over
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}
and
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}
is ensured, since all summands involve now factors lesser than 1. In particular, since
A
-leaves are constrained to attach at scale at least
h_{\eta}-2
, choosing any
\varepsilon\in(0,1-c|\lambda|)
, we may extract a gain from the minimum depth of
\tau
(which amounts to
h_{\eta}-2-h
) by employing the short-memory property of GN trees; we have

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}^{\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,C_{\varepsilon}^{m+n}|\lambda|^{n}\,\eta^{2-m}\,2^{\varepsilon(h-h_{\eta}+2)}\;,

(4.142)

where the prime denotes the constraint that all
A
-endpoints on scale
<0
multiply fermions with the same chirality. Summing (
4.142
) over
n
and combining the resulting bound with (
4.134
), for
\lambda
small enough one gets the following estimate for the correlators, for
m\geq 3
:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\beta L}|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{d})}|\\
&\qquad\leq m!\,\eta^{2-m}\left[C_{\varepsilon}^{m}\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{h_{\eta}-5}2^{\varepsilon(h-h_{\eta}+2)}+\tilde{C}^{m}\sum_{h=h_{\eta}-4}^{0}2^{(m-2)(h-h_{\eta}+4)}\right]\\
&\qquad\leq m!\,C^{m}\eta^{2-m}\;,\end{split}

(4.143)

for
C=\mathrm{max}\{C_{\varepsilon},\tilde{C}\}
.

Consider now the second term in (
4.131
). Recall that this contribution is given by trees with one off-diagonal vertex renormalization, corresponding to the external field
\hat{A}^{\nu_{m}}_{-\underline{p}_{m}}
. The associated running coupling constant satisfies the bound:

|\mathsf{Z}^{\mu_{v}}_{h_{v},\omega_{v},\omega^{\prime}_{v}}|\leq C2^{-c|\lambda|h_{v}}\;.

(4.144)

By Remark
4.18
, the scale
h_{v}
satisfies
h_{v}\geq\lfloor\log_{2}m\eta\rfloor-2=:h(m,\eta)
; this scale constraint will ultimately allow to to show that the sum of all these trees gives a subleading contribution.

We distinguish three different cases for the scale
h
of the root: the first is
h\geq h(m,\eta)
; the second is
h(m,\eta)>h\geq h_{\eta}-5
; the last is
h\leq h_{\eta}-5
. Consider the first case. Denoting by a double prime the condition that there is exactly one off-diagonal endpoint:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,C^{m+n}|\lambda|^{n}\,2^{h(2-m-c|\lambda|)}\qquad\text{for $h\geq h(m,\eta)$.}

(4.145)

This bound is a direct consequence of (
4.135
), taking into account the bound (
4.144
) for the off-diagonal running coupling constant. Observe that, for
m\geq 3
:

\sum_{h\geq h(m,\eta)}^{0}2^{h(2-m-c|\lambda|)}\leq C2^{h(m,\eta)(2-m-c|\lambda|)}\leq K^{m}m^{2-m-c|\lambda|}\eta^{2-m-c|\lambda|}\;,

(4.146)

where we used that
h(h,m)\geq\log_{2}m+\log_{2}\eta-c
. In particular, since
2-m\leq-1
:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{h\geq h(m,\eta)}^{0}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,K^{m+n}|\lambda|^{n}\bigg(\frac{1}{m}\bigg)^{1-c|\lambda|}\eta^{2-m-c|\lambda|}\;.

(4.147)

Next, consider the second case. Here we proceed as in the previous case, with the only addition of using the short-memory property of the GN trees, Remark
4.12
, to take into account the fact that the off-diagonal endpoint is on scale
\geq h(m,\eta)
, which might be much larger than the root scale
h
. We have, for
h(m,\eta)>h\geq h_{\eta}-5
:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,C_{\varepsilon}^{m+n}|\lambda|^{n}\,2^{h(2-m)}2^{\varepsilon(h-h(m,\eta))}2^{-c|\lambda|h(m,\eta)}\;.

(4.148)

Observe that, for
m\geq 3
:

\begin{split}\sum_{h=h_{\eta}}^{h(m,\eta)}2^{h(2-m)}2^{\varepsilon(h-h(m,\eta))}&=2^{h_{\eta}(2-m)}\sum_{h=h_{\eta}}^{h(m,\eta)}2^{(h-h_{\eta})(2-m)}2^{\varepsilon(h-h(m,\eta))}\\
&\leq C^{m}\eta^{2-m}2^{(\varepsilon/2)(h_{\eta}-h(m,\eta))}\\
&\leq K^{m}\eta^{2-m}\bigg(\frac{1}{m}\bigg)^{\varepsilon/2}\;.\end{split}

(4.149)

In particular,

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{h=h_{\eta}-5}^{h(m,\eta)}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,K^{m+n}|\lambda|^{n}\eta^{2-m}\bigg(\frac{1}{m}\bigg)^{\varepsilon/2}\bigg(\frac{1}{\eta m}\bigg)^{c|\lambda|}\;.

(4.150)

For the third case, we proceed as in (
4.142
), extracting the short memory factor introduced by the off-diagonal endpoint. We have:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,C_{\varepsilon}^{m+n}|\lambda|^{n}\,\eta^{2-m}\,2^{\varepsilon(h-h(m,\eta))}2^{-c|\lambda|h(m,\eta)}\qquad\text{for $h<h_{\eta}-5$.}

(4.151)

Observe that:

\sum_{h\leq h_{\eta}}2^{\varepsilon(h-h(m,\eta))}\leq C2^{\varepsilon(h_{\eta}-h(m,\eta))}\leq C\bigg(\frac{1}{m}\bigg)^{\varepsilon}\;.

(4.152)

Therefore, we get:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{h<h_{\eta}-5}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m}}^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq m!\,K^{m+n}|\lambda|^{n}\,\eta^{2-m}\bigg(\frac{1}{m}\bigg)^{\varepsilon}\bigg(\frac{1}{\eta m}\bigg)^{c|\lambda|}\;.

(4.153)

The estimate for the second contribution in (
4.131
) is given by the sum of the estimates (
4.147
), (
4.150
), (
4.153
). Next, we observe that, in order for the off-diagonal vertex to be nonzero, we need to have that the spatial component of the momentum
\underline{p}_{m}
satisfies
|p_{m}|\geq c\min_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}:\omega\neq\omega^{\prime}}|k_{F}^{\omega}-k_{F}^{\omega^{\prime}}|
, which is only possible for
m\geq K|\theta|^{-1}\equiv m(\theta)
, since
|p_{i}|\leq B|\theta|
for all
i=1,\ldots,m-1
. We then have, for
m\geq m(\theta)
:

|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{od})}|\leq m!\,C^{m}\eta^{2-m}|\theta|^{\varepsilon/2+c|\lambda|}\eta^{-c|\lambda|}\;.

(4.154)

This bound, combined with (
4.130
) and (
4.143
), concludes the proof of (
4.128
).
∎

Proposition
4.15
provides bounds uniform in
\beta,L
large, and with the correct
\eta
-dependence, however they are not strong enough to ensure summability of the original (Wick-rotated) Duhamel series, Eq. (
3.12
). Summability could be ensured by assuming that
\|\hat{\mu}\|_{1}
is small enough. Remarkably, this is not needed; in the next section, we will improve the
m
-dependence of these bounds, in a way that will allow to prove summability of the Duhamel series (
3.12
) without any extra assumption on the strength of the external perturbation.

4.6.2
Improved estimates on the Euclidean density-current correlations

Keeping in mind the GN representation (
4.126
), in order to get bounds which are summable in
m
, we will consider separately the cases with few and many (with respect to
m
) interactions. Indeed, setting

\widehat{W}_{m;n}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{0}\sum_{\tau\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{h,n}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]\;,
