(4.155)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{h,n}^{m}
is the set of trees that extend in the ultraviolet regime, recall the end of Section
4.5
, we rewrite Eq. (
4.126
) as:

\begin{split}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}&=\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{u.v.})}+\sum_{n=0}^{n_{*}}\widehat{W}_{m;n}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\\
&\quad+\sum_{n=n_{*}+1}^{\infty}\widehat{W}_{m;n}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\;,\end{split}

(4.156)

with
n_{*}\equiv n_{*}(m):=\lfloor\alpha m\rfloor
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
constant to be chosen appropriately.
Let us start by considering the first sum in (
4.156
). We will show that this term admits an estimate with an improved
m
-dependence, with respect to the dimensional bounds in Proposition
4.15
.

Fixing a root scale
h
, a GN tree
\tau\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{m}_{h,n}
and a labelling
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}
, and recalling (
4.106
)-(
4.115
), we can write:

\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]=\sum_{{\bf T}=\bigcup_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}T_{v}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau;{\bf T}]

(4.157)

where
\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau;{\bf T}]
is constructed via the tree expansion described in Section
4.5
, keeping fixed the spanning trees
T_{v}
’s. Recall that each
T_{v}
arises from the use of the BBF formula, Eq. (
4.109
). In the estimate for
\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau;{\bf T}]
, the propagators associated with the edges of
{\bf T}
are bounded in
L^{1}
norm; in what follows, we shall discuss a modification of this part of the estimate of
\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau;{\bf T}]
, that will allow us to extract a summable factor in
m
.

To this end, let us analyse the structure of
\mathbf{T}
more in detail. Since it is given by the union of the anchored trees
(T_{v})_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}
, it follows that
{\bf T}
is itself a tree spanning all endpoints of
\tau
, and
|\mathbf{T}|=m+n-1
. Since
n\leq n_{*}(m)<m
by assumption, we see that any spanning tree
\mathbf{T}
will have a certain number of
chains
contained in it: by chain we mean the graph that is formed by only connecting
A
-vertices. If
n=0
, the only admissible graphs are (closed) chains. In general, if
n\neq 0
, there exists a set of chains
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{T}}\subseteq\mathbf{T}
with
N_{c}
connected components such that

\mathbf{T}=\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{T}}\cup(\mathbf{T}\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{T}})\qquad\qquad\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{T}}:=\coprod_{j=1}^{N_{c}}\mathbf{C}_{j},

(4.158)

and where each chain
\mathbf{C}_{j}
is obtained by considering the maximum number of consecutive edges between only
A
-type leaves along
\mathbf{T}
(see Fig.
4
for an example). Thus, the number of propagators in a chain with
m_{j}

A
-vertices is
|\mathbf{C}_{j}|=m_{j}-1
. It should be noted that the number of chains is bounded as
1\leq N_{c}\leq\max\{2n,1\}
.

\underline{x}_{1}^{j}
\underline{x}_{2}^{j}
\underline{x}_{m_{j}}^{j}
h_{1}
h_{2}
h_{m_{j}-1}
\underline{p}_{m_{j}}^{j}
\underline{p}_{1}^{j}
\underline{p}_{2}^{j}

Figure 4:
An example of a chain
\mathbf{C}_{j}
with
m_{j}

A
-leaves: external momenta injections are denoted by wiggly lines, and the scale of the
i
-th edge
\ell_{i}
on the chain is indicated with
h_{i}
. We have indicated also the position variables associated to each density leaf.

Notice that the number of chains
N_{c}
and their lengths
m_{j}-1
depend on the triple
(\tau,\mathbf{\Gamma},\mathbf{T})
, with the following additional constraint: by construction we have that

|\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{T}}|=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{c}}|\mathbf{C}_{j}|\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{N_{c}}(m_{j}-1)=m-N_{c},

and therefore
|\mathbf{T}\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{T}}|=n-1+N_{c}
.
Each
\mathbf{C}_{j}
may be parameterized (see Fig.
4
) by the ordered set of leaves connected by it, and these in turn are identified by the ordered external momenta
m_{j}
-tuple
(\underline{p}_{1}^{j},\dots,\underline{p}_{m_{j}}^{j})
and directed edges
(\ell_{i})_{i=1}^{m_{j}-1}
, with
\ell_{i}\equiv(\underline{x}_{i}^{j},\underline{x}_{i+1}^{j})
.

Therefore, the value of a chain may be written (up to the action of
\mathfrak{R}
) as

\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}_{j}}g^{(h_{\ell})}_{\ell}\equiv\prod_{i=1}^{m_{j}-1}g^{(h_{i})}(\underline{x}_{i}^{j}-\underline{x}_{i+1}^{j})

(4.159)

with
h_{i}\equiv h_{\ell_{i}}
. As we will show below, for the special choice of external momenta that we are considering, in which the temporal part of all but one external momenta are the same and equal to
\eta
, the presence of “long chains” will introduce a gain in the final estimate which is
factorial
in their length. In turn, this will imply an improvement in the bounds for the kernels
\widehat{W}_{m;n}
with
n<n_{*}(m)
, providing overall summability in
m
.

Lemma 4.19

(Bounding chains)
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathbf{C}
be a chain as described above, with
r
oriented edges labelled as
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{r}
, and scale labels
h_{1},\ldots,h_{r}
. Let us indicate with
h^{\mathrm{min}}:=\min_{i}h_{i}
the lowest scale; then, for any given choice of scales
\{h_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r}
compatible with the values of the external momenta, and for any subset
G\subseteq\mathbf{C}
containing at least one line with scale
h^{\mathrm{min}}
, we have

\prod_{\ell\in G}2^{-h_{\ell}}\leq\left(\frac{C}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(|G|-1)!}\;.

(4.160)

In particular, by picking
G=\mathbf{C}
we obtain

\prod_{i=1}^{r}2^{-h_{i}}\leq\left(\frac{C}{\eta}\right)^{r-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(r-1)!}\;.

Proof.

We start by considering the value of the chain in momentum space. We start by assuming that all propagators in the chain are on scale
\leq 0
. We will comment at the end on the case of chains extending in the ultraviolet regime.
Recalling (
4.159
), the value of the chain
\mathbf{C}
(up to the action of
\mathfrak{R}
) is given by

\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}}g^{(h_{\ell})}_{\ell}\equiv\prod_{s=1}^{r}g_{\omega_{s}}^{(h_{s})}(\underline{x}_{s}-\underline{x}_{s+1})

(4.161)

with
h_{s}\equiv h_{\ell_{s}}
. Observe that the chain is attached to an incoming and an outgoing line; taking into account these lines, the value of the extended chain is:

g^{(h_{in})}_{\ell_{in}}(\underline{x}_{in}-\underline{x}_{1})\Big[\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}}g^{(h_{\ell})}_{\ell}\Big]g^{(h_{out})}_{\ell_{out}}(\underline{x}_{r+1}-\underline{x}_{out})\;.

(4.162)

Observe that the coordinates
\underline{x}_{1},\dots,\underline{x}_{r+1}
, which are the coordinates of the
A
-leaves attached to the chain, only appear in (
4.162
), and nowhere else in the evaluation of the GN tree. Let us consider (
4.162
), multiplied by the phase factors
e^{-i\underline{p}_{i}\cdot\underline{x}_{i}}
attached to the external fields,
i=1,\ldots,r+1
. Using the Fourier representation for the single-scale propagator

g_{\omega}^{(h)}(\underline{x}-\underline{x}^{\prime})=\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,e^{i\underline{k}^{\prime}\cdot(\underline{x}-\underline{x}^{\prime})}\hat{g}_{\omega}^{(h)}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\;,

(4.163)

and introducing the short-hand

\underline{p}_{\leq s}:=\sum_{s^{\prime}\leq s}\underline{p}_{s^{\prime}}\qquad\forall s\in\{1,\dots,r+1\},

the Fourier transform of (
4.162
) is:

\begin{split}&\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{x}_{1}\cdots d\underline{x}_{r+1}\,e^{-i\sum_{s=1}^{r+1}\underline{p}_{s}\cdot\underline{x}_{s}}g^{(h_{in})}_{\ell_{in}}(\underline{x}_{in}-\underline{x}_{1})\Big[\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}}g^{(h_{\ell})}_{\ell}\Big]g^{(h_{out})}_{\ell_{out}}(\underline{x}_{r+1}-\underline{x}_{out})\\
&=e^{-i\underline{p}_{\leq r+1}\cdot\underline{x}_{out}}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{k}^{\prime}\,e^{i\underline{k}^{\prime}\cdot(\underline{x}_{in}-\underline{x}_{out})}\hat{g}^{(h_{in})}_{\omega_{in}}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\Big[\prod_{s=1}^{r}\hat{g}^{(h_{s})}_{\omega_{s}}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq s})\Big]\hat{g}^{(h_{out})}_{\omega_{out}}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq r+1})\;.\end{split}

(4.164)

Observe that, by momentum conservation, the momenta of the propagators on the chain are completely determined by the ingoing momentum
\underline{k}^{\prime}
and the external momenta of the source terms. By the support properties of the single-scale propagators
\hat{g}^{(h_{i})}
we see that

\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq s}\|\geq 2^{h_{s}-1}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad 2^{-h_{s}}\leq\frac{1}{2\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq s}\|}\;.

(4.165)

In what follows, we shall assume that
p_{i,0}=\eta
for all
i=1,\dots,r
. Later we will discuss the adaptation to the case in which one of the momenta among
\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{r}
corresponds to
\underline{p}_{m}
,
i.e.
it has temporal component equal to
-(m-1)\eta
.

Notice that the first propagator in the chain is supported for the following values of
k_{0}
,
|k_{0}+\eta|\leq 2^{h_{1}+1}
. We will partition this interval as follows. Let us define:

\mathcal{A}:=\coprod_{s=1}^{\bar{n}}\mathcal{A}_{s}\qquad\qquad\mathcal{A}_{s}:=\left\{k_{0}\in\mathbb{M}_{\beta}\ |\ |k_{0}+s\eta|<\eta/2\right\}

(4.166)

with
3
3

3

Since the width of the intervals is
\eta
, the number of disjoint intervals needed to cover the region
|k_{0}+\eta|\leq 2^{h_{1}+1}
is less than
\lfloor 2^{h_{1}+1-h_{\eta}}\rfloor
.

\bar{n}=\min\{r,\lfloor 2^{h_{1}+1-h_{\eta}}\rfloor\}
. Then, for all
k_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}
and for all
l\neq s
, we have:

\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l}\|\geq|k_{0}+l\eta|\geq\frac{|l-s|\eta}{2}.

(4.167)

Let us number the lines appearing in
G
with labels
l_{\alpha}
,
\alpha=1,\dots,|G|
. Consider the estimate for the chain in momentum space, in the support of
\mathcal{A}_{s}
:

1_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}\;,

(4.168)

for
\mathcal{A}_{s}
as in (
4.166
). If
s=l_{\bar{\alpha}}
for some
\bar{\alpha}=1,\ldots,|G|
, applying (
4.167
) to all the other lines with indices
l_{\alpha}
with
\alpha\neq\bar{\alpha}
, we obtain:

1_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}\leq\left(\frac{2}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h_{l_{\bar{\alpha}}}}}{(\bar{\alpha}-1)!(|G|-\bar{\alpha})!}\leq\left(\frac{4}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(|G|-1)!};

(4.169)

in the first step we used that
|l_{\alpha}-l_{\bar{\alpha}}|\geq|\alpha-\bar{\alpha}|
, and in the last step we used the binomial theorem combined with the fact that
h_{l_{\bar{\alpha}}}\geq h^{\mathrm{min}}
. Instead, if
s\neq l_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha=1,\ldots,|G|
, then
\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|\geq\eta/2
for
k_{0}
in
\mathcal{A}_{s}
. We write:

1_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}=1_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\Bigg[\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha=1\\
l_{\alpha}\leq s\end{subarray}}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}\Bigg]\Bigg[\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha=1\\
l_{\alpha}>s\end{subarray}}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}\Bigg]\;.

(4.170)

In the first term, we bound the denominator from below as
|k_{0}+l_{\alpha}\eta|\geq\eta(s-l_{\alpha}-1/2)
, while in the second term we bound the denominator from below as
|k_{0}+l_{\alpha}\eta|\geq\eta(l_{\alpha}-s-1/2)
. Consider the first product, and let us denote by
\bar{\alpha}
the largest
\alpha
in the product. We have:
(s-l_{\alpha}-1/2)=(s-l_{\bar{\alpha}}-1/2)+(l_{\bar{\alpha}}-l_{\alpha})\geq 1/2+|\alpha-\bar{\alpha}|
. Concerning the second product, we have
(l_{\alpha}-s-1/2)\geq 1/2+(\alpha-\bar{\alpha})
. Therefore, using these bounds for all
\alpha\neq\bar{\alpha}
, and estimating the term with
\bar{\alpha}
as

\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\bar{\alpha}}}\|\geq 2^{h_{l_{\bar{\alpha}}}-1}\geq 2^{h^{\mathrm{min}}-1}\;,

(4.171)

we get:

\begin{split}1_{\mathcal{A}_{s}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}&\leq\left(\frac{C}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(\bar{\alpha}-1)!(|G|-\bar{\alpha})!}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{K}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(|G|-1)!}\;.\end{split}

(4.172)

Next, let us consider the case in which
k_{0}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}
. In this case, we have:

\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l}\|\geq|k_{0}+l\eta|\geq\begin{cases}(l-1/2)\eta&\text{if $k_{0}\geq-\eta/2$}\\
(r+1/2-l)\eta&\text{if $k_{0}\leq-\eta r-\eta/2$,}\end{cases}

(4.173)

where the second case holds only if
\bar{n}=r
,
i.e.
if
r<\lfloor 2^{h_{1}+1-h_{\eta}}\rfloor
. Let us consider the first case. We apply the bound (
4.173
), together with
l_{\alpha}-1/2\geq\alpha-1/2\geq\alpha/2
, to all the lines with
\alpha\neq 1
and we estimate the line with
\alpha=1
by
2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}
. We have:

1_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}\leq\left(\frac{2}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(|G|-1)!}\;.

(4.174)

Instead, in the second case we bound the propagator with
\alpha=|G|
by
2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}
and we estimate the product over all the other lines in
G
using the second of (
4.173
). We obtain:

1_{\mathcal{A}^{c}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}\frac{1}{\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}_{\leq l_{\alpha}}\|}\leq 2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|-1}\frac{1}{\eta(r+1/2-l_{\alpha})}\leq\left(\frac{2}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(|G|-1)!}\;.

(4.175)

Combining the bounds (
4.169
)-(
4.174
) together with equation (
4.165
) only for lines in
G
, we are then led to

\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}2^{-h_{l_{\alpha}}}\leq\left(\frac{C}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}}{(|G|-1)!}

(4.176)

which is the claim, equation (
4.160
).

Let us now briefly discuss the case in which one of the
A
-lines carries a momentum
-(m-1)\eta
. Here, we divide the chain
G
in two parts, called
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
,
G_{1}
being on the left of the
A
-line carrying momentum
-(m-1)\eta
and
G_{2}
on the right. We extract the factorial gain separately for each part of the chain. We obtain:

\prod_{\alpha=1}^{|G|}2^{-h_{l_{\alpha}}}\leq\left(\frac{C}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\text{min}}}}{(|G_{1}|-1)!(|G_{2}|-1)!}\leq\left(\frac{K}{\eta}\right)^{|G|-1}\frac{2^{-h^{\text{min}}}}{(|G|-1)!}\;.

(4.177)

To conclude, let us comment on the case of chains extending in the ultraviolet regime. Here, the chirality of the propagators is not defined, so in the above argument the single-scale propagator
g_{\omega_{s}}^{(h_{s})}(\underline{k}^{\prime})
is replaced by
g^{(h_{s})}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{k}_{F}^{\omega})
with
h_{s}>0
. The analysis applies unchanged, using
2^{-h_{s}}\leq\|k_{0}+(\underline{p}_{\leq s})_{0}\|
. This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.19
.
∎

Next, we will use Lemma
4.19
to obtain an improved bound for the first term in (
4.156
).

Proposition 4.20

(Improved estimate for low perturbative orders)
.

For any
m\geq 16
,

\frac{1}{\beta L}|\widehat{W}_{m;0}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|\leq m!\,C^{m}\,\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}\,\eta^{2-m}(1+\mathbbm{1}_{m\geq m(\theta)}|\theta|^{\varepsilon/2+c|\lambda|}\eta^{-c|\lambda|})\;,

(4.178)

and for any
\alpha\in(0,1/34)
, and any
1\leq n\leq n_{*}\equiv\lfloor\alpha m\rfloor

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\beta L}|\widehat{W}_{m;n}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|\\
&\quad\leq m!\,C^{m+n}\left|\lambda\right|^{n}\,\left(\frac{2n}{m-32n}\right)^{m(1/4-8\alpha)}\,\eta^{2-m}(1+\mathbbm{1}_{m\geq m(\theta)}|\theta|^{\varepsilon/2+c|\lambda|}\eta^{-c|\lambda|})\;.\end{split}

(4.179)

Proof.

The starting point is the identity (
4.157
). Let us focus on the propagators that belong to the chains
\mathbf{C}_{j}
. One would be tempted to employ Lemma
4.19
with
G=\mathbf{C}_{j}
to extract a full factorial gain along the chain, namely

\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}_{j}}\|g^{(h_{\ell})}_{\ell}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C^{m_{j}}\prod_{i=1}^{m_{j}-1}2^{-h_{i}}\leq\left(\frac{K}{\eta}\right)^{m_{j}-2}\frac{2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}_{j}}}{(m_{j}-2)!}\;

(4.180)

with
h^{\mathrm{min}_{j}}
the minimum scale on
\mathbf{C}_{j}
; however, as it will become clear later in the proof, this will not work, due to the fact that the factor
(1/\eta)^{m_{j}-2}2^{-h^{\mathrm{min}}}
might be much bigger than the factor
\prod_{i=1}^{m_{j}-1}2^{-h_{i}}
. Estimating in this way the propagators that belong to
{\bf T}
would not allow to obtain an estimate for the corresponding GN tree that is summable over the internal scale labels.

In order to deal with this issue, we shall apply the previous reasoning to a proper subset
G^{j}\subset\mathbf{C}_{j}
of
good
lines, defined as follows.

Definition 4.21

(Good and bad lines)
.

Let
\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau;{\bf T}]
as in (
4.157
), and suppose that
\mathbf{C}_{j}
is a chain contained in
{\bf T}
. We define the set
B^{j}_{v}
of
bad lines
associated to a non-trivial, internal vertex
v\in V(\tau)
as follows. Let
\Gamma^{A,j}_{v}\subseteq\Gamma^{A}_{v}
be the set of
A
-lines corresponding to vertices belonging to the chain
\mathbf{C}_{j}
(see Fig.
4
), contained in the cluster labelled by
v
. Recall that
T_{v}
is the spanning tree of the cluster
v
.

a)

Suppose that
v
has no sub-clusters,
i.e.
it is followed only by leaves. If
|T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j}|<3
, then
B^{j}_{v}=T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j}
. Otherwise,
B^{j}_{v}=\{\ell,\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime}\}
for three arbitrary lines in
T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j}
.

b)

Suppose that
v
has at least one sub-cluster. The definition is given inductively, as follows:

–

if
|\bigcup_{\bar{v}\succ v}B^{j}_{\bar{v}}|\geq 3
, then
B^{j}_{v}=\varnothing
;

–

if
|\bigcup_{\bar{v}\succ v}B^{j}_{\bar{v}}|=3-k
and
|T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j}|<k
, then
B^{j}_{v}=T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j}
. Otherwise, if
|T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j}|\geq k
then
B^{j}_{v}=\{\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{k}\}
for
k
arbitrary lines in
T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j}
.

We define the set of bad lines on the chain
\mathbf{C}_{j}
as:

B^{j}:=\bigcup_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}B^{j}_{v}\;,

(4.181)

and the set of
good lines
in the cluster
v
and on the whole chain as:

G_{v}^{j}:=(T_{v}\cap\mathbf{C}_{j})\setminus B_{v}^{j}\;,\qquad G^{j}:=\bigcup_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}G^{j}_{v}\;.

(4.182)

Remark 4.22
.

Observe that if
|G_{v}^{j}|\neq 0
then
|\bigcup_{\bar{v}\succeq v}B_{\bar{v}}^{j}|\geq 3
.

For the moment, let us ignore the action of the
\mathfrak{R}
operator. We shall apply an estimate similar to (
4.180
), but only on the good lines. Consider a chain
\mathbf{C}_{j}
such that
m_{j}\geq 5
, and let
s_{j}\in\{1,\dots,m_{j}-1\}
be a scale minimiser on the chain,
i.e.

h_{s_{j}}=h^{\mathrm{min}}_{j}
. Then:

\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}_{j}}\|g^{(h_{\ell})}_{\ell}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C^{m_{j}}\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}_{j}}2^{-h_{\ell}}\leq C^{m_{j}}\left(\frac{C}{\eta}\right)^{|G^{j}|-1}\frac{2^{-h_{s_{j}}}}{(|G^{j}|-1)!}\prod_{\ell\notin G^{j}}2^{-h_{\ell}}\;;

(4.183)

we can further rewrite the right-hand side of (
4.183
) as:

\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}_{j}}\|g^{(h_{\ell})}_{\ell}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C^{m_{j}}\Big[\prod_{\ell\in\mathbf{C}_{j}}2^{-h_{\ell}}\Big]\left(\frac{C}{\eta}\right)^{|G^{j}|-1}\frac{1}{(|G^{j}|-1)!}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in G^{j}\\
\ell\neq\ell_{s_{j}}\end{subarray}}2^{h_{\ell}}\;.

(4.184)

Observe that the line
\ell_{s_{j}}
can be chosen to belong to
G^{j}
. This follows from the fact that, since we are assuming that
m_{j}\geq 5
, the largest cluster (
i.e.
the cluster on the smallest scale) containing the chain
\mathbf{C}_{j}
must contain at least one good line. The action of
\mathfrak{R}
on propagators of a chain is dimensionally equivalent to taking derivatives in real space of the propagators and multiplying them by weights, and it does not hinder the application of Lemma
4.19
. Its only effect is to change the first factor product in (
4.184
), ensuring that all cluster scaling dimensions are eventually negative.

The rewriting (
4.184
) allows us to recover the standard estimate, up to an extra multiplicative factor. Indeed, we have:

\frac{1}{\beta L}|W^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq|L[\tau]|C^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{T}}\left[2^{h(2-m)}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}\frac{2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}}{s_{v}!}\right]\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\frac{(C/\eta)^{|G^{j}|-1}}{(|G^{j}|-1)!}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in G^{j}\\
\ell\neq\ell_{s_{j}}\end{subarray}}2^{h_{\ell}}\;,

(4.185)

where, as usual,
D_{v}
indicates the internal dimension of the cluster
v
, namely

\begin{split}D_{v}&=2-\frac{|\Gamma_{v}^{\psi}|}{2}-|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|-z_{v}+c|\lambda|\qquad\text{if $h_{v}\leq 0$}\\
D_{v}&=1-n_{v}-|\Gamma_{v}^{A}|\qquad\text{if $h_{v}>0$.}\end{split}

(4.186)

The last two factors in equation (
4.185
) are the main difference with respect to the previous estimate (
4.116
). Let us focus on the factorial gain. By the multinomial theorem, we have that

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\frac{1}{(|G^{j}|-1)!}\leq\frac{(N_{c}-N_{c}^{\leq 4})^{\sum_{j}^{*}(|G^{j}|-1)}}{\big[\sum_{j}^{*}(|G^{j}|-1)\big]!}\leq\left(\frac{eN_{c}}{\sum_{j}^{*}(|G^{j}|-1)}\right)^{\sum_{j}^{*}(|G^{j}|-1)}

(4.187)

where
N_{c}^{\leq 4}
is the number of chains with
m_{j}\leq 4
and the star in the sum enforces the constraint
m_{j}\geq 5
. We shall derive a lower bound for
|G^{j}|
in terms of
m_{j}
; we start by noticing that
|G^{j}|=m_{j}-1-|B^{j}|
, so it is enough to find an upper bound for
|B^{j}|
. The worst possible case is when all
m_{j}
leaves are distributed in clusters with
|\Gamma^{A,j}|=4
, each containing
3
bad lines. Since the number of such clusters is bounded by
\lfloor m_{j}/4\rfloor
, we have

|B^{j}|\leq 3\lfloor m_{j}/4\rfloor+3

(4.188)

where the
+3
takes into account the case in which
m_{j}
is not divisible by
4
. Thus,

|G^{j}|-1\geq m_{j}-3\lfloor m_{j}/4\rfloor-4\;.

Hence,
\sum_{j}^{*}(|G^{j}|-1)
is bounded below as:

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\Big(m_{j}-3\lfloor m_{j}/4\rfloor-4\Big)&\geq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\Big(\frac{m_{j}}{4}-4\Big)\\
&=\frac{m}{4}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\leq 4\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\frac{m_{j}}{4}-4N_{c}^{\geq 5}\\
&\geq\frac{m}{4}-N_{c}^{\leq 4}-4N_{c}^{\geq 5}\\
&\geq\frac{m}{4}-4N_{c}\;,\end{split}

(4.189)

where
N_{c}^{\leq b}
(resp.
N_{c}^{\geq b}
) indicates the number of chains with length at least (resp. at most)
b
. Insertion of (
4.189
) into (
4.187
) yields then

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\frac{1}{(|G^{j}|-1)!}\leq\Bigg(\frac{4eN_{c}}{m-16N_{c}}\Bigg)^{\sum_{j}^{*}(|G^{j}|-1)}\;.

(4.190)

Observe that the number of chains is bounded as
N_{c}\leq 2n
, and we are assuming that
n\leq\lfloor\alpha m\rfloor
. Thus, for
\alpha
small enough, the argument of the bracket is less than
1
. Hence, we get an upper bound if we replace the exponent by the lower bound in (
4.189
):

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\frac{1}{(|G^{j}|-1)!}\leq(4e)^{m}\Bigg(\frac{N_{c}}{m-16N_{c}}\Bigg)^{\frac{m}{4}-4N_{c}}\;.

(4.191)

We then observe that for
N_{c}<m/17
the right-hand side is an increasing function of
N_{c}
. Thus, since
N_{c}\leq 2\lfloor\alpha m\rfloor
, for
\alpha
small enough we get:

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\frac{1}{(|G^{j}|-1)!}\leq f(n;m):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}&n=0\\
\displaystyle\left(\frac{2n}{m-32n}\right)^{\frac{m}{4}-8\alpha m}&\mathrm{else\;,}\end{cases}

(4.192)

reducing (
4.185
) to

\frac{1}{\beta L}|W^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq\tilde{C}^{m}|L[\tau]|f(n;m)\sum_{\mathbf{T}}\left[2^{h(2-m)}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}\frac{2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}}{s_{v}!}\right]\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in G^{j}\\
\ell\neq\ell_{s_{j}}\end{subarray}}2^{h_{\ell}-h_{\eta}}\;,

(4.193)

with
\tilde{C}=4eC
. Next, let us consider the last factor in the right-hand side. This factor is a priori dangerous, due to the fact that
h_{\ell}\geq h_{\eta}
. We will take care of this bad factor using the negativity of the scaling dimensions. Let us define the set:

\mathfrak{G}^{j}_{v}:=\Big\{\ell\in\bigcup_{v\preceq\bar{v}}T_{\bar{v}}\ \Big|\,\ \ell\in\mathbf{C}_{j}\setminus(B^{j}\cup\{\ell_{s_{j}}\})\Big\}\;,\qquad\mathfrak{G}_{v}=\bigcup_{j}\mathfrak{G}^{j}_{v}\;.

(4.194)

Observe that
\mathfrak{G}^{j}_{v}=\bigcup_{v\preceq\bar{v}}(G^{j}_{\bar{v}}\setminus\{\ell_{s_{j}}\})
. Also, observe that, as a consequence of Definition
4.21
,
|\mathfrak{G}^{j}_{v}|=0
if
|\Gamma^{A,j}_{v}|\leq 4
, and in particular
|\mathfrak{G}^{j}_{v}|=0
if
m_{j}\leq 4
. In fact, if
|\mathfrak{G}^{j}_{v}|\neq 0
then
|B^{j}_{v}|\geq 3
, which means that
|\Gamma^{A,j}_{v}|
must be at least
5
. Also, observe that
\mathfrak{G}_{v_{0}}=\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\geq 5}(G^{j}\setminus\{\ell_{s_{j}}\})
.

By a telescopic argument, we rewrite the last factor in (
4.193
) as:

\begin{split}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\prod_{\ell\in G^{j}\setminus\{\ell_{s_{j}}\}}2^{h_{\ell}}&=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
m_{j}\geq 5\end{subarray}}^{N_{c}}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}2^{h_{v}|G^{j}_{v}\setminus\{\ell_{s_{j}}\}|}\\
&=2^{h|\mathfrak{G}_{v_{0}}|}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})|\mathfrak{G}_{v}|}\;,\end{split}

(4.195)

which allows to rewrite (
4.193
) as

\frac{1}{\beta L}|W^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]|\leq\tilde{C}^{m}|L[\tau]|f(n;m)\sum_{\mathbf{T}}\frac{2^{h\tilde{D}_{v_{0}}}}{\eta^{|\mathfrak{G}_{v_{0}}|}}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}\frac{2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\tilde{D}_{v}}}{s_{v}!}\;,

(4.196)

with modified scaling dimension

\begin{split}\tilde{D}_{v}&=2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|+|\mathfrak{G}_{v}|-z_{v}+c|\lambda|\qquad\text{if $h_{v}\leq 0$}\\
\tilde{D}_{v}&=1-n_{v}-|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|+|\mathfrak{G}_{v}|\qquad\text{if $h_{v}>0$,}\end{split}

(4.197)

and modified external dimension

\begin{split}\tilde{D}_{v_{0}}&=2-m+|\mathfrak{G}_{v_{0}}|\qquad\text{if $h\leq 0$}\\
\tilde{D}_{v_{0}}&=1-n-m+|\mathfrak{G}_{v_{0}}|\qquad\text{if $h>0$}\;.\end{split}

(4.198)

The key feature that prompted us to extract a factorial gain only from the good lines becomes now clear: since by construction we have that

|\mathfrak{G}_{v}|\leq\Theta(|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|-5)\times\Big(|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|-4\Big)\;,

(4.199)

with
\Theta
the Heaviside function, it follows that
\tilde{D}_{v}
is always strictly negative, hence we have preserved summability over the internal scales and labellings of the GN tree. Had we taken as sets of good lines the whole chains, this might not have been the case.

In particular, the external scaling dimension
\tilde{D}_{v_{0}}
is also strictly negative: therefore, proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
4.15
, but with modified scaling dimensions, one is able to show that summation over choices of
(h,\tau,\mathbf{\Gamma},\mathbf{T})
yields

\begin{split}&\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{0}\sum_{\tau\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{h,n}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}|L[\tau]|\sum_{\mathbf{T}}\frac{2^{h(2-m+|\mathfrak{G}_{v_{0}}|)}}{\eta^{|\mathfrak{G}_{v_{0}}|}}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}\frac{2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})\tilde{D}_{v}}}{s_{v}!}\\
&\qquad\leq C^{n+m}|\lambda|^{n}\,\eta^{2-m}(1+\mathbbm{1}_{m\geq m(\theta)}|\theta|^{\varepsilon/2+c|\lambda|}\eta^{-c|\lambda|})\;.\end{split}

(4.200)

The bound (
4.200
) is proved as (
4.128
), replacing
|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|
in the proof of (
4.128
) with
|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|-|\mathfrak{G}_{v}|
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.20
.
∎

This improved bound is enough to show a summable estimate for the
m
-point cumulants.

Proposition 4.23

(Improved estimate for density correlators)
.

For
m\geq 16
, there exists
C>0
, such that for
\lambda
sufficiently small (but fixed), and for
\alpha\in(0,1/34)
, we have that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\beta L}|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m-1}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}|\\
&\quad\leq m!\,\eta^{2-m}\,C^{m}\left[\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}+\left(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\right)^{m(1/4-8\alpha)}+\lambda^{\alpha m}\right]R_{m}(\eta,\theta)\end{split}

(4.201)

with

R_{m}(\eta,\theta)=(1+\mathbbm{1}_{m\geq m(\theta)}|\theta|^{\varepsilon/2+c|\lambda|}\eta^{-c|\lambda|})\;.

(4.202)

Proof.

The starting point is Eq. (
4.156
). As already commented, the first term in the right-hand side is bounded by
C^{m}m!
, which is much smaller than the right-hand side of (
4.201
). Then, to estimate the first sum in the right-hand side of (
4.156
) we use Proposition
4.20
. The contribution with
n=0
reproduces the first term in the right-hand side of (
4.201
). The other terms in the sum are bounded as:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{n_{*}}\widehat{W}_{m;n}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\right|\leq m!\,C^{m}\,\left(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\right)^{m(1/4-8\alpha)}\,\eta^{2-m}R_{m}(\eta,\theta)\sum_{n=1}^{n_{*}}C^{n}\left|\lambda\right|^{n}\;.

(4.203)

and the thesis is obtained by estimating the sum with
C|\lambda|/(1-C|\lambda|)
. Finally, consider the second sum in the right-hand side of (
4.156
). Here it is sufficient to proceed as in the proof of Proposition
4.15
:

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\beta L}\left|\sum_{n>n_{*}}\widehat{W}_{m;n}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\right|&\leq m!\,C^{m}\,\eta^{2-m}R_{m}(\eta,\theta)\sum_{n>n_{*}}C^{n}|\lambda|^{n}\\
&\leq m!\,C^{m(1+\alpha)}|\lambda|^{\alpha m}\,\eta^{2-m}R_{m}(\eta,\theta)\;.\end{split}

(4.204)

This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Remark 4.24
.

(i)

Recall that the presence of the non-trivial factor
R_{m}(\eta,\theta)
is due to the estimate for the GN trees with one off-diagonal
A
endpoint. If we only restrict to the contribution of these trees, we have:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\beta L}|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m-1}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{od})}_{\beta,L}|\leq C^{m}m!\,\eta^{2-m}\\
&\quad\cdot\left[\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}+\left(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\right)^{m(1/4-8\alpha)}+\lambda^{\alpha m}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{m\geq m(\theta)}|\theta|^{\varepsilon/2+c|\lambda|}\eta^{-c|\lambda|}\;.\end{split}

(4.205)

Plugging this estimate in (
3.12
), we see that the corresponding contribution is summable in
m
and it is vanishing as
\eta\to 0^{+}
, for
|\theta|\leq K\eta
and for
|\lambda|,\eta
small enough, as assumed in Theorem
2.6
.

(ii)

Proposition
4.23
allows to prove that the contribution of infrared trees with diagonal
A
end-points is summable, but so far the bounds are not strong enough to show that the linear response dominates. Actually, without a smallness condition on the perturbation, the sum of the higher order terms is of the same order of the linear response. In order to prove that the higher order terms are indeed subleading as
\eta\to 0
, we will prove a cancellation for the scaling limit of the density-density correlation functions, which is reminiscent of bosonization.

5
The reference model

Here we discuss a
1+1
dimensional quantum field theory, called the reference model, that describes the infrared scaling limit of the correlation functions of the lattice model. We will follow the work
[
35
]
, to which we refer for further details. The key feature of the model is that its correlation functions can be explicitly determined, combining Schwinger-Dyson equations and Ward identities, associated with the (anomalous) chiral gauge symmetry of the QFT. Also, the low-energy behavior of the lattice correlation functions can be quantitatively captured by the reference model, for a suitable choice of parameters.

Remark 5.1
.

All bare parameters of this reference model QFT should carry a ‘ref’ superscript to distinguish them from the corresponding lattice model ones. In order to avoid cluttering an already heavy notation, we shall omit such superscript for the rest of this section (unless otherwise stated). We will reintroduce it in Section
6
, when comparing lattice and reference model correlations.

5.1
Sets and cutoffs

Let
\beta>0
, and let
L,\mathfrak{N}_{0},\mathfrak{N}_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathfrak{a}:=\beta/\mathfrak{N}_{0}=L/\mathfrak{N}_{1}
. We define the space-time lattice of sides
\beta,L
and mesh
\mathfrak{a}
as:

\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}:=\left\{\underline{x}=(n_{0}\mathfrak{a},n_{1}\mathfrak{a})\;|\;0\leq n_{\mu}\leq\mathfrak{N}_{\mu}-1\right\}.

(5.1)

Also, we define the set of momenta compatible with antiperiodic boundary conditions as:

\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}:=\left\{\underline{k}=\left(\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\left(m_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\right),\frac{2\pi}{L}\left(m_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\;\Bigg|\;0\leq m_{\mu}\leq\mathfrak{N}_{\mu}-1\right\}.

(5.2)

We will consider Grassmann fields defined on
\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}
, satisfying antiperiodic boundary conditions. Let
\chi
be the smooth cutoff function defined in equation (
4.1
). We define its
\varepsilon
-deformation as:

\chi^{\varepsilon}(t)=C_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\infty}dse^{-|t-s|^{2}/\varepsilon}\chi(s)\;,

(5.3)

where
C_{\varepsilon}
is chosen such that
C_{\varepsilon}\int dt\,e^{-|t-s|^{2}/\varepsilon}=1
. We have that
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\chi^{\varepsilon}(t)=\chi(t)
and
\chi^{\varepsilon}(t)>0
for all
t
. Furthermore, given
v_{\omega}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
for
\omega=1,\dots,N_{f}
, we define the norm:

|\underline{k}|_{\omega}:=\sqrt{k_{0}^{2}+v_{\omega}^{2}k_{1}^{2}}

(5.4)

where the collection
\{v_{\omega}\}_{\omega}
will play the role of bare velocities for the reference model. The set
\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}
is naturally equipped with periodic boundary conditions. Thus, it is convenient to adapt this norm to
\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}
by introducing:

\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega}:=\inf_{a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}|\underline{k}-a_{1}\underline{G}_{1}-a_{2}\underline{G}_{2}|_{\omega}\;,

(5.5)

with
\underline{G}_{1}=(2\pi/\mathfrak{a},0)
,
\underline{G}_{2}=(0,2\pi/\mathfrak{a})
. Finally, we shall introduce a function encoding an ultraviolet cutoff, as follows:

\chi^{\omega,\varepsilon}_{N}(\underline{k}):=\chi^{\varepsilon}(2^{-N}\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega})\;.

(5.6)

Observe that the function
\chi^{\omega}_{N}(\underline{k})=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\chi^{\omega,\varepsilon}_{N}(\underline{k})
is supported for momenta
\underline{k}
such that
\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega}\leq\frac{3}{2}2^{N}
. The limit
N\to\infty
will be referred to as the ultraviolet limit.

5.2
Fields and propagators

To each
\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}
, and for each
\omega=1,\ldots,N_{f}
, we associate Grassmann variables
\hat{\psi}^{\pm}_{\underline{k},\omega}
. They are extended periodically to the whole
\frac{2\pi}{\beta}(\mathbb{Z}+1/2)\times\frac{2\pi}{L}(\mathbb{Z}+1/2)
as:

\hat{\psi}^{\pm}_{\underline{k}+a_{1}\underline{G}_{1}+a_{2}\underline{G}_{2},\omega}:=\hat{\psi}^{\pm}_{\underline{k},\omega}\qquad\forall(a_{1},a_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\;.

(5.7)

The configuration-space Grassmann field is then:

{\psi}^{\pm}_{\underline{x},\omega}:=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}e^{\mp i\underline{k}\cdot\underline{x}}\hat{\psi}^{\pm}_{\underline{k},\omega}\;,

(5.8)

and we notice that it satisfies
(\beta,L)
-antiperiodicity in space-time. The relation (
5.8
) can be inverted as:

\hat{\psi}^{\pm}_{\underline{k},\omega}=\mathfrak{a}^{2}\sum_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}e^{\pm i\underline{k}\cdot\underline{x}}\psi^{\pm}_{\underline{x},\omega}\;.

(5.9)

The momentum-space propagator is set to be, for
\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}
,

\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k}):=\frac{1}{Z_{\omega}}\frac{\chi^{\omega,\varepsilon}_{N}(\underline{k})}{\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})}\;,\quad\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k}):=\frac{i}{\mathfrak{a}}\sin(\mathfrak{a}k_{0})+\frac{v_{\omega}}{\mathfrak{a}}\sin(\mathfrak{a}k_{1})\;,

(5.10)

where
Z_{\omega},v_{\omega}
are free parameters, to be suitably chosen later on. Observe that the denominator in (
5.10
) vanishes for
\underline{k}=(0,0),(\pi/\mathfrak{a},0),(0,\pi/\mathfrak{a}),(\pi/\mathfrak{a},\pi/\mathfrak{a})
(and their translations by
\underline{G}_{i}
). However, for
\mathfrak{a}>0
these points do not belong
\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}
, which means that the propagator is well-defined. Let us define:

\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}):=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{\mathfrak{a}\to 0}\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})=\frac{1}{Z_{\omega}}\frac{\chi^{\omega}_{N}(\underline{k})}{ik_{0}+v_{\omega}k_{1}}\;.

(5.11)

This limiting propagator is only singular at
(0,0)
(and its translations). For non-zero
\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}
, the propagator is arbitrarily small at
(\pi/\mathfrak{a},0),(0,\pi/\mathfrak{a}),(\pi/\mathfrak{a},\pi/\mathfrak{a})
, provided
\mathfrak{a}
is small enough. Thus, in the regime
\mathfrak{a}\ll\varepsilon
, the main singular contribution is due to the point
(0,0)
. Finally, the real-space propagator is:

{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{x}):=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}e^{i\underline{k}\cdot\underline{x}}\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})\;,\qquad\forall\underline{x}\in\mathfrak{a}\mathbb{Z}^{2}\;.

(5.12)

5.3
Generating functional of correlations

Let
P_{N}[d\psi]
be the Grassmann Gaussian integration with the propagators
\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})
:

\int P_{N}[d\psi]\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{q},\omega^{\prime}}=\beta L\delta_{\underline{k},\underline{q}}\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\,\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})\;;

(5.13)

equivalently,

\int P_{N}[d\psi]\psi^{-}_{\underline{x},\omega}\psi^{+}_{\underline{y},\omega^{\prime}}=\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\,{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{x}-\underline{y})\;.

(5.14)

The interaction of the Grassmann QFT is defined as:

V(\psi):=\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{4}}{2}\sum_{\underline{x},\underline{y}\in\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}Z_{\omega}Z_{\omega^{\prime}}v(\underline{x}-\underline{y})n_{\underline{x},\omega}n_{\underline{y},\omega^{\prime}}

(5.15)

with
\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R},\;\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}=\lambda_{\omega^{\prime}\omega},\;\lambda_{\omega\omega}=0
. The function
v
satisfies the periodicity condition:

v(\underline{x}+a_{0}\beta\underline{e}_{0}+a_{1}L\underline{e}_{1})=v(\underline{x})\qquad\qquad\forall(a_{0},a_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},

(5.16)

and also
\hat{v}(0)=1
. In equation (
5.15
),
n_{\underline{x},\omega}=\psi^{+}_{\underline{x},\omega}\psi^{-}_{\underline{x},\omega}
is the Grassmann counterpart of the density operator, as usual. Let
\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}
be the set of momenta compatible with periodic boundary conditions:

\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}:=\left\{\underline{p}=\left(\frac{2\pi}{\beta}n_{0},\frac{2\pi}{L}n_{1}\right)\;\Bigg|\;(n_{0},n_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\right\}.

(5.17)

Then, we can write:

v(\underline{x})=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{p}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}e^{i\underline{p}\cdot\underline{x}}\,\hat{v}(\underline{p})\;,\qquad\hat{v}(\underline{p})=\mathfrak{a}^{2}\sum_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}e^{-i\underline{p}\cdot\underline{x}}\,v(\underline{x})\;.

(5.18)

We will suppose the function
\hat{v}
to be the restriction to
\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}
of an even, smooth and compactly supported function on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, so that

\|\underline{x}\|^{n}|v(\underline{x})|\leq C_{n}\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N},

(5.19)

where
\|\underline{x}\|:=\inf_{a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}|\underline{x}-a_{1}L\underline{e}_{1}-a_{2}L\underline{e}_{2}|
. In Fourier space, equation (
5.15
) reads:

V(\psi)=\frac{1}{2\beta L}\sum_{\underline{p}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}Z_{\omega}Z_{\omega^{\prime}}\hat{v}(\underline{p})\hat{n}_{-\underline{p},\omega}\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}

(5.20)

where

\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega}=\mathfrak{a}^{2}\sum_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}e^{-i\underline{p}\cdot\underline{x}}n_{\underline{x},\omega}=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega}.

(5.21)

Let us define the source terms as:

\begin{split}B(\psi;\phi)&:=\sum_{\omega=1}^{N_{f}}\mathfrak{a}^{2}\sum_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}\left[\psi^{+}_{\underline{x},\omega}\mathsf{Q}_{\omega}\phi^{-}_{\underline{x},\omega}+\phi^{+}_{\underline{x},\omega}\bar{\mathsf{Q}}_{\omega}\psi^{-}_{\underline{x},\omega}\right]\\
C(\psi;A)&:=\sum_{\omega=1}^{N_{f}}\sum_{\mu=0}^{1}\mathfrak{a}^{2}\sum_{\underline{x}\in\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mu}_{\omega}A^{\mu}_{\underline{x},\omega}n_{\underline{x},\omega}=\sum_{\omega=1}^{N_{f}}\sum_{\mu=0}^{1}\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{p}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mu}_{\omega}\hat{A}^{\mu}_{-\underline{p},\omega}\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega}\end{split}

(5.22)

where the external fields
A_{\underline{x},\omega},\phi^{\pm}_{\underline{x},\omega}
take the form

A^{\mu}_{\underline{x},\omega}=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{p}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}e^{i\underline{p}\cdot\underline{x}}\hat{A}^{\mu}_{\underline{p},\omega}\;,\qquad\phi^{\pm}_{\underline{x},\omega}:=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}e^{\mp i\underline{k}\cdot\underline{x}}\hat{\phi}^{\pm}_{\underline{k},\omega}\;.

(5.23)

Finally, we define the partition function with source terms as

\mathcal{Z}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A):=\int P_{N}[d\psi]e^{-V(\psi)+B(\psi;\phi)+C(\psi;A)}\;,

(5.24)

and also the generating functional of correlations as

\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A):=\log\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A)}{\mathcal{Z}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(0,0)}\;.

(5.25)

Then, the
m
-point density-current correlation functions are set to be

\prod_{i=1}^{m}[\mathsf{Z}^{\mu_{i}}_{\omega_{i}}]\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}:=(\beta L)^{m}\frac{\partial^{m}\;\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A)}{\partial\hat{A}^{\mu_{1}}_{-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\cdots\partial\hat{A}^{\mu_{m}}_{-p_{m},\omega_{m}}}\Bigg|_{A=\phi=0}\;,

(5.26)

with
\underline{p}_{m}:=-\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\underline{p}_{i}
. At fixed cut-offs, all expressions are well-defined, for
|\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}|
small enough, thanks to the convergence of fermionic cluster expansion. Renormalization group methods are then used to prove a result that is uniform in all cut-offs.

5.4
Sketch of RG analysis of the reference model

The generating functional admits a multi-scale expansion similar to the one presented for the lattice model in Section
4
, but now the scales will run from
h_{\beta}
to
N
; we shall distinguish two different regimes, according to whether the scale is positive or negative.

Ultraviolet regime.

We rewrite the total propagator as its infre-red part plus the sum of single (positive) scale propagators
\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})
, that is

\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})=\hat{g}^{[h_{\beta},0]}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})+\sum_{h=1}^{N}\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})\;,\qquad\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})=\frac{1}{Z_{\omega}}\frac{\chi_{h}^{\omega,\varepsilon}(\underline{k})-\chi_{h-1}^{\omega,\varepsilon}(\underline{k})}{\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})}\;,

(5.27)

and we iteratively integrate them out, obtaining

\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A)=\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A)+\log\int P_{h}[d\psi^{(\leq h)}]e^{V^{(h)}(\sqrt{Z}\psi^{(\leq h)};\phi,A)}

(5.28)

with
P_{h}
being the Grassmann Gaussian measure associated to
\{\hat{g}^{(\leq h)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}\}_{\omega}
and
\sqrt{Z}\psi^{(\leq h)}
a short-hand for
\sqrt{Z_{\omega}}\psi^{(\leq h)}_{\omega}
. The effective interaction
V^{(h)}
takes the form

V^{(h)}(\psi;\phi,A)=\sum_{\Gamma=(\Gamma_{\psi},\Gamma_{\phi},\Gamma_{A})}\int_{\beta,L}d\underline{X}\,\psi_{\Gamma_{\psi}}(\underline{X}_{\psi})\phi_{\Gamma_{\phi}}({\underline{X}_{\phi}})A_{\Gamma_{A}}({\underline{X}_{A}})W_{\Gamma}^{(h)}(\underline{X})

(5.29)

and
\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,h,N}
admits an analogous expansion.
For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, if we define the intensive weighted
k
-norms

\|W_{\Gamma}^{(h)}\|_{1,k}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{m_{ij}\}_{i,j=1}^{|\Gamma|}\\
\sum_{ij}m_{i,j}=k\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2|\Gamma|}}{|\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}|}\sum_{\underline{X}}|W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}(\underline{X})|\prod_{i<j}\|\underline{x}_{i}-\underline{x}_{j}\|^{m_{ij}}_{\beta,L}\;,

(5.30)

then, for the case of relevant and marginal kernels, these norms satisfy bounds which are better than the standard ones given in (
4.62
).

Proposition 5.2

(Improved estimates for UV kernels, Proposition 8.1 of
[
35
]
)
.

Let us call
\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}:=\max_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}|\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}|
. Then, for
\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}
small enough uniformly in all cut-off parameters, for all
h\in[0,N]
, there exists
C>0
independent of
\beta
,
L
,
\mathfrak{a}
,
\varepsilon
,
N
, such that:

\|W^{(h)}_{\Gamma}-W^{(N)}_{\Gamma}\|_{1,k}\leq C\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}2^{-kh}2^{h(D_{\Gamma}-\vartheta_{\Gamma})}\bigg[\prod_{f\in\Gamma_{A}}\big|\mathsf{Z}^{\mu(f)}_{\omega(f)}\big|\bigg]\bigg[\prod_{f\in\Gamma_{\phi}}\big|\mathsf{Q}^{\varepsilon(f)}_{\omega(f)}\big|\bigg]\;,

(5.31)

where
D_{\Gamma}
is the scaling dimension of
W^{(j)}_{\Gamma}
, namely

D_{\Gamma}:=2-\frac{|\Gamma_{\psi}|}{2}-\frac{3|\Gamma_{\phi}|}{2}-|\Gamma_{A}|

and
\vartheta_{\Gamma}
is given by

\vartheta_{\Gamma}=\begin{cases}2&\text{if }(|\Gamma_{\psi}|,|\Gamma_{\phi}|,|\Gamma_{A}|)=(2,0,0)\\
1&\text{if }(|\Gamma_{\psi}|,|\Gamma_{\phi}|,|\Gamma_{A}|)=(4,0,0),(1,0,1),(2,0,1)\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(5.32)

These improved estimates suggest, following
[
6
]
, that it is convenient to localize the relevant and marginal kernels wholly: indeed, for any
h\geq 0
, we set

\mathfrak{L}V^{(h)}_{n,m,l}(\psi,\phi,A):=\begin{cases}V^{(h)}_{n,m,l}&\text{if }(n,m,l)=(2,0,0),(4,0,0),(1,0,1),(2,0,1)\\
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}

(5.33)

Reintroducing temporarily the ‘ref’ superscript, we see that (
5.27
) implies that the bare couplings
(Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega},v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega})
do not need to be updated for positive scales. The relevant and marginal kernels are controlled by (
5.31
), and all scaling dimensions in the GN tree expansion are negative.

Infrared regime.

For negative scales
h\in[h_{\beta},-1]
, one proceeds in the same exact fashion as with the infrared leg of the lattice model described in Section
4
; one localizes relevant and marginal kernels according to Definition
4.2
, thus obtaining effective couplings
(Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega},v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega},\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}},\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega})
, but no coupling
\nu^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}
is created due to the reference model propagator being odd. The flow is then controlled thanks to the following Proposition, which is a consequence of Theorem 8.5 of
[
35
]
, in turn based on the analysis for the case of two chiralities
[
11
,
13
]
.

Proposition 5.3

(Bounds for the reference model running coupling constants.)
.

There exists
\bar{\lambda}>0
such that for all
\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}:=\max_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}|\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}|<\bar{\lambda}

\begin{split}\left|\frac{Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h-1,\omega}}{Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}}\right|\leq e^{c\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}}\;,&\qquad|v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}-v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}|\leq C\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}\;,\\
|\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}|\leq C|\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}|\;,&\qquad|\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}-\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}|\leq C\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}\;,\end{split}

(5.34)

for some
c,C>0
independent of
\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,h,N
, for all
\omega,\omega^{\prime}=1,\dots,N_{f}
,
\mu=0,1
, and for all
h_{\beta}\leq h<0
.

Remark 5.4
.

Notice that the independence of these bounds from
\beta
allows to control the dynamical system also in the zero temperature limit
h_{\beta}\to-\infty
.

All in all, we may summarize these finding for the ultraviolet and infrared regimes in a GN tree expansion for the generating functional of the reference model: we have,

\begin{split}\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A)&=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{N}\sum_{n,m\geq 0}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{m,\mathrm{ref}}_{h,n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}\\
\Gamma^{\psi}_{v_{0}}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\phi,A;\tau]\\
\mathcal{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\phi,A;\tau]&=\int_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}d\underline{X}_{I_{v_{0}}}\,\phi_{\Gamma^{\phi}_{v_{0}}}A_{\Gamma^{A}_{v_{0}}}W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\underline{X}_{\Gamma_{v_{0}}};\tau]\;,\end{split}

(5.35)

with

\int_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}d\underline{X}_{\Gamma}:=\prod_{f\in\Gamma}\mathfrak{a}^{2}\sum_{\underline{x}(f)\in\Lambda_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}\;,

and where
\mathcal{T}^{m,\mathrm{ref}}_{h,m}
denotes the set of GN trees with root scale
h
,
m
external field leaves and
n
leaves of type
\lambda
that can attach on any scale between
h
and
N
. The set of labellings
\mathcal{L}_{\tau}
respects the two different localization protocols for positive and negative scales.
The tree-dependent kernels
W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
satisfy the GN-type estimate

\|W^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]\|_{1,0}\leq C^{n+m}\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}^{n}\,2^{h\big(2-\frac{3}{2}|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v_{0}}|-|\Gamma^{A}_{v_{0}}|\big)}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\;,

(5.36)

with internal dimension

D_{v}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle 2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|}{2}-\frac{3|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|&\text{if }h_{v}\geq 0\\
\displaystyle 2-\frac{|\Gamma^{\psi}_{v}|}{2}-\frac{3|\Gamma^{\phi}_{v}|}{2}-|\Gamma^{A}_{v}|-z_{v}+c\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}&\text{if }h_{v}\leq-1\;.\end{cases}

We stress that
D_{v}
is always strictly negative for all vertices
v\notin V_{f}(\tau)
: while this is manifest for vertices at negative scale, it is true also for the case
h_{v}\geq 0
, since thanks to (
5.33
) we have localized all the relevant and marginal kernels.

5.5
Ward identities for the
m
-point correlation functions

In this section we will prove identities for the
m
-point correlation functions of the reference model, that allow to prove the vanishing of the density-density correlations with
m\geq 3
after removing all cutoffs. This will follow from the anomalous chiral gauge symmetry of the QFT, to be discussed here. In what follows, we shall use the following short-hand notation:

\begin{split}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{N}&\equiv\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}\\
\mathcal{Z}_{N}(\phi,A)&\equiv\mathcal{Z}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A)\\
\mathcal{W}_{N}(\phi,A)&\equiv\mathcal{W}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N}(\phi,A).\end{split}

(5.37)

We notice that, thanks to the presence of the antiperiodic boundary conditions, the propagator is not singular for
\mathfrak{a}>0
, and the Grassmann algebra is finite dimensional.

Let us consider the chiral local gauge transformation:

\psi^{\pm}_{\underline{x},\omega}\mapsto e^{\pm i\alpha_{\omega}(\underline{x})}\psi^{\pm}_{\underline{x},\omega}

(5.38)

with
\{\alpha_{\omega}(\underline{x})\}
a collection of functions on
(\mathfrak{a}\mathbb{Z})^{2}
such that
\alpha_{\omega}(\underline{x}+n_{1}L\underline{e}_{1}+n_{2}L\underline{e}_{2})=\alpha_{\omega}(\underline{x})
for all chiralities
\omega
.
Then, it is possible to check that, given any monomial
Q(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})
, denoting with
Q_{\alpha}(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})
the same monomial under the transformation (
5.38
):

\int d\psi\,Q(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})=\int d\psi\,Q_{\alpha}(\psi^{+},\psi^{-}).

(5.39)

Thus, by linearity and by finiteness of the Grassmann algebra, for any function
f(\psi)
we have:

\int d\psi\,f(\psi)=\int d\psi\,f_{\alpha}(\psi)\;.

(5.40)

Let:

\begin{split}\Delta_{\underline{p},\omega}(\psi)&:=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}^{a}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p},\omega}\Delta_{\omega}(\underline{k},\underline{p})\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega}\\
\Delta_{\omega}(\underline{k},\underline{p})&:=-Z_{\omega}\frac{\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p})}{\chi_{N}^{\omega,\varepsilon}(\underline{k}-\underline{p})}+Z_{\omega}\frac{\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})}{\chi_{N}^{\omega,\varepsilon}(\underline{k})}+Z_{\omega}\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p})\;,\end{split}

(5.41)

with
\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})=i\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\sin(\mathfrak{a}k_{0})+v_{\omega}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\sin(\mathfrak{a}k_{1})
. Applying the identity (
5.40
) to the partition function, and differentiating with respect to
\hat{\alpha}_{\omega}(\underline{p})
, we get:

0=\int P_{N}[d\psi]e^{-V(\psi)+C(\psi;A)}\left[Z_{\omega}\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p})\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega}-\Delta_{\underline{p},\omega}(\psi)\right],

(5.42)

where
P_{N}
is the Grassmann integration given by:

\begin{split}P_{N}[d\psi]&\propto d\psi\,e^{-K_{N}(\psi)}\\
K_{N}(\psi)&=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\omega}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}^{\mathrm{a}}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega}\left[\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})\right]^{-1}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega}\;,\end{split}

(5.43)

with

{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})=\frac{1}{Z_{\omega}}\frac{\chi^{\omega,\varepsilon}_{N}(\underline{k})}{\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})}\;,\qquad\chi^{\omega,\varepsilon}_{N}(\underline{k})=\chi^{\varepsilon}(2^{-N}\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega})\;.

(5.44)

From now on, we shall use the short-hand notation
\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega}(\underline{k})\equiv\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{k})
.

Proposition 5.5

(Ward identity for the density-density correlators)
.

The following identity holds true:

\begin{split}&Z_{\omega_{1}}\mathcal{D}_{\omega_{1},\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p}_{1})\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&\qquad=-\mathcal{D}_{\omega_{1},\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p}_{1})\mathfrak{B}^{N}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{p}_{1})\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\hat{v}(\underline{p}_{1})\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&\quad\qquad+\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}\Delta_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k},\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k};{\bf{\underline{p}}}_{m})\;,\end{split}

(5.45)

where
\mathfrak{B}^{N}_{\omega}(\underline{p})
is the anomalous bubble diagram,

\mathfrak{B}^{N}_{\omega}(\underline{p}):=-\frac{1}{\beta L}\frac{Z_{\omega}}{\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p})}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}\Delta_{\omega}(\underline{k},\underline{p})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}-\underline{p})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega}(\underline{k})\;,

(5.46)

\underline{{\bf p}}_{m}=(\underline{p}_{1},\ldots,\underline{p}_{m})
, and the error term
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}
satisfies the estimate, for
m\geq 3
:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sup_{\underline{k}:\,c_{1}2^{N}\leq\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega_{1}}\leq c_{2}2^{N}}\Big|\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k};{\bf{\underline{p}}}_{m})\Big|\leq C_{\beta,m}2^{-\gamma N}\;,

(5.47)

where
\gamma>0
and the constant
C_{\beta,m}
is independent of
\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon,N
, provided
\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon
and
\mathfrak{a}/\varepsilon
are small enough.

Corollary 5.6

(Vanishing of the density-density correlators)
.

Let
m\geq 3
. Then, there exists
\gamma>0
such that density-density correlators satisfy the following estimate:

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{\mathfrak{a}\to 0}\frac{1}{\beta L}\big|\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\big|\leq C_{\beta,m}N2^{-\gamma N}

(5.48)

with a positive constant
C_{\beta,m}
independent of
N
.

Remark 5.7
.

Notice that the bounds (
5.47
), (
5.48
) are not optimal in their
\beta
-dependence. With quite some extra effort, one could establish an estimate uniform in
\beta
, and not uniform in the external momenta. This improvement will however not be needed in our application, where the external momenta are bounded, and where the
N\to\infty
limit is performed at
\beta
fixed.

Proof of Corollary
5.6
.

Let us start by estimating the last term in the right-hand side of (
5.45
). Since
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}
is bounded uniformly in
\mathfrak{a}
,
\varepsilon
small (see Remark
A.3
), we may take the limit
\mathfrak{a}\to 0
and then
\varepsilon\to 0
as well. In this limit, the function
\Delta_{\omega_{1}}\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}
becomes, by (
5.41
) and (
5.44
):

\frac{\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{1})-\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})}{Z_{\omega_{1}}^{2}D_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})}\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k})

(5.49)

with
D_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}):=ik_{0}+v_{\omega_{1}}k_{1}
for
\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}^{\mathrm{a}}_{\beta,L}=\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\big(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}\big)\times\frac{2\pi}{L}\big(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}\big)
, so that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}\Delta_{\omega_{1}}&(\underline{k},\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k};{\bf{\underline{p}}}_{m})\\
&\underset{\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon\to 0}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}^{\mathrm{a}}_{\beta,L}}\frac{\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{1})-\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})}{Z_{\omega_{1}}^{2}D_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})}\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k})\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\;,\end{split}

(5.50)

where with a slight abuse of notation we renamed
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}
its
\mathfrak{a},\varepsilon\to 0
limit.

Thanks to the properties of the cut-off function
\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}
, we notice that, for fixed
\underline{p}_{1}\neq\underline{0}
, the function (
5.49
) is supported on very high momenta,
i.e.
for
c_{1}2^{N}\leq\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega_{1}}\leq c_{2}2^{N}
.
Hence, we have:

\begin{split}&\Bigg|\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}^{\mathrm{a}}_{\beta,L}}\frac{\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{1})-\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})}{Z_{\omega_{1}}^{2}D_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})}\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k})\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\Bigg|\\
&\quad\leq C_{\beta,m}2^{-\gamma N}\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}^{\mathrm{a}}_{\beta,L}}\Bigg|\frac{\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{1})-\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})}{Z_{\omega_{1}}^{2}D_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})}\chi^{\omega_{1}}_{N}(\underline{k})\Bigg|\\
&\quad\leq\tilde{C}_{\beta,m}2^{-\gamma N}N\;,\end{split}

(5.51)

where in the first step we employed (
5.47
); the last factor
N
comes from the logarithmic divergence of the sum. Therefore, dividing left-hand side and right-hand side of (
5.45
) by
Z_{\omega_{1}}D_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{p}_{1})
, we have:

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{\mathfrak{a}\to 0}\Big|\sum_{\bar{\omega}}(T^{-1}_{N}(\underline{p}_{1}))_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\Big|\leq\frac{\tilde{C}_{\beta,m}}{\|\underline{p}_{1}\|_{\omega_{1}}}2^{-\gamma N}N,

(5.52)

where
T^{-1}_{N}(\underline{p})
is the matrix:

(T^{-1}_{N}(\underline{p}))_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}=\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}+\mathfrak{B}^{N}_{\omega}(\underline{p})Z_{\omega}^{-1}\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}Z_{\omega^{\prime}}\hat{v}(\underline{p})\;.

(5.53)

The final bound (
5.48
) follows from the invertibility of the matrix
T^{-1}_{N}(\underline{p})
defined in (
5.53
), for
\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}:=\max_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}|\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}|
small enough.
∎

We refer the reader to Appendix
A
for the proof of Proposition
5.5
. The identity (
5.48
) will play a key role in the proof of exactness of linear response for the lattice model. In the bosonization approach to the chiral Luttinger liquid, the identity (
5.48
) as
N\to\infty
is a (formal) consequence of the fact that the density operator is described by (the derivative of) a bosonic Gaussian free field. In our setting, this identity is proved rigorously starting from a regularized QFT. The advantage of this setting is that it is robust enough to be used to derive consequences about the original lattice model, at low energies. This will be discussed in the next section.

To conclude this section, we report briefly the structure of the density-density and vertex correlation functions, when all cut-offs are removed and when
\beta,L
are sent to infinity.

Proposition 5.8

(Density-density correlation and vertex function; Proposition 9.11 of
[
35
]
)
.

Let us set

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle=\lim_{\beta,L\to\infty}\lim_{N\to\infty}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{\mathfrak{a}\to 0}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{N}\;;

then, for any
\underline{p}\neq 0
, the density two-point function takes the form

\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega};\hat{n}_{-\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\rangle=T_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{p})\frac{\mathfrak{B}_{\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{p})}{Z_{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}}\;,

(5.54)

and the vertex function is given by

\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega^{\prime}};\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\rangle=\frac{T_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{p})}{Z_{\omega}D_{\omega}(\underline{p})}\big[\langle\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega^{\prime}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega^{\prime}}\rangle-\langle\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\rangle]\;.

(5.55)

Here

\mathfrak{B}_{\omega}(\underline{p})=\lim_{\beta,L\to\infty}\lim_{N\to\infty}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{\mathfrak{a}\to 0}\mathfrak{B}^{N}_{\omega}(\underline{p})=\frac{1}{4\pi|v_{\omega}|}\frac{-ip_{0}+v_{\omega}p_{1}}{ip_{0}+v_{\omega}p_{1}}\;,

(5.56)

and

T(\underline{p}):=\lim_{\beta,L\to\infty}\lim_{N\to\infty}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{\mathfrak{a}\to 0}T_{N}(\underline{p})=\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(\underline{p})\Lambda_{Z}\hat{v}(\underline{p})}\;,

(5.57)

where

\Lambda_{Z}:=Z^{-1}\Lambda Z\;,

with
Z=\mathrm{diag}\,Z_{\omega}
, and
\Lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}=\lambda_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

We refer to Section 9 of
[
35
]
for the proof. It should be noted that the expression (
5.56
) of the relativistic bubble
\mathfrak{B}
takes a (slightly) different form with respect to the one in
[
35
]
, due to different Fourier conventions adopted in this paper.
∎

6
Proof of Main Theorem

6.1
Matching the lattice and reference models

In this section we shall prove that lattice correlators are well-approximated by the corresponding ones in the reference model, with summable bounds on the deviations.

Remark 6.1
.

In this section, we shall denote the reference model expectation, having already performed the
\mathfrak{a}\to 0
and
\varepsilon\to 0
limits, with
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}
.

Proposition 6.2

(Comparison of correlations)
.

Let
\beta,L,N
be large enough. Calling
\lambda
the bare coupling of the lattice model, there exists
\bar{\lambda}>0
independent of
\beta,L
such that, for all
|\lambda|<\bar{\lambda}
, one may find a choice of the bare couplings of the reference model, satisfying

|Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}-1|=C|\lambda|\;,\qquad|v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}-v_{\omega}|=C|\lambda|\;,\qquad|\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}-\lambda|=C|\lambda|^{2}\;,

(6.1)

for all
\omega,\omega^{\prime}
, such that the following points hold.

Let
m\geq 3
,
\underline{p}_{i}=(\eta_{\beta},p_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,m-1
, and set
\underline{p}_{m}=-\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\underline{p}_{i}
. Assume that
|p_{i}|\leq B|\theta|
, and that
|\theta|\leq K\eta
, as in Theorem
2.6
. Then, for
\eta
small enough, the lattice density-current
m
-point correlations can be written as

\begin{split}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m-1}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}=\sum_{\underline{\omega}}\Big[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{i}}\Big]\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{m}}\,\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}\\
+R^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\;;\end{split}

(6.2)

the error term admits the following estimate:

\frac{1}{\beta L}|R^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|\leq m!C^{m}\,\eta^{2-m+\gamma}F(m;\lambda,\alpha)\;,

(6.3)

where

F(m;\lambda,\alpha):=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }m<16\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}+\Big(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\Big)^{(1/4-8\alpha)m}+|\lambda|^{\alpha m}&\text{if }m\geq 16\end{cases}

(6.4)

with
\gamma\in(0,1)
and any
\alpha\in(0,1/34)
, and
C
uniform in
\beta,L,N
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.15
, we start by writing:

\begin{split}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}&=\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{u.v.})}\\
&\quad+\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{d})}+\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{od})}\;;\end{split}

(6.5)

as noticed already in (
4.130
), (
4.205
), the first and the last term already satisfy the bound (
6.3
), for
\eta
small enough. Let us focus on the second term. We begin by recalling the GN expansion of the density correlations, Eq. (
4.156
):

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{d})}=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{0}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum^{\prime}_{\tau\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{h,n}^{m}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]

(6.6)

with
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{m}_{h,n}
the set of scale-labelled GN trees extending up to the scale of the UV cutoff, with root-scale
h
;
m-1
leaves of
A^{0}
-type, and one
A^{\mu}
-leaf;
\mathcal{L}_{\tau}
the set of labellings of such trees selecting momenta
\underline{p}_{i}
on such leaves, as described in equations (
4.126
)-(
4.127
). The prime in the tree sum enforces the constraint that all
A
-leaves on scale
<0
are diagonal, that is they involve the same fermionic chiralities.

Splitting
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{m}_{h,n}
into
\mathcal{T}^{m,(\leq-1)}_{h,n}
, the set of labelled trees
\tau
with all leaves at scales
\leq-1
, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{m,[0,N_{0}]}_{h,n}
, accounting for trees having at least one endpoint on a scale between
0
and
N_{0}
, we may rewrite (
6.6
) as

\begin{split}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}^{(\text{i.r.}),(\text{d})}&=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum^{\prime}_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m,(\leq-1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]+E^{\beta,L}_{m;\mu,1}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\\
E^{\beta,L}_{m;\mu,1}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})&=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{0}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum^{\prime}_{\tau\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{h,n}^{m,[0,N_{0}]}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]\;.\end{split}

(6.7)

Now, we substitute the first term on right-hand side with the reference model correlation, obtaining

\begin{split}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle^{(\text{i.r.})}_{\beta,L}&=\sum_{\underline{\omega}}\Big[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{i}}\Big]\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{m}}\,\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{3}E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,i}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\end{split}

(6.8)

where (observe that the sum over the GN trees of the reference model only involves diagonal
A
-leaves):

\begin{split}E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,2}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})&:=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum^{\prime}_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m,(\leq-1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\Big(\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]-\widehat{W}^{(h),\mathrm{ref}}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]\Big)\\
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,3}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})&:=-\sum_{h=0}^{N}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{h,n}^{m,[0,N]}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\widehat{W}^{(h),\mathrm{ref}}_{\bf\Gamma}[\tau]\;.\end{split}

(6.9)

Notice that
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,1}\equiv E^{\beta,L}_{m;\mu,1}
does not depend on the reference model, hence it is independent of the reference model cut-off
N
; also it will satisfy estimates which are uniform in the lattice model ultraviolet cut-off
N_{0}
.
Furthermore, here
\mathcal{T}^{m,[0,N]}_{h,n}
indicates the set of reference model GN trees which have at least one leaf attaching on a scale between
0
and
N
. Finally, since no counterterm is needed for the reference model,
i.e.

\nu^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}=0
, we use the convention
\widehat{W}^{(h),\mathrm{ref}}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]=0
whenever there is at least one leaf of type
\nu
.

Thus, our goal is to estimate:

R^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu}:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,i}\;.

(6.10)

We follow the reasoning in (
4.156
) by splitting this remainder into a term with few leaves of
\lambda
or
\nu
type, and one with many:

\begin{split}R^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu}&=\sum_{n=0}^{n_{*}}R^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu}+\sum_{n=n_{*}+1}^{\infty}R^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu}\equiv R^{\beta,L,N}_{m,\leq n_{*};\mu}+R^{\beta,L,N}_{m,>n_{*};\mu}\\
R^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu}&=\sum_{i=1}^{3}E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,i}\;,\end{split}

(6.11)

with
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,i}
collecting the contribution to
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,i}
coming from trees with
n
leaves of type
\lambda,\nu
, and with
n_{*}=\lfloor\alpha m\rfloor
, for
\alpha\in(0,1/34)
to be chosen in the proof of the main Theorem.

Consider the
i=1
error term. Since trees
\tau
contributing to
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,1}
have at least one vertex on scale
0
, by the short-memory property of GN trees the associated kernels will have an extra factor
2^{\gamma(h-0)}
at the root, for
\gamma\in(0,1)
; combining this scaling gain with Proposition
4.20
, we are led to

\frac{1}{\beta L}|E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,1}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|\leq m!\,\eta^{2-m+\gamma}\,C^{m}_{\gamma}\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}&n=0\\
\displaystyle C_{\gamma}^{n}|\lambda|^{n}\left(\frac{2n}{m-32n}\right)^{m/4-8n}&0<n\leq n_{*}\;,\end{cases}

(6.12)

which implies (for
|\lambda|
small enough)

\frac{1}{\beta L}|E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,\leq n_{*};\mu,1}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|\leq m!\,\eta^{2-m+\gamma}C_{\gamma}^{m}\left[\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}+\left(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\right)^{(1/4-8\alpha)m}\right]\;.

(6.13)

Analogously, one obtains

\frac{1}{\beta L}|E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,>n_{*};\mu,1}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|\leq m!\,\eta^{2-m+\gamma}C_{\gamma}^{m}|\lambda|^{\alpha m}\;,

(6.14)

which proves the claim for
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,1}
.

One may reason analogously for the case
i=3
: indeed, we know that trees contributing to
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,3}
have at least one leaf at a non-negative scale, so let us call such scale
h_{*}\geq 0
; by the short-memory property of GN trees we have a gain
2^{\gamma(h-h_{*})}
at the root. Repeating the procedure
4
4

4

To be very precise, in the reference model, for
A
-leaves attaching at positive scales, the corresponding propagator
g^{(h_{i})}_{\mathrm{ref}}
appearing on a chain, see (
4.164
), is also multiplied by a kernel
W^{(h_{i})}_{2,0,1}
; this is due to the localization operator
\mathfrak{L}
acting as the identity on marginal kernels at positive scales. Nevertheless, this does not play any role in the proof of the bounds we need.
outlined in (
6.12
) we may obtain an estimate analogous to (
6.13
) (respectively, (
6.14
)) for
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,\leq n_{*};\mu,3}
(respectively,
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,>n_{*};\mu,3}
).

Finally, we move on to the
i=2
error term. We start by noticing that the splitting (
6.8
) obtained above does not require a specific choice of the bare parameters of the reference model, but in order to interpret
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,2}
as an error term we need to tune such bare parameters in the infrared region: indeed, we choose
(Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega},v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega},\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega\omega^{\prime}},\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega},\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega})
so that

Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h_{\beta},\omega}=Z_{h_{\beta},\omega}\qquad v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h_{\beta},\omega}=v_{h_{\beta},\omega}\qquad\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h_{\beta},\omega\omega^{\prime}}=\lambda_{h_{\beta},\omega\omega^{\prime}}\;,

(6.15)

and

\mathsf{Q}^{\pm,\mathrm{ref}}_{h_{\beta},\omega}=\mathsf{Q}^{\pm}_{h_{\beta},\omega}\qquad\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{h_{\beta},\omega}=\mathsf{Z}^{\mu}_{h_{\beta},\omega}\;.

(6.16)

To do this, one can use the implicit function theorem, since all effective couplings are non-constant, differentiable functions of their bare counterparts. Therefore, thanks to Propositions
4.5
and
5.3
, we obtain that

\begin{split}\left|\frac{Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}}{Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h-1,\omega}}-\frac{Z_{h,\omega}}{Z_{h-1,\omega}}\right|\leq C|\lambda|^{2}2^{\gamma h}\qquad|v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}-v_{h,\omega}|\leq C|\lambda|2^{\gamma h}\\
|\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}-\lambda_{h,\omega\omega^{\prime}}|\leq C|\lambda|^{2}2^{\gamma h}\qquad|\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{h,\omega}-\mathsf{Z}^{\mu}_{h,\omega}|\leq C|\lambda|2^{\gamma h}\end{split}

(6.17)

for all
h=h_{\beta},\dots,-1
, with
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
C
uniform in
\beta,L,N
. Recall also that

|\nu_{h,\omega}|\leq C2^{\gamma h}\;;

(6.18)

furthermore, comparing the lattice and reference model single-scale propagators, we can write
\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega}=\hat{g}^{(h),\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}+\delta\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega}
, and one can check that the deviation satisfies the bound

|\delta\hat{g}^{(h)}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})|\leq C2^{\gamma h}2^{-h}\;.

(6.19)

Since kernels contributing to
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,2}
are given by differences of the lattice and reference model ones, an estimate for them boils down to the control of bounds of the kind (
6.17
)-(
6.19
). Hence, by the continuity property of GN trees, estimates for the contributions to
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,n;\mu,2}
carry an extra
2^{\gamma h}
at the root, so that, proceeding as for the case of
E^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu,1}
, one obtains

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\beta L}|E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,\leq n_{*};\mu,2}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|&\leq m!\,\eta^{2-m+\gamma}C_{\gamma}^{m}\left[\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}+\left(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\right)^{(1/4-8\alpha)m}\right]\\
\frac{1}{\beta L}|E^{\beta,L,N}_{m,>n_{*};\mu,2}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})|&\leq m!\,\eta^{2-m+\gamma}C_{\gamma}^{m}|\lambda|^{\alpha m}\;.\end{split}

(6.20)

Adding together all the bounds above produces the claimed estimate for
|R^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\mu}|
.
∎

Next, we report for later use the comparisons of the 2-point and vertex functions.

Proposition 6.3

(Comparison of 2-point and vertex functions)
.

Under the same hypotheses of Proposition
6.2
, we may compare the 2-point and vertex functions of the lattice model to the corresponding reference model quantities.

Lattice
2
-point function.
There exist
\mathsf{Q}^{\pm,\mathrm{ref}}
satisfying

\mathsf{Q}^{-,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}=\overline{\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}}\;,\qquad\qquad\|\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{+,ref}}_{\omega}\|^{2}:=\sum_{\rho\in S_{M}}|\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}|^{2}=1+O(\lambda)\;,

such that, for
\underline{k}^{\prime}:=\underline{k}-\underline{k}^{\omega,\lambda}_{F,L}
small enough we have:

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{a}_{\underline{k},\rho}\hat{a}^{*}_{\underline{k},\rho^{\prime}}\rangle_{\beta,L}=\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}\mathsf{Q}^{-,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega,\rho^{\prime}}\,\langle\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}+R^{\beta,L,N}_{2;\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\;.

(6.21)

with the remainder term being more regular for small
\underline{k}^{\prime}
, namely

\frac{1}{\beta L}|R^{\beta,L,N}_{2;\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k}^{\prime})|\leq C_{\gamma}\|\underline{k}^{\prime}\|^{\gamma-1}

(6.22)

with
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
C_{\gamma}
independent of
\beta,L,N
.

Lattice vertex function.
Furthermore, for
\|\underline{k}^{\prime}\|
small enough, and
\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}\|=\|\underline{k}^{\prime}\|
, we have that:
