\begin{split}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}};\hat{a}_{\underline{k},\rho};\hat{a}^{*}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p},\rho^{\prime}}\rangle_{\beta,L}=\sum_{\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}\mathsf{Q}^{-,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega,\rho^{\prime}}\,\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega};\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p},\omega}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}\\
+R^{\beta,L,N}_{\mu;\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})\;,\end{split}

(6.23)

with the error term again being more regular, that is

\frac{1}{\beta L}|R^{\beta,L,N}_{\mu;\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})|\leq C_{\gamma}\|\underline{k}^{\prime}\|^{\gamma-2}\;,

(6.24)

with
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
C_{\gamma}
independent of
\beta,L,N
.

Density-current correlation function.
Finally, we have that

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,-\underline{p}}\rangle_{\beta,L}=\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega};\hat{n}_{-\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}+R^{\beta,L,N}_{2;\mu}(\underline{p})

(6.25)

where
R^{\beta,L,N}_{2;\mu}
is Hölder-continuous at
\underline{0}
, for an exponent
\gamma>0
; that is,

\frac{1}{\beta L}|R^{\beta,L,N}_{2;\mu}(\underline{p})-R^{\beta,L,N}_{2;\mu}(\underline{0})|\leq C\|\underline{p}\|^{\gamma}\;,

(6.26)

with
C
independent of
\beta,L,N
.

Proof.

We refer to Proposition 6.1 of
[
1
]
, where a similar statement was proven for the case of a single, spin-degenerate edge mode on a single boundary of a 2
d
topological insulator, and Proposition 10.2 of
[
35
]
, which generalized the former to the case of arbitrarily many edge modes. In any case, the proof strategy is similar to Proposition
6.2
, yet simpler: since the number of points is at most three, one does not have to distinguish between the
n\leq n_{*}
and
n>n_{*}
cases, and dimensional bounds analogous to Proposition
4.15
(combined with the short-memory and continuity properties of GN trees) are enough.
∎

6.2
Evaluation of the linear response

In this section, we shall evaluate explicitly the linear term (
m=2
) in the expansion for
\chi^{\beta,L}_{\nu}
, for
\beta,L
large. A similar computation has been performed in
[
35
]
, for the edge conductance of
2d
topological insulators. We have

\chi^{\beta,L,\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=-\frac{1}{L}\sum_{p\in B_{L}}\hat{\mu}_{\theta}(-p)e^{-ipx}\frac{1}{\beta L}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,-\underline{p}}\rangle_{\beta,L}\;;

(6.27)

noticing that equations (
6.25
)-(
6.26
) prove boundedness of the density-current 2-point correlation uniformly in
\beta,L
, and
N
, and recalling that the perturbation
\mu
is given by the
L
-periodization of a compactly supported smooth function
\mu_{\infty}
, we may rewrite (
6.27
) as

\chi^{\beta,L,\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\theta,\eta)+O(\beta^{-1})+O(L^{-1})\;,

(6.28)

with
\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}
encoding the zero-temperature, infinite-volume response:

\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mu}_{\infty}(-p/\theta)e^{-ipx}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,-\underline{p}}\rangle\frac{dp}{2\pi\theta}

(6.29)

with
\underline{p}=(\eta,p)
, and with
\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,-\underline{p}}\rangle=\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\lim_{L\to\infty}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,-\underline{p}}\rangle_{\beta,L}/(\beta L)
being the zero-temperature, infinite volume correlation function.

Proposition 6.4

(Final form of linear response)
.

Let
\theta=a\eta
. For
\nu=0,1
, the linear response
\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}
of the system,
i.e.
the
m=2
contribution in the Duhamel expansion for
\chi^{\beta,L}_{\nu}
, is given by

\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mu}_{\infty}(q)e^{i\theta qx}(\vec{1},\mathcal{K}^{\nu}(q)\vec{1})\frac{dq}{2\pi}+O(\eta^{\gamma})

(6.30)

for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
(\cdot,\cdot)
being the scalar product on
\mathbb{R}^{N_{f}}
and with
\vec{1}
being the vector with all components equal to 1. The matrix
\mathcal{K}^{\nu}
given by

\mathcal{K}^{\nu}(q)=\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(q)\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\nu}}{2\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}{-i/a+v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}\mathfrak{K}^{\nu}\;,

(6.31)

with
v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\nu}=\delta_{\nu,0}\mathbbm{1}+\delta_{\nu,1}v^{\mathrm{ref}}
, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
,
\mathfrak{K}^{\nu}
defined to be

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(q)&=\left[1-\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\Lambda\right]\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(-1/a,q)\Lambda}\\
\mathfrak{K}^{0}&\equiv\mathbbm{1}\\
\mathfrak{K}^{1}&\equiv\mathrm{sgn\,}v^{\mathrm{ref}}\,\frac{1+(4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda}{1-(4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda}\,\mathrm{sgn\,}v^{\mathrm{ref}}\;.\end{split}

(6.32)

Proof.

Since equations (
6.25
)-(
6.26
) hold uniformly in
\beta,L,N
, we may write

\begin{split}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,-\underline{p}}\rangle&=\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}\mathsf{Z}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega};\hat{n}_{-\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}+R_{2;\nu}(\underline{0})+\tilde{R}_{2;\nu}(\underline{p})\\
\tilde{R}_{2;\nu}(\underline{p})&=R_{2;\nu}(\underline{p})-R_{2;\nu}(\underline{0})\;,\qquad\qquad|\tilde{R}_{2;\nu}(\underline{p})|\leq C\|\underline{p}\|^{\gamma}\;.\end{split}

(6.33)

Next, we determine
R_{2;\nu}(\underline{0})
in terms of the reference model correlations. The lattice continuity equation (
2.32
) implies the following lattice Ward identity,

i\partial_{x_{0}}\langle\mathbf{T}n_{\underline{x}};j_{\nu,\underline{y}}\rangle+\mathrm{d}_{x}\langle\mathbf{T}j_{\underline{x}};j_{\nu,\underline{y}}\rangle=i\delta(x_{0}-y_{0})\langle[n_{x},j_{\nu,y}]\rangle\;,

(6.34)

where as usual
\mathcal{O}_{\underline{x}}:=\gamma_{x_{0}}(\mathcal{O}_{x})
, and where the contact term on the right-hand side arises due to the definition of time-ordering. Taking the Fourier transform on both sides, we obtain:

p_{0}\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,-\underline{p}}\rangle+(1-e^{-ip})\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{\jmath}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,-\underline{p}}\rangle=i\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-ipx}\langle[n_{x},j_{\nu,0}]\rangle\;.

(6.35)

Evaluating (
6.35
) at
\underline{p}=(p_{0},0)
, and using that
[\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Z}}n_{x},j_{\nu,0}]=[\mathcal{N},j_{\nu,0}]=0
, we are then led to

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{(p_{0},0)};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,(-p_{0},0)}\rangle=0\;.

Hence, by (
6.33
), we obtain

R_{2;\nu}(\underline{0})=-\sum_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}\mathsf{Z}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}\lim_{p_{0}\to 0}\langle\hat{n}_{(p_{0},0),\omega};\hat{n}_{(-p_{0},0),\omega^{\prime}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}\;.

(6.36)

We set
S^{\mathrm{ref}}_{2}(p_{0},p)\in\mathcal{M}_{N_{f}}(\mathbb{C})
to be the matrix of the reference model density correlations,
i.e.

S^{\mathrm{ref}}_{2;\omega\omega^{\prime}}(p_{0},p):=\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega};\hat{n}_{-\underline{p},\omega^{\prime}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}\;.

(6.37)

By Proposition
5.8
, it takes the form

S^{\mathrm{ref}}_{2}(p_{0},p)=\frac{1}{1+\hat{v}(\underline{p})\mathfrak{B}(\underline{p})\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{\mathfrak{B}(\underline{p})}{Z^{2}}=\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(\underline{p})\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{\mathfrak{B}(\underline{p})}{Z^{2}}+O(\|\underline{p}\|^{\gamma})

(6.38)

with

\mathfrak{B}(\underline{p})=\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\frac{-ip_{0}+v^{\mathrm{ref}}p}{ip_{0}+v^{\mathrm{ref}}p}\;,\qquad\qquad\Lambda_{Z}=Z^{-1}\Lambda Z

(6.39)

and
v^{\mathrm{ref}}=\mathrm{diag\;}{v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}}
,
\Lambda=(\lambda^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega\omega^{\prime}})_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}
, and
Z=\mathrm{diag}\,Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}
.
In the second step we approximated the reference model interaction
\hat{v}(\underline{p})
with
\hat{v}(\underline{0})\equiv 1
; also, we used the convenient short-hand of writing
AB^{-1}\equiv\frac{A}{B}
whenever
A,B
commute.

Next, realising that
\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon\underline{p})=\mathfrak{B}(\underline{p})
for all
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
, recalling that
\theta=a\eta
, and reparameterizing
\underline{p}=\theta\underline{q}
, with
\underline{q}=(a^{-1},q)
, we get

\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\theta\underline{q}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,-\theta\underline{q}}\rangle=(\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{0,\mathrm{ref}},\mathcal{Q}(q;a)\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}})+O(\eta^{\gamma}(1+a^{2}q^{2})^{\gamma/2})

(6.40)

with
\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}}
the vector in
\mathbb{R}^{N_{f}}
with components
\mathsf{Z}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}
, and
(\cdot\,,\cdot)
the standard scalar product on
\mathbb{R}^{N_{f}}
, and with the matrix-valued function
\mathcal{Q}
being

\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}(q;a)&=\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(1,aq)\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{\mathfrak{B}(1,aq)}{Z^{2}}-\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(1,0)\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{\mathfrak{B}(1,0)}{Z^{2}}\\
&=\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(1,aq)\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}aq}{i+v^{\mathrm{ref}}aq}\frac{1}{1-(4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{1}{2\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|Z^{2}}\;.\end{split}

(6.41)

Then, inserting of (
6.40
) into (
6.29
), and using the fact that
\hat{\mu}_{\infty}
is of Schwartz class, we are led to

\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mu}_{\infty}(q)e^{i\theta qx}(\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{0,\mathrm{ref}},\mathcal{Q}(-q;a)\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}})\frac{dq}{2\pi}+O(\eta^{\gamma})\;.

(6.42)

In the case in which
a(\eta):=\theta/\eta=o(1)
, we see that
\|\mathcal{Q}(q;a)\|=O(a)
, hence the response is trivial, namely

\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=O(a)+O(\eta^{\gamma})\;;

therefore we may focus on the Euler scaling case,
i.e.
we shall assume
a
to be constant.

We shall now evaluate
\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}}
in terms of the other bare parameters of the reference model, namely
Z
,
v^{\mathrm{ref}}
, and
\Lambda
.

Computing the vertex renormalizations.

To avoid cluttering of the notation, we set, for
\mu=0,1
:

\begin{split}\Sigma_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k})&:=\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{a}_{\underline{k},\rho};\hat{a}^{*}_{\underline{k},\rho^{\prime}}\rangle\qquad\qquad S_{1,2;\mu,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k}):=\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{\jmath}_{\mu,\underline{p}};a_{\underline{k},\rho};a^{*}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p},\rho^{\prime}}\rangle\\
\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})&:=\langle\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}\qquad\qquad S^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1,2;\omega\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime}):=\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}^{\prime},\omega};\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p},\omega}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}\end{split}

(6.43)

The lattice vertex function satisfies the following lattice vertex Ward identity:

\sum_{\mu=0}^{1}p_{\mu}Y_{\mu}(p)S_{1,2;\mu,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k})=\Sigma_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k})-\Sigma_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k}+\underline{p})\;.

(6.44)

with
Y_{0}(p)\equiv i
,
p_{1}=p
, and
Y_{1}(p)=(1-e^{-ip})/ip=1+O(p)
. By (
6.23
), for
\underline{k}=\underline{k}^{\omega}_{F}(\lambda)+\underline{k}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle S_{1,2;\mu,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k})=\sum_{\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\text{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Q}^{+,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}\mathsf{Q}^{-,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho^{\prime}}S^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1,2;\omega^{\prime}\omega}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})+R_{\mu,\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})\;,

(6.45)

and, recalling (
6.21
):

\Sigma_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k})=\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}\mathsf{Q}^{-,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega,\rho^{\prime}}\,\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})+R_{2;\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\;.

(6.46)

Since
\|\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}\|=1+O(\lambda)
, we may multiply both sides of (
6.44
) by
\mathsf{Q}^{-,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}\mathsf{Q}^{+,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho^{\prime}}/\|\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}\|^{4}
, and we may sum over all
\rho,\rho^{\prime}
, obtaining:

\begin{split}\sum_{\mu=0}^{1}p_{\mu}Y_{\mu}(p)\Big[\sum_{\omega^{\prime}}\mathsf{Z}^{\mu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}&S^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1,2;\omega^{\prime}\omega}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})+R_{\mu,\omega}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})\Big]=\\
&\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p})+R_{2;\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-R_{2;\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p})\;,\end{split}

(6.47)

where we introduced the notations:

\displaystyle R_{\mu,\omega}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\|\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}\|^{4}}\sum_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}R_{\mu,\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})\mathsf{Q}^{-,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}\mathsf{Q}^{+,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho^{\prime}}

\displaystyle R_{2;\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\|\mathsf{Q}^{+,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}\|^{4}}\sum_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}R_{2;\omega,\rho\rho^{\prime}}(\underline{k}^{\prime})\mathsf{Q}^{-,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho}\mathsf{Q}^{+,\text{ref}}_{\omega,\rho^{\prime}}\;.

(6.48)

Next, recall the explicit expression for the vertex function of the reference model, equation (
5.55
):

S^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1,2;\omega^{\prime}\omega}(\underline{p},\underline{k}^{\prime})=\frac{T_{\omega^{\prime}\omega}(\underline{p})}{Z^{\text{ref}}_{\omega}D_{\omega}(\underline{p})}\big[\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p})]\;.

(6.49)

Recalling the estimates (
6.22
), (
6.24
) for the error terms, and choosing
\|\underline{k}^{\prime}\|=\kappa
,
\|\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p}\|=\kappa
,
\|\underline{p}\|=O(\kappa)
, we see that in (
6.47
) the
R
-terms give contributions that are suppressed by a factor
\kappa^{\gamma}
with respect to the other quantities. Hence, we get:

\sum_{\omega^{\prime}}D^{\mathsf{Z}}_{\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{p})\frac{T_{\omega^{\prime}\omega}(\underline{p})}{Z^{\text{ref}}_{\omega}D_{\omega}(\underline{p})}\big[\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p})\big]=\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p})+O(\kappa^{\gamma-1})

(6.50)

with

D^{\mathsf{Z}}_{\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{p}):=ip_{0}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\text{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}+p\mathsf{Z}^{1,\text{ref}}_{\omega^{\prime}}\;.

(6.51)

Dividing both sides of (
6.50
) by
\big[\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime})-\Sigma^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}(\underline{k}^{\prime}+\underline{p})\big]/[Z^{\text{ref}}_{\omega}D_{\omega}(\underline{p})]=O(\kappa^{-2})
, we find:

\sum_{\omega^{\prime}}D^{\mathsf{Z}}_{\omega^{\prime}}(\underline{p})T_{\omega^{\prime}\omega}(\underline{p})=Z^{\text{ref}}_{\omega}D_{\omega}(\underline{p})+O(\kappa^{1+\gamma})\;.

(6.52)

Recalling the expression (
5.53
) for
T(\underline{p})
, we have that

\lim_{p_{0}\to 0}\lim_{p\to 0}T(\underline{p})=\frac{1}{1-(4\pi|v^{\text{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda_{Z}}\;,\qquad\lim_{p\to 0}\lim_{p_{0}\to 0}T(\underline{p})=\frac{1}{1+(4\pi|v^{\text{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda_{Z}}\;.

(6.53)

Therefore, evaluation of equation (
6.52
) by taking
\underline{p}\to 0
horizontally and vertically implies the following two equalities:

\left(\frac{1}{1-(4\pi|v^{\text{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda_{Z}}\right)^{T}\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{0,\text{ref}}=\vec{Z}^{\text{ref}}\;,\qquad\left(\frac{1}{1+(4\pi|v^{\text{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda_{Z}}\right)^{T}\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{1,\text{ref}}=v^{\text{ref}}\vec{Z}^{\text{ref}}\;,

(6.54)

with
\vec{Z}^{\mathrm{ref}}
the vector of components
Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega}
. Inverting, we obtain, for
\mu=0,1
,

\vec{\mathsf{Z}}^{\mu,\text{ref}}=\big[1-(-1)^{\mu}\Lambda_{Z}^{T}(4\pi|v^{\text{ref}}|)^{-1}\big]v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\mu}\vec{Z}^{\text{ref}}\;,

(6.55)

where
v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{0}:=\mathbbm{1}_{N_{f}}
and
v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1}:=v^{\mathrm{ref}}
.

Final form of the response.

Substituting equation (
6.55
) into (
6.42
), we obtain, for
\nu=0,1
,

\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mu}_{\infty}(q)e^{i\theta qx}(\vec{Z}^{\mathrm{ref}},\mathcal{K}^{\nu}_{Z}(q)\vec{Z}^{\mathrm{ref}})\frac{dq}{2\pi}+O(\eta^{\gamma})\;,

(6.56)

with
\mathcal{K}^{\nu}_{Z}
being

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{\nu}_{Z}(q)&=\left[1-\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\Lambda_{Z}\right]\mathcal{Q}(-q;a)\left[1-(-1)^{\nu}\Lambda^{T}_{Z}\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\right]v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\nu}\\
&=\frac{1}{Z}\left[1-\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\Lambda\right]Z\mathcal{Q}(-q;a)Z\left[1-(-1)^{\nu}\Lambda\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\right]\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\nu}}{Z}\;.\end{split}

(6.57)

Here, we employed the symmetry of
\Lambda
and the fact that
Z
commutes with
v^{\mathrm{ref}}
(since both are diagonal) to bring the conjugation by
Z
from just
\Lambda
to the whole expression. Now we will show that
Z\mathcal{Q}Z
is independent of
Z
: indeed, since
\mathfrak{B}
is diagonal, we have

\begin{split}Z\mathcal{Q}(-q)Z&=Z\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(-1,aq)\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}{-i/a+v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}\frac{1}{1-(4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda_{Z}}\frac{1}{2\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|Z^{2}}Z\\
&=\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(-1,aq)\Lambda}\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}{-i/a+v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}\frac{1}{1-(4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda}\frac{1}{2\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\;.\end{split}

(6.58)

Inserting (
6.57
) and (
6.58
) into (
6.56
), we obtain at last

\chi^{\mathrm{lin}}_{\nu}(x)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mu}_{\infty}(q)e^{i\theta qx}(\vec{1},\mathcal{K}^{\nu}(q)\vec{1}\,)\frac{dq}{2\pi}+O(\eta^{\gamma})\;,

(6.59)

with
\vec{1}
being the vector with all components equal to 1, and
\mathcal{K}^{\nu}
a matrix-valued function depending only on
v^{\mathrm{ref}}
and
\Lambda
, given as

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{0}(q)&=\left[1-\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\Lambda\right]\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(-1/a,q)\Lambda}\frac{1}{2\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}{-i/a+v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}\\
\mathcal{K}^{1}(q)&=\left[1-\frac{1}{4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|}\Lambda\right]\frac{1}{1+\mathfrak{B}(-1/a,q)\Lambda}\frac{\mathrm{sgn\,}v^{\mathrm{ref}}}{2\pi}\frac{v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}{-i/a+v^{\mathrm{ref}}q}\mathfrak{K}\\
\mathfrak{K}&\equiv\mathrm{sgn\,}v^{\mathrm{ref}}\,\frac{1+(4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda}{1-(4\pi|v^{\mathrm{ref}}|)^{-1}\Lambda}\,\mathrm{sgn\,}v^{\mathrm{ref}}\;.\end{split}

(6.60)

The proof is completed by recognising
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
,
\mathfrak{K}^{\nu}
and
v^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\nu}
in the expression above.
∎

6.3
Proof of Theorem
2.6

Proof of Theorem
2.6
.

We begin by recalling the expansion for
\chi^{\beta,L}_{\nu}
, namely

\begin{split}\chi^{\beta,L}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)=-\sum_{m\geq 2}\frac{(-\theta)^{m-2}}{(m-1)!}\frac{1}{L^{m-1}}&\sum_{p\in B^{m-1}_{L}}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}[\hat{\mu}_{\theta}(-p_{i})]e^{ip_{m}x}\\
&\cdot\frac{\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m-1}};\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}}{\beta L}+E^{\beta,L}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)\end{split}

(6.61)

with
\underline{p}_{i}=(\eta_{\beta},p_{i})
,
\underline{p}_{m}=-\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\underline{p}_{i}
, and with
\hat{\mu}_{\theta}(\cdot):=\hat{\mu}(\cdot/\theta)/\theta
. Thanks to (
3.13
), we know that
E^{\beta,L}_{\nu}
is bounded as

|E^{\beta,L}_{\nu}(x;\eta,\theta)|\leq\frac{C}{\eta^{3}\beta}\;.

Let us focus on the corrections to the linear order
m=2
. We estimate them as

\sum_{m\geq 3}\frac{\theta^{m-2}}{(m-1)!}\frac{1}{L^{m-1}}\sum_{p\in B^{m-1}_{L}}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\big[|\hat{\mu}_{\theta}(-p_{i})|\big]\frac{\big|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}\big|}{\beta L}\;.

(6.62)

Thanks to Proposition
6.2
, we rewrite the
m
-point correlation functions in terms of the corresponding ones for the reference model:

\begin{split}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}=\sum_{\underline{\omega}}\Big[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{i}}\Big]\mathsf{Z}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{m}}\,\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}\\
+R^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\nu}(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})\;.\end{split}

(6.63)

By Corollary
5.6
, the reference model density correlations are vanishing when the UV cut-off is removed, that is, uniformly in
N
large,

\frac{1}{\beta L}\left|\Big[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathsf{Z}^{0,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{i}}\Big]\mathsf{Z}^{\nu,\mathrm{ref}}_{\omega_{m}}\,\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\beta,L,N}\right|\leq C_{\beta,m}\,N2^{-\gamma N}\;,

(6.64)

with
\gamma>0
and
C_{\beta,m}
depending on
\beta,\eta
, but independent of
\underline{\omega},L,N
; also, since the bounds in Proposition
6.2
for
R^{\beta,L,N}_{m;\nu}
are uniform in
N
large, by combining these latter with (
6.64
), in the
N\to\infty
limit equation (
6.63
) yields

\frac{1}{\beta L}\big|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}\big|\leq m!\,C^{m}\eta^{2-m+\gamma}F(m;\lambda,\alpha)

(6.65)

for some
\gamma\in(0,1-c|\lambda|)
, and for any
\alpha\in(0,1/34)
, with
C
uniform in
\beta,L
large. Here,
F
is given by (
6.4
), namely

F(m;\lambda,\alpha):=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }m<16\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lfloor m/4-4\rfloor!}+\left(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\right)^{(1/4-8\alpha)m}+|\lambda|^{\alpha m}&\text{if }m\geq 16\;.\end{cases}

(6.66)

By employing (
6.65
), we therefore get the following estimate for (
6.62
):

\begin{split}\sum_{m\geq 3}\frac{\theta^{m-2}}{(m-1)!}\frac{1}{L^{m-1}}&\sum_{\{p_{i}\}\in B^{m-1}_{L}}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\big[|\hat{\mu}_{\theta}(-p_{i})|\big]\frac{\big|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}\big|}{\beta L}\\
&\leq\sum_{m\geq 3}mC^{m}\eta^{\gamma}F(m;\lambda,\alpha)a^{m-2}\frac{1}{L^{m-1}}\sum_{\{p_{i}\}\in B^{m-1}_{L}}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\frac{|\hat{\mu}(-p_{i}/\theta)|}{\theta}\\
&\leq\eta^{\gamma}\sum_{m\geq 3}mC^{m}F(m;\lambda,\alpha)a^{m-2}\|\hat{\mu}_{\infty}\|^{m-1}_{L^{1}}\end{split}

(6.67)

where we recall that
a:=\theta/\eta
, and where in the first step we employed (
6.65
).

Now, if
a
is small enough, we do not need the improvement guaranteed by
F(\cdot;\lambda,\alpha)
for large inputs, since the sum over
m
is convergent. The smallness of the sum is guaranteed by the
\eta^{\gamma}
factor. Instead, for more general values of
a
, the aforementioned improvement is crucial to perform the sum. For given
a
, we choose
\alpha\in(0,1/34)
small enough and
|\lambda|
small enough so that:

2C\|\hat{\mu}_{\infty}\|_{L^{1}}a\left(\frac{2\alpha}{1-32\alpha}\right)^{1/4-8\alpha}<1\;,\qquad\qquad 2C\|\hat{\mu}_{\infty}\|_{L^{1}}a|\lambda|^{\alpha}<1\;.

(6.68)

With this choice, we obtain:

\sum_{m\geq 3}\frac{\theta^{m-2}}{(m-1)!}\frac{1}{L^{m-1}}\sum_{\{p_{i}\}\in B^{m-1}_{L}}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\big[|\hat{\mu}_{\theta}(-p_{i})|\big]\frac{\big|\langle\mathbf{T}\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}};\cdots;\hat{\jmath}_{\nu,\underline{p}_{m}}\rangle_{\beta,L}\big|}{\beta L}\leq\tilde{C}_{\lambda,\alpha}\eta^{\gamma}

(6.69)

for some
\tilde{C}_{\lambda,\alpha}
independent of
\beta,L
. This estimate for the higher order terms in the Duhamel expansion, together with Proposition
6.4
, concludes the proof of Theorem
2.6
.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of Proposition
5.5

Recall Eq. (
5.42
),

0=\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(A)}\int P_{N}[d\psi]\,e^{-V(\psi)+\tilde{C}(\psi;A)}\left[Z_{\omega}\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p})\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega}-\Delta_{\underline{p},\omega}(\psi)\right]\;,

(A.1)

where the source term is:

\tilde{C}(\psi;A):=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\omega=1}^{N_{f}}\sum_{\underline{p}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\hat{A}_{-\underline{p},\omega}\hat{n}_{\underline{p},\omega}\;.

Differentiating with respect to
\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}},\;i=2,\dots,m
and setting
A=0
we get:

Z_{\omega_{1}}\mathcal{D}_{\omega,\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p}_{1})\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}=\langle\Delta_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}(\psi);\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;.

(A.2)

The next lemma provides a useful rewriting of the right-hand side of (
A.2
).

Lemma A.1

(Schwinger-Dyson equation for density correlation functions)
.

Let
m\geq 3
. For any collection of momenta
\{\underline{p}_{i}\neq\underline{0}\}_{i=1}^{m}
such that
p_{i,0}=p_{1,0}\neq 0
for
i=1,\ldots,m-1
,
\underline{p}_{m}
fixed by momentum conservation, and for any choice of chiralities
\{\omega_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
, we have:

\begin{split}\langle\Delta_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}(\psi);\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};&\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
=&-\mathcal{D}_{\omega_{1},\mathfrak{a}}(\underline{p}_{1})\mathfrak{B}^{N}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{p}_{1})\hat{v}(\underline{p}_{1})\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}\Delta_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k},\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})\,\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\omega}}^{N}(\underline{k};{\bf{\underline{p}}}_{m})\;,\end{split}

(A.3)

where:

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\omega}}^{N}(\underline{k};{\bf\underline{p}}_{m}):=&\sum_{\bar{\omega},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}\frac{Z^{2}_{\omega_{1}}}{(\beta L)^{2}}\sum_{\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime}}\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q})\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}^{2,m+1}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k},\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\\
&-\sum_{i=2}^{m}\delta_{\omega_{1},\omega_{i}}\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\frac{Z^{2}_{\omega_{1}}}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{q}}\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q})\mathcal{F}^{2,m-1}_{\underline{\omega};i}(\underline{k},\underline{q};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=2\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{m}\delta_{\omega_{1},\omega_{i}}\delta_{\omega_{1},\omega_{j}}Z_{\omega_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{F}^{2,m-3}_{\underline{\omega};i,j}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\;,\end{split}

(A.4)

with
{\bf{\underline{p}}}_{m}=(\underline{p}_{1},\dots,\underline{p}_{m})
and
\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q}):=[\hat{v}(q)+\hat{v}(-\underline{q})]/2
the even part of the interaction potential. The functions
\mathcal{F}
are given by:

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}^{2,m+1}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k},\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m}):=&\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;,\\
\mathcal{F}^{2,m-1}_{\underline{\omega};i}(\underline{k},\underline{q};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m}):=&\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\langle\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;,\\
\mathcal{F}^{2,m-3}_{\underline{\omega};i,j}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m}):=&\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{j},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{j},\omega_{j}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;,\end{split}

(A.5)

with the wide hat indicating omission of a density.

Proof.

We can write
\langle\Delta_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}(\psi);\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\dots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}
as follows:

\begin{split}&\langle\Delta_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}(\psi);\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\dots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&=\frac{(\beta L)^{m}\partial^{m}}{\partial\hat{B}_{-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\partial\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}}\cdots\partial\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}}\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(A)}\int P_{N}[d\psi]e^{-V(\psi)+\tilde{C}(\psi;A)+\Delta(\psi;B)}\bigg|_{A,B=0}\\
&=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L,\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathrm{a}}}\Delta_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k},\underline{p}_{1})\frac{(\beta L)^{m-1}\partial^{m-1}}{\prod_{i=2}^{m}\partial\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}}\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(A)}\int P_{N}[d\psi]\,e^{-V(\psi)+\tilde{C}(\psi;A)}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}\bigg|_{A=0}\end{split}

(A.6)

where we used (
5.41
) and where

\Delta(\psi;B):=\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{\omega=1}^{N}\sum_{\underline{p}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\hat{B}_{-\underline{p},\omega}\Delta_{\underline{p},\omega}(\psi)\;,

(A.7)

with
\hat{B}_{\underline{p},\omega}
external fields defined as
\hat{A}_{\underline{p},\omega}
(see (
5.23
)).

From the definition of the Grassmann measure given by (
5.43
), we have:

\frac{1}{(\beta L)^{2}}e^{-K_{N}(\psi)}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}=\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}\partial\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}}e^{-K_{N}(\psi)}

(A.8)

so that, integrating by parts the argument of the derivatives in (
A.6
), we obtain:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(A)}\int P_{N}[d\psi]\,e^{-V(\psi)+\tilde{C}(\psi;A)}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}\\
&\qquad=\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})\frac{(\beta L)^{2}}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(A)}\int P_{N}[d\psi]\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\partial\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}}e^{-V(\psi)+\tilde{C}(\psi;A)}\\
&\qquad=\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})\frac{(\beta L)^{2}}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(A)}\int P_{N}[d\psi]\,e^{-V(\psi)+\tilde{C}(\psi;A)}\times\\
&\qquad\times\Bigg[\frac{\partial^{2}[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)]}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\partial\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}}+\frac{\partial[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)]}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}}\frac{\partial[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)]}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}}\Bigg].\end{split}

(A.9)

Let us consider the term in (
A.9
) with the second derivative of
V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)
. From (
5.20
), (
5.21
), (
5.22
), and employing the fact that
\lambda_{\omega\omega}=0
, we find:

\frac{\partial^{2}[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)]}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\partial\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}}=\frac{Z_{\omega_{1}}}{(\beta L)^{2}}\bigg[\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\hat{v}(\underline{p}_{1})\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}}-\hat{A}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}\bigg]\;.

(A.10)

Using this identity in (
A.9
) and differentiating with respect to
\{\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}\}_{i=2}^{m}
, the contribution of the first term in the right-hand side of (
A.9
) to (
A.6
) is:

Z_{\omega_{1}}\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1})\hat{g}^{(\leq N)}_{\omega_{1}}(\underline{k})\hat{v}(\underline{p}_{1})\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;.

(A.11)

Observe that, since the momenta
\underline{p}_{i}
are all nonzero, by momentum conservation we see that the term corresponding to
\hat{A}_{\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}
in (
A.10
) does not contribute to the result. The identity (
A.3
) follows after inserting (
A.11
) in (
A.6
), recalling the definition (
5.46
) for the anomalous bubble diagram
\mathcal{B}^{N}_{\omega_{1}}
, and setting:

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\omega}}^{N}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m}):=\frac{(\beta L)^{m-1}\partial^{m-1}}{\prod_{i=2}^{m}\partial\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}}\frac{(\beta L)^{2}}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}(A)}\int&P_{N}[d\psi]\,e^{-V(\psi)+\tilde{C}(\psi;A)}\,\times\\
&\times\frac{\partial[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)]}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}}\frac{\partial[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)]}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}}\Bigg|_{A=0}\;.\end{split}

(A.12)

The remaining part of the proof is the explicit evaluation of (
A.12
). We proceed as follows: first, an explicit computation yields

\begin{split}&\frac{\partial[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}}}\\
&=\frac{Z_{\omega_{1}}}{(\beta L)^{2}}\sum_{\underline{q}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\bigg[\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\frac{\hat{v}(\underline{q})}{2}\Big(\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{-\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}+\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\Big)-\hat{A}_{-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\bigg]\\
&=\frac{Z_{\omega_{1}}}{(\beta L)^{2}}\sum_{\underline{q}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\bigg[\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q})\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}-\hat{A}_{-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\bigg]\;,\end{split}

(A.13)

where
\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q}):=[\hat{v}(\underline{q})+\hat{v}(-\underline{q})]/2
, and

\begin{split}&\frac{\partial[V(\psi)-\tilde{C}(\psi;A)]}{\partial\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}}}\\
&=\frac{Z_{\omega_{1}}}{(\beta L)^{2}}\sum_{\underline{q}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\bigg[\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\frac{\hat{v}(\underline{q})}{2}\left(\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{-\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}+\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\right)-\hat{A}_{-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\bigg]\\
&=\frac{Z_{\omega_{1}}}{(\beta L)^{2}}\sum_{\underline{q}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}}\bigg[\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q})\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}-\hat{A}_{-\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\bigg]\;.\end{split}

(A.14)

Second, multiplying (
A.13
) with (
A.14
), inserting the result in (
A.12
), deriving with respect to the external fields
\{\hat{A}_{-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}\}_{i=2}^{m}
and setting
A=0
, we are led to a host of different terms contributing to
\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\omega}}^{N}
:

1.

the first term of (
A.13
) with the first one of (
A.14
) gives

\sum_{\bar{\omega},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}\frac{Z^{2}_{\omega_{1}}}{(\beta L)^{2}}\sum_{\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime}}\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q})\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}^{2,m+1}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k},\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})

(A.15)

with

\mathcal{F}^{2,m+1}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k},\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m}):=\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;;

2.

the first (resp. second) term of (
A.13
) with the second (resp. first) one of (
A.14
) produces

-\sum_{i=2}^{m}\delta_{\omega_{1},\omega_{i}}\sum_{\bar{\omega}}\lambda_{\omega_{1}\bar{\omega}}Z_{\bar{\omega}}\frac{Z^{2}_{\omega_{1}}}{\beta L}\sum_{\underline{q}}\hat{v}_{\mathrm{s}}(\underline{q})\mathcal{F}^{2,m-1}_{\underline{\omega};i}(\underline{k},\underline{q};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})

(A.16)

with

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}^{2,m-1}_{\underline{\omega};i}(\underline{k},\underline{q};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m}):=&\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\langle\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}}\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\end{split}

where the wide hat indicates omission of the corresponding density;

3.

finally, the second term of (
A.13
) with the second one of (
A.14
) yields

Z_{\omega_{1}}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=2\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{m}\delta_{\omega_{1},\omega_{i}}\delta_{\omega_{1},\omega_{j}}\mathcal{F}^{2,m-3}_{\underline{\omega};i,j}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})

(A.17)

with

\mathcal{F}^{2,m-3}_{\underline{\omega};i,j}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m}):=\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}}\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{j},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{j},\omega_{j}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
A.1
.
∎

The next lemma establishes the bound for the remainder
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}
.

Lemma A.2
.

There exist
\gamma>0
,
C>0
such that for all
\underline{\omega}
, and for
\underline{p}_{i}
as in Lemma
A.1
, with the supplementary requirement that
|p_{i}|\leq C|\theta|
and
p_{i,0}=\eta
for all
i\leq m-1
, we have:

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sup_{c_{1}2^{N}\leq\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega_{1}}\leq c_{2}2^{N}}\big|\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\big|\leq C^{m}m!\beta^{m+2}2^{-\gamma N}\;.

(A.18)

Note that, from Lemma
A.2
and from definition of
\mathfrak{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}
(see (
A.4
)), the bound (
5.47
) immediately follows.

Proof.

In order to use the GN tree estimates introduced earlier, we rewrite all of the
\mathcal{F}
in terms of
connected
correlation functions, keeping in mind the following three rules:

1.

momentum conservation must hold in each connected correlation function separately;

2.

\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{q}}\rangle_{N}=0
for all
\underline{q}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta,L}^{\mathrm{p}}
;

3.

\langle\hat{\psi}^{\pm};\hat{n};\cdots;\hat{n}\rangle_{N}=0
by the fact that expectations of odd fermionic monomials vanish.

Thus, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}^{2,m+1}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k},\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})=&\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\delta_{\underline{q},\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{q}^{\prime}}\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}}\rangle_{N}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\delta_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\underline{p}_{1}}\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}}\rangle_{N}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\delta_{\underline{q},\underline{p}_{1}}\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}\rangle_{N}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\delta_{\underline{q},-\underline{q}^{\prime}}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{-\underline{q},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}\rangle_{N}\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\end{split}

(A.19)

and

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}^{2,m-1}_{\underline{\omega};i}&(\underline{k},\underline{q};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})=\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\\
&+\delta_{\underline{q},\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{p}_{i}}\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}}\rangle_{N}\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{p}_{i},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;.\end{split}

(A.20)

Instead, we see that the terms
\mathcal{F}^{2,m-3}_{\underline{\omega};i,j}
are already connected, namely

\mathcal{F}^{2,m-3}_{\underline{\omega};i,j}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})=\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}+\underline{p}_{j},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{j},\omega_{j}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\;.

(A.21)

First, consider all of the correlations occurring in (
A.19
)-(
A.20
) with no
\hat{\psi}
field: these involve
m+1
,
m
,
m-1
or 2 densities. For the sake of concreteness, let us consider a token example of
m
-point correlation functions, namely
\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}
, whose GN-type estimate is

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\beta L}\big|\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\big|\leq\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{N}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau,\mathbf{\Gamma}}\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}^{n}\,2^{h(2-m)}\prod_{v\notin V_{f}(\tau)}2^{(h_{v}-h_{v^{\prime}})D_{v}}\;;\end{split}

(A.22)

summing over
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}
,
\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{m,\mathrm{ref}}_{h,n}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, for
\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}
small enough and for
m\geq 3
, we obtain

\frac{1}{\beta L}\big|\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\big|\leq m!\,C^{m}\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{N}2^{h(2-m)}\leq C_{\beta,m}\;,

(A.23)

with
C_{\beta,m}\propto m!\,\beta^{m-2}
. One may proceed in the same way for all the other correlators with
m
or
m+1
densities. This bound is of course not optimal, since it does not take into account the dependence on the external momenta, but it will be enough for our purposes.

For the density correlator with
m-1
points appearing in the second row of (
A.20
), performing the same estimate we obtain

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\beta L}\big|\langle\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{p}_{i},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\widehat{\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{i},\omega_{i}}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\big|\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq C^{m}m!\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{N}2^{h(3-m)}\leq C^{m}m!\cdot\begin{cases}\log\beta+N&m=3\\
\beta^{m-3}&m>3\end{cases}\end{split}

(A.24)

and analogously for the two-point density correlation function in the last row of (
A.20
). Therefore, all correlation functions in (
A.19
)-(
A.20
) involving only densities may be bounded by
C^{m}m!(\beta^{m-1}+N)
. Again, this is of course not optimal, but it will be enough for our purposes.

Consider now the correlation functions appearing in (
A.19
)-(
A.21
) and having two
\hat{\psi}
fields: we notice that, since
c_{1}2^{N}\leq\|\underline{k}\|_{\omega_{1}}\leq c_{2}2^{N}
, for
N
large enough the momenta of these
\hat{\psi}
fields will be also of order
2^{N}
, since
\|\underline{q}\|_{\omega_{1}},\|\underline{q}^{\prime}\|_{\omega_{1}},\|\underline{p}_{i}\|_{\omega_{1}}
are of order 1 in
N
: recall that the momenta
\underline{q}
,
\underline{q}^{\prime}
are in the support of
\hat{v}
, that we are assuming
|p_{i}|\leq C|\theta|
,
p_{i,0}=\eta
for all
i=1,\ldots,m-1
, and that, by momentum conservation,
|\underline{p}_{m}|\leq C(m-1)(\eta+|\theta|)
. For the sake of concreteness let us consider the first row of (
A.19
). Its GN expansion is:

\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}=\sum_{h=h_{\beta}}^{N}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau,\mathbf{\Gamma}}\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]\;.

(A.25)

Since
\phi
-leaves attach only on the (couple of) scales that contain the momentum by which they are labelled, by the discussion above we see that the two
\phi
-leaves will necessarily be on scale
N
(or
N-1
). Therefore, we may extract a short-memory factor at the root, namely

\frac{1}{\beta L}\sum_{n\geq 0}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{m+4,\mathrm{ref}}_{h,n}}\sum_{\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\tau}}\big|\widehat{W}^{(h)}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}[\tau]\big|\leq m!\,C^{m}\,2^{h(-m-2)}2^{\tilde{\gamma}(h-N)}\;;

(A.26)

summation over the root scale then yields

\frac{1}{\beta L}\big|\langle\hat{\psi}^{+}_{\underline{k}-\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{q},\omega_{1}};\hat{\psi}^{-}_{\underline{k}-\underline{q}^{\prime},\omega_{1}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q},\bar{\omega}};\hat{n}_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\bar{\omega}^{\prime}};\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{2},\omega_{2}};\cdots;\hat{n}_{\underline{p}_{m},\omega_{m}}\rangle_{N}\big|\leq C^{m}m!\beta^{m+2}2^{-\tilde{\gamma}N}\;.

(A.27)

It is enough to proceed in the same way for all other correlators having two
\hat{\psi}
fields. Combining all of the estimates that we have obtained, we may bound the
\mathcal{F}
terms as

\begin{split}\big|\mathcal{F}^{2,m+1}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k},\underline{q},\underline{q}^{\prime};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\big|&\leq(\beta L)C^{m}m!(\beta^{m+2}+N)2^{-\tilde{\gamma}N}\big[1+\beta L(\delta_{\underline{q},\underline{p}_{1}}+\delta_{\underline{q}^{\prime},\underline{p}_{1}}+\delta_{\underline{q},\underline{p}_{1}}+\delta_{\underline{q},-\underline{q}^{\prime}})\big]\\
\big|\mathcal{F}^{2,m-1}_{\underline{\omega};i}(\underline{k},\underline{q};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\big|&\leq(\beta L)C^{m}m!(\beta^{m+2}+N)2^{-\tilde{\gamma}N}\big[1+\beta L\delta_{\underline{q},\underline{p}_{1}+\underline{p}_{i}}\big]\\
\big|\mathcal{F}^{2,m-3}_{\underline{\omega};i,j}(\underline{k},\underline{q};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})\big|&\leq(\beta L)C^{m}m!(\beta^{m+2}+N)2^{-\tilde{\gamma}N}\end{split}

Insertion of these bounds in equation (
A.4
) yields the claim (
A.18
), for some
\gamma<\tilde{\gamma}
.
∎

Remark A.3
.

Actually, the bounds for the correlators of the reference model imply that:

|\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\underline{\omega}}(\underline{k};\underline{\mathbf{p}}_{m})|\leq\beta LC^{m}m!\beta^{m+2}

(A.28)

for all
\underline{k}
, uniformly in
\mathfrak{a}
,
\varepsilon
small (with
\mathfrak{a}/\varepsilon
small enough). This bound is used to take the limits in (
5.49
), (
5.50
).
